# Recording start and stop reliability: evidence

Companion to the independent audit of 2026-11-12 (`2026-11-12-independent-audit.md`, in this folder), whose executive summary carries the conclusions drawn from this file. Evidence appendix to `2026-11-13-recording-reliability-audit.md`. Caution: the failure-mode mechanisms, the fix history, the signal inventory and the owner-run probes below were written by code tracers before the field pack arrived, under the wrong assumption that the owner runs the home Mac's stale install (code from about 2026-08-18) every day; where they say the installed build, the August build or advise installing HEAD, they mean that home Mac. The field status, the verifier lines and the incident tables were written with the corrected picture.

## Field metrics and incidents by symptom

### A Google Meet call in a browser is rarely picked up

| Metric | Value | How computed |
|---|---|---|
| chrome_rows_and_days | 331 rows on 6 of 38 active days; 0 rows for any other browser or PWA bundle | Events whose bundle_id or winner_bundle_id is com.google.Chrome, all four events files sorted by ts; days are distinct UTC dates. Other browsers: same test against the ten registry browser ids and the three PWA prefixes. |
| chrome_detection_funnel | 9 picks, 11 discovery engages, 8 lifecycle starting, 7 prompts, 1 recording | Counts of detector/discovery_shadow_pick (winner Chrome), lifecycle/starting, coordinator/prompt_shown, coordinator/recording_started with bundle_id com.google.Chrome. Engages are the 13 Chrome coordinator/ax_handles_built rows minus the 2 record-start builds logged while the state was recording (2026-10-05T15:08:43Z). |
| chrome_prompt_outcomes | 1 recorded (3.5 s after the prompt), 2 user_skipped (7.6 s, 12.6 s), 2 prompt_timeout (30 s), 2 dismissed by a lifecycle end (0.18 s, 0.84 s) | For each Chrome prompt_shown, the first of recording_started, user_skipped, prompt_timeout for Chrome within 40 s, or a state_change prompting to idle with neither. |
| meet_call_episodes | 6 call episodes: 1 recorded automatically (title matched, Meet identity inferred), 5 missed with a Meet-shaped title logged (1 certain, 3 probable, 1 possible; 83 percent if all count). One further Chrome episode (2026-09-30, title matched, [...] | An episode is a Chrome engage whose window titles (candidate_titles of signal/shareable_content_no_match, classified by shape only) show a Meet-shaped title, or whose title matched and was recorded. Missed means no prompt that stayed up longer than 1 s and no automatic recording. |
| named_title_time_unrecorded | 27.8 + 13.6 + 23.5 = 64.9 min in three probable calls, plus one possible call of unknown length (2026-10-07T12:00Z) | First to last no_match row carrying a title of the shape word Meet, colon, quoted name, per engage; rows are emitted only when the candidate list changes, so the title is on screen between rows. No recording_started overlaps any of the three spans. |
| pick_then_no_start | 4 of 9 Chrome picks (first window read 25, 44, 74 and 181 ms after the pick row) | Chrome discovery_shadow_pick followed by shareable_content_no_match reason initial and window_present false, with no lifecycle starting within 5 s. The first read shows the code-less named title in 3 cases and the bare product word in 1, which became the named title 1.2 s later. |
| mic_in_use_tier_rows | 0 mic_busy_started, 0 mic_busy_ended, 0 mic_in_use_prompt, 0 mic_device_unavailable; against 420 process_audio_is_running_input true rows (Teams 415, Zoom 5; 354, 58, 8 by era), 74 recordings and 12 launch proxies | Action counts over all 114,553 events. The launch proxy is the permission_granted kind microphone row written in each startup burst. The emitters exist in the source at all three era commits (git show). |
| stale_browser_engages | 4 engages that never started stayed alive 95.3, 176.5, 119.5 and 15.0 min | From a Chrome ax_handles_built to the next ax_handles_built of any bundle, lifecycle idle, or app launch; Chrome signal rows inside each interval: 11, 43, 191 and 9; the last one lands 65.8, 176.5, 119.5 and 15.0 min after the engage. |
| manual_recordings_stopped_by_lifecycle_end | 1 of 4 | recording_started with bundle_id manual, then lifecycle rows between start and stop. Only stem 20261111-155951 has any: Chrome starting at 14:12:18.901Z and Chrome ended at 14:14:28.163Z, 6 ms before state_change recording to stopping. |
| newest_build_exposure | 0 Chrome rows in 22.4 h of era 3; 6 recordings there (5 Teams, 1 Zoom) | Events from the app_rebuild at 2026-11-12T09:19:44Z to the last event at 2026-11-13T07:44:15Z. |
| six_month_browser_history | Chrome 24 prompts, 5 recordings, 7 timeouts, 7 skips; Chrome PWA 9 prompts, 0 recordings; Teams 843 prompts, 370 recordings | daemon.log lines 2026-05-18 to 2026-11-13 matching the prompt, recording started, user skipped and prompt timed out messages, grouped by the bundle id in the line. All 5 Chrome recordings have a meta title of none in the same log. |
| window_read_failures | 1,656 shareable_content_unavailable rows (1,655 reason poll, 1 reason initial); Chrome 39 of them | Count of signal/shareable_content_unavailable by bundle and reason. screenRecording permission_granted appears in 12 of 12 startup bursts, so these are probe failures, not a missing grant. |
| commits_after_newest_build | 35 commits f2d65038..HEAD, none changing detection logic | git rev-list with its count option over f2d65038..HEAD, and git diff over Lifecycle, the scanner, scorer, discovery watcher, cadence and MeetingSessionController; the only hunk in those files is REC20 and REC26 code in MeetingSessionController. |

### A native call (Zoom, Teams, others) is missed, picked up late, or prompts again and again

| Metric | Value | How computed |
|---|---|---|
| Events and span | 114,553 events, 2026-09-29T09:45Z to 2026-11-13T07:44Z, 38 active days, 12 daemon launches | All four events files merged and sorted by ts; active day = calendar day with at least one event; launch = transcription engine_resolved row. |
| Build eras | A: 14 days, ae035d58. B: 24 days, 8d4a9449. C: 2 days (22.4 h), f2d65038 | Split at the two main app_rebuild rows. Commit per era from first-seen event names and field sets against git log -S, and committer dates against the rebuild timestamps. git diff f2d65038 HEAD is empty for Lifecycle/, MeetingDiscoveryWatcher, MeetingSourceScanner, MeetingSourceScorer, DetectionStateMachine, MeetingPromptWindow. |
| Native prompts | 410 (407 Teams, 3 Zoom); A 351, B 54, C 5 | coordinator prompt_shown rows with bundle com.microsoft.teams2 or us.zoom.xos. |
| Prompts on screen under 1 s | 308, all Teams, all era A (2026-10-08: 217, 10-09: 82, 10-12: 9); median 15 ms on screen, median 11.3 s between consecutive ones | prompt_shown to the next state_change out of prompting, under 1.0 s, with no recording, timeout or skip. Gap = time between consecutive such prompts under 60 s apart (n=298). |
| Outcome of the 102 native prompts that stayed up 1 s or more | recorded 60, prompt_timeout 30, withdrawn by a lifecycle end 8, skipped by the owner 4 | State change out of prompting, classified by the events within 1.5 s of it; all 32 prompt_timeout rows in the pack carry default_action skip and all 417 prompts timeout_sec 30. |
| Teams call ledger | 95 scanner-confident intervals (A 34, B 56, C 5); 88 last 120 s or more; recording began within 30 s of first sighting on 54 of the 88 (61 percent): A 13 of 31, B 36 of 52, C 5 of 5 | Interval = discovery_shadow_pick for Teams to the next Teams candidate_dropped or daemon launch. Valid because process audio is in the scan in all eras: 0 Teams picks and only 5 Teams drops fell inside the 61 Teams recordings of eras A and B, all 5 after the lifecycle had already read process audio false. |
| What followed the 30 Teams prompt timeouts | 19 calls never recorded (19,133 s of confident call time, 107 s to 3,199 s each); 8 started by hand; 2 inside storm intervals; 1 false 3 s sighting | prompt_timeout rows joined to the call ledger; unrecorded seconds = interval length where no recording overlapped. An upper bound: the interval closes on the next title-only drop row, and Teams logs none while it has no titled window (one recorded interval on 2026-11-10 ran to 17,732 s that way). |
| Start-side failure intervals | 37 Teams intervals of 60 s or more with no recording inside 30 s of first sighting: timeout 27, storm with a timeout 2, storm 5, never prompted 2, skip then hand start 1. Timeout-involved 29 of 37 (78 percent) | Call ledger; excluded as under 60 s: one 22 s owner skip, one 3 s false sighting, one 21 s call that ended during its prompt. |
| Lost or late call seconds by class | 32,986 s in all: timeout 22,873 (69 percent), storm 8,457 (26 percent), never prompted 1,620, skip then hand start 35 | Per failure interval: whole length if nothing was recorded; length minus recorded seconds for a storm; first sighting to the hand start otherwise. |
| Lateness of the 8 hand starts after a timeout | 33, 39, 56, 89, 154, 491, 1,108, 1,633 s; median 121.5 s | prompt_shown to recording_started for record_attached or bundle-manual starts that followed a prompt_timeout in the same call interval. |
| Timeouts versus recordings in the same daily slot | 07:30Z slot: 11 recorded on the prompt, 3 timed out unrecorded, 1 by hand. 13:30Z slot: 9, 4, 2. 14:30Z slot: 7, 5, 0 | Teams intervals whose first sighting is within 6 min of the half hour, grouped by class. |
| Teams timeouts per month in daemon.log | June 15 of 113 prompts, July 17 of 84, August 16 of 90, September 35 of 110, October 26 of 381, November 3 of 21; 112 in all | daemon.log lines matching the prompt-timed-out-to-idle text and the meeting-detected-prompting text, by month and trailing bundle id. |
| Storm intervals | 7 Teams call intervals, 323 prompts, 9,163 s of confident call time, 705 s recorded | Ledger intervals with 2 or more prompt_shown; recorded seconds = overlap with recording_started to recording_stopped spans. |
| Starting-phase ends on window-gone with nothing confirming | A 316 (313 withdrew a prompt), B 0 (30 engages with window false and audio true, all end_vetoed), C 1 (2026-11-12T15:46:06.614Z) | lifecycle ended rows with leading_signal shareable_content_window_gone and empty confirmed_by; per-engage order of the three initial readings within 2 s of lifecycle starting. |
| End-side Teams window matcher misses | 1,264 shareable_content_no_match rows with a non-empty candidate list (A 328, B 878, C 58) from 2026-10-08T14:31:18Z, plus 5 with an empty list; before that 0 non-empty and 7 empty; none of the non-empty lists contains 'microsoft teams' or [...] | signal shareable_content_no_match rows for Teams, candidate_titles length over 0; the two tokens tested in a script, titles never printed. |
| Share of recorded Teams time the window signal read present | Before 2026-10-08T14:31Z: 100 percent (17 recordings, 35,877 s true, 8 s false). Era B: 47 percent (40 recordings, 27,883 s true, 31,271 s false; 14 recordings under 10 percent). Era C: 0.3 percent (5 recordings, 13 s true, 3,893 s false) | shareable_content_window_present values for Teams integrated over each Teams recording span, carrying the last reading of the engagement into the recording. |
| Cooldown-held prompts | 4 (2026-10-01, 10-22, 11-05, 11-06), held 40, 71, 56 and 10 s after the first sighting; 4 of 4 then timed out | Teams pick inside 60 s of the same app's recording_stopped, prompt_shown only after the cooldown lapsed. |
| Calls never prompted under an inherited skip latch | 2: 2026-10-20T14:53:25Z (81 s) and 2026-10-21T14:56:31Z (1,538 s) | Ledger intervals with a pick, no lifecycle starting and no prompt_shown, beginning under 60 s after the previous timed-out call dropped to title only. |
| First sighting to recording_started, calls recorded on the prompt | n=52, median 5.5 s, p90 17.0 s, max 26.1 s; over 10 s on 14 | discovery_shadow_pick that opened the interval to recording_started. |
| Prompt to recording, answered native prompts | n=60, median 4.0 s, p90 13.0 s, max 24.0 s; era C: 15.0, 2.3, 24.0, 3.6, 3.9 s | prompt_shown to recording_started. |
| First sighting to prompt_shown | A median 1.0 s (1 of 27 over 5 s), B median 1.1 s (5 of 50 over 5 s, max 28 s), C median 0.46 s (0 of 5, max 0.66 s) | Pick that opened the interval to its first prompt_shown, excluding storm and cooldown-held intervals. |
| Transcripts that open mid-speech | 18 of 58 prompt-answered native recordings have first speech within 3 s of the start; 11 of those had a Leave button at the engage walk | First segment start in <stem>.json; found_leave from the ax_handles_built row before the prompt. Class only, no text read. |
| Mic-in-use tier rows | 0 mic_busy_started, 0 mic_busy_ended, 0 mic_in_use_prompt, 0 mic_device_unavailable, against 420 process_audio_is_running_input true readings and 74 recordings | Action counts over the whole pack. |
| Zoom | 3 calls, 3 prompted, 3 recorded; picks: title+Leave+Mute+audio score 10 (A), Mute+audio score 5 (B), Mute+audio score 5 (C); candidate_count 2 on all three | discovery_shadow_pick rows with winner us.zoom.xos. |
| Era C sample size | 6 native call intervals, 5 prompts, 0 timeouts; chance of 0 timeouts in 5 at the 29 percent base rate is 17.5 percent | (1 - 30/102) to the fifth power. |

| How recordings started | prompt answered 61, hand attach 8, hand start with no source 4, resumed after an end 1 | state_change prompting->recording = prompt; record_attached within 15 s before = attach; bundle_id manual = no source; recording_resumed_after_end beside the start = resume. |
| How recordings stopped | lifecycle end 63, idle backstop 1, no cause row 10 (A 2, B 6, C 2) | lifecycle/ended within 5 s before state_change recording->stopping (one more counted at 5.9 s), else auto_stop_silence, else none. 'No cause row' is a hand stop by elimination. |
| Prompt outcomes | 417 raised: Record 61, timeout 32, skipped by hand 5, dismissed by an end already raised before the prompt 306, dismissed by an end during the prompt 10, other 3 | Each coordinator/prompt_shown paired with the next state_change out of prompting; cause from prompt_timeout or user_skipped near it, or a lifecycle/ended since the engagement's lifecycle/starting. |

## Detail for the critical, high and field-demonstrated ones (54)

**D001. A Meet tab titled by its meeting name, not its room code, is never a candidate**

- Severity high (partially confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/Lifecycle/Adapters/LifecycleAdapter.swift:97`. Related: START2, END5, DET6, F040, commits c2c47ff9, b9ea01be, ce887ff8. Effort M.
- Owner sees: A scheduled Meet joined from a calendar link shows no prompt at all, for the whole call, while an ad hoc call (title carries the room code) does prompt. This matches the report that it works sometimes but rarely.
- Mechanism: MeetingTitlePatterns.googleMeet (LifecycleAdapter.swift:97-110) admits only a title holding meet.google.com, or the word meet plus a 3-4-3 room code. The work laptop's real Chrome Meet tab is titled in the shape Meet: "<name>", sometimes with a trailing speaker glyph, and never carries a code or the host. That title fails both the scanner's strong-title gate and the browser lifecycle adapter's ShareableContent window check, which uses the same matchers. Two parts of the original claim are wrong. First, the miss is not silent and not only at the candidate stage. In the observed misses Chrome was admitted (discovery_shadow_pick, title_match true, presumably on a transient code-bearing title). The adapter then logged shareable_content_no_match reason initial with the code-less Meet title in candidate_titles and window_present false, so no lifecycle starting and no prompt followed. Second, the title shape is not "<event> - Google Meet". The matcher has been unchanged since 88871c85 (END5, 2026-07-12), so it is identical at c56f2869, in all three work-laptop eras, and at HEAD.
- Field: demonstrated (5 misses (1 certain, 3 probable, 1 possible) of 6 counted Chrome call episodes). Chrome picks at 2026-10-06T08:58:38Z, 2026-10-07T09:03:34Z, 2026-10-21T12:32:54Z and 2026-11-11T13:59:31Z are each followed by shareable_content_no_match reason initial whose candidate_titles hold a Meet title with no room code and no host (word Meet, colon, quoted name; in one case the bare word, named 1.2 s later), window_present false, no lifecycle starting, no prompt. Named title then stays on screen 27.8, 13.6, 23.5 and 12.8 min (rows are change-only, so the last figure runs to the match at 14:12:18Z). Fifth, possible only: 2026-10-07T12:00:03Z, the title matched for about half a second, then became the named shape and the prompt died after 0.18 s; one named row, no audio glyph, no duration. Control: 2026-10-05T15:08:39Z, title matched from the first read to the end, recorded (Meet identity inferred, a matching title is not logged). The tracer said no row could confirm this; the candidate_titles rows do. The title the scanner admitted on is not logged.
- Build eras: Era 1: 3 misses. Era 2: 2 misses. Era 3: no Chrome rows. Matcher byte-identical at ae035d58, 8d4a9449, f2d65038 and HEAD.
- Verifier: Code: I confirmed the snippet at HEAD and at c56f2869 (git log -L shows the last change was 88871c85 on 2026-07-12). In MeetingSourceScanner, decideBrowserAdmission admits a plain browser only on a url_fragment found in the window title, or on strongTitleMatchers, which holds only googleMeet. A weak match is logged as browser_tab_rejected, and noMeetingSignal is a silent continue. BrowserMeetingLifecycleAdapter.defaultTitleMatchers (googleMeet, browserTeams, browserWebex, slackHuddle) also drive the ShareableContent window-present signal. A title like Meet: "<name>" matches none of them, so window_present stays false. No guard, timer or fallback admits a code-less Meet title. Field pack: I wrote my own script at scratchpad/d001/a.py and b.py, run with python3 -I. It sorts all four events files by ts and filters on bundle_id com.google.Chrome. Across every Chrome shareable_content_no_match row, the candidate titles that contain "meet" break down as follows: 79 have the shape Meet: "W ..." (72 plain, 6 with a trailing speaker glyph, 1 other variant), 3 are the bare word Meet, and 1 starts with Google. None contains a 3-4-3 code or meet.google.com. Each one evaluates false under a re-implementation of the matcher. Episodes: Chrome picks (discovery_shadow_pick, title_match true) at 2026-10-06T08:58:38Z, 2026-10-07T09:03:34Z, 2026-10-21T12:32:54Z and 2026-11-11T13:59:31Z. Each is followed within about 25 to 250 ms by shareable_content_window_present reason initial value false, plus no_match rows carrying the code-less Meet title, and there is no lifecycle starting or prompt_shown at that point. On 2026-10-07 at 12:00:03Z, starting and prompt_shown fired, then ended 0.5 s later when the window went back to no-match. On 2026-11-11 the prompt came only at 14:14:36Z and timed out. Compare the one successful Chrome recording on 2026-10-05: window_present initial was true there. Eras: 10-06 and 10-07 fall in era 1 (before 10-12), and 10-21 and 11-11 in era 2. No Chrome Meet episode occurs in the newest era (from 11-12), but the matcher is unchanged at HEAD, so the defect persists there. Corrections to the original claim: the drop is logged (shareable_content_no_match with candidate_titles), and the real title shape differs from the extractor's "- Google Meet" form. I lowered severity to high because Meet in Chrome is a minority of the work-laptop calls (Teams dominates). It is still a full miss for every scheduled Meet. I did not re-derive the claimed on-screen durations.
- Direction: Stop using the page title as the admission test. Admit a browser on evidence that it is capturing the mic (see redesign), and use the title or URL only to name the meeting. As a stopgap, treat the Meet brand at the start of the title as a weak candidate that is confirmed by the mic, and capture real titles (lobby, in call, named, post-call) into the fixture before touching the pattern again.

**D002. The mic-in-use catch-all (DET1) has never fired, so nothing backstops a title miss**

- Severity high (confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.swift:183`. Related: DET1, DET3, DET10, DET11, F039, F037. Effort M.
- Owner sees: A call the title gate misses is supposed to raise a quiet generic prompt after about 30 s of mic use. It never appears. This is also the mic usage is not identified correctly complaint.
- Mechanism: At HEAD and at c56f2869 the only mic-busy input is AVCaptureDevice.isInUseByAnotherApplication, read on the device that was the default when the watcher started (MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.swift:165 KVO, :183 sample, :100 poll backstop). MicBusySpanTracker.update emits .started on the first true sample, and the DET1 tier runs only on an open span. On the work laptop this property never read true in any build era, so the DET1 catch-all and the DET3 mic-busy spans have been dead throughout. The device was not nil, because mic_device_unavailable (emitted since DET10, 23992db1, 2026-08-15, so in all three eras) never appears. Why the property stays false is still not established: it could be structurally false for this audio setup, or the launch-time device could be stale.
- Field: demonstrated (0 mic-busy rows of any kind in 114,553 events). 0 mic_busy_started, 0 mic_busy_ended, 0 mic_in_use_prompt, 0 mic_device_unavailable across 12 launch proxies (startup permission bursts), while process_audio_is_running_input read true 420 times (Teams 415, Zoom 5) and 74 recordings ran, 68 of them on the built-in microphone. MicBusySpanTracker emits started on the first true read, so the property never read true. None of the five missed Meet calls got the fallback prompt. Cause not established by the log; a stale launch-time device (audit F039) is a weak explanation when 68 of 74 recordings resolved the built-in microphone.
- Build eras: All three: process-audio-true rows 354, 58 and 8 by era, mic-busy rows 0 in each. Emitter lines present at ae035d58, 8d4a9449, f2d65038 and HEAD.
- Verifier: Code. The snippet is at MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.swift:179-186 and is unchanged from c56f2869, where it sits at lines 166-170. Samples come from two places: the KVO with .initial, and a backstop poll in armPollTimer that runs every 3 to 12 s whatever the session state. PERF17 gates only the scans, not sampleMicBusy. In MicBusySpanTracker.update, a busy=true sample with no open span returns .started with no debounce, and emitMicBusy logs it as detector/mic_busy_started. So a single true read anywhere would leave a row. I found no guard, timer or caller that would suppress the emit. If AVCaptureDevice.default returned nil, the code logs mic_device_unavailable instead.

Field pack. I ran the session scratchpad and d002b.py with python3 -I over all events*.jsonl: 114,553 events parsed. The only actions containing "mic" are micgate_attached 6, mic_tap_stall_detected 4 and micgate_start_failed 1. That means 0 mic_busy_started, 0 mic_busy_ended, 0 mic_in_use_prompt and 0 mic_device_unavailable. The detector category is logged (candidate_dropped 445, discovery_shadow_pick 108, browser_tab_rejected 1), so the category is not filtered out. Rows of process_audio_is_running_input with value True: 420 (com.microsoft.teams2 415, us.zoom.xos 5).

Per era, split by ts:
- before 2026-10-12: 344 true reads, 23 recordings, 0 mic_busy
- 2026-10-12 to 2026-11-12T09:19: 68 true reads, 45 recordings, 0 mic_busy
- newest: 8 true reads, 6 recordings, 0 mic_busy

Another process held the input all through these windows, yet the property never read true even once. The signature is therefore demonstrated in every era, including the newest build, which is close to HEAD. The fallback prompt is missing because no span ever opens.

Corrections to the claim:
- The applies_to label should be both, not claimed_fixed_not_holding. The producer is identical at c56f2869 and HEAD, and the defect holds on the work laptop at HEAD-era code too.
- The root cause is still open. A nil device is ruled out by the missing mic_device_unavailable row. Stale launch device versus structural false is not settled.
- Direction: Replace the input, not the wiring: listen on the HAL process-object list and each object's IsRunningInput (the mechanism that reads true in this log), which also gives attribution without guessing from the frontmost app. Run the DET10 probe once to close the question of why the AVCapture property stays false.

**D018. An unanswered prompt becomes Skip after 30 s and the call is then held off for its length**

- Severity high (confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingSessionController.swift:1033`. Related: F011, UX10, DET7, END11, F045. Effort S.
- Owner sees: You join, start talking, miss the small panel, and the meeting is not recorded at all.
- Mechanism: If nobody answers the prompt, it times out after prompt_timeout_sec, which is 30 s by default. The action then falls back to defaultPromptAction, and both config.example.toml and the code's nil fallback make that "skip". The skip path calls abandonPrompt, which cools the bundle down and arms the skip latch. Discovery keeps refreshing that latch while it still sees the app, so the call goes unrecorded unless the user starts it by hand: through the notification's start-late action, through the Suppressed menu row, or with a manual Record. In the work-laptop pack, 30 Teams prompt_timeout rows, all with skip, appear in eras A and B, and none in era C. Of those 30, 8 were followed by record_attached between 2.6 s and 1,987 s later, and 1 by a manual start 9 s later. Most of the rest had no recording until the next day.
- Field: demonstrated (30 Teams prompt_timeout rows (A 15, B 15, C 0 of 5 prompts)). Every row carries default_action skip 30.0 to 30.2 s after prompt_shown; process audio read true at 29 of the 30 engages. 19 calls then went unrecorded for 107 s to 3,199 s (longest: 2026-10-20T13:59:44Z 3,199 s, 2026-10-19T13:30:18Z 2,858 s, 2026-10-02T14:30:31Z 2,811 s); 8 were started by hand 33 s to 1,633 s after the prompt (6 by record_attached manual, 1 by the notification action, 1 by a source-less manual start). 0 timeouts in era C is 5 prompts in 22 h.
- Build eras: A, B; code unchanged in C and HEAD
- Verifier: Code at HEAD: MeetingSessionController.swift:1033 contains the claimed line verbatim. Lines 1027 to 1064 run the timeout closure. When the action is skip, it logs prompt_timeout, calls stateMachine.abandonPrompt, sets a Suppressed status for as long as isSkipLatched holds, and sends notifySkippedMeeting(origin: .timedOut). At c56f2869 the same line sits at :783. The latch lives in DetectionStateMachine (skipLatch arm, refresh and isLatched with skipLatchGraceSec), and the MeetingSessionController.swift:1508-1521 path refreshes it every time discovery sees the bundle, which is what holds the call off for its whole length. Nothing in the code path auto-records once the latch is set. The only recovery is the user acting on the notification, the menu row or Record. The shipped default is in config.example.toml:73 (default_prompt_action = "skip") and :64 (prompt_timeout_sec = 30). Field evidence: a script I ran over all four events files, sorted by ts (the session scratchpad It found 32 prompt_timeout rows: 30 for com.microsoft.teams2 and 2 for com.google.Chrome, every one with default_action skip. Each came 30.01 to 30.21 s after the nearest preceding prompt_shown. All of them fall between 2026-09-30 and 2026-11-11, which is eras A and B. The newest is 2026-11-11T14:15Z, before the 2026-11-12 rebuild, so era C has none. I defined "next recording" as the first recording_started or record_attached after the timeout. For the 30 Teams rows it was record_attached in 8 cases (2.6 s to 1,987 s later) and a manual start 9 s later in 1. For most of the rest, the next recording came hours later or the following day, for example 2026-10-02 09:16Z, 13:30Z and 14:31Z, and 2026-10-20 13:30Z, 14:00Z and 14:56Z. In other words, those calls were never recorded by detection. I did not re-derive the field checker's exact figure of 19 unrecorded calls with their durations. Why I did not refute it: the timeout to skip to latch behaviour is deliberate and can be changed in config, and the notification gives a way back. But the owner-visible symptom, a missed prompt leading to no recording, is the documented default behaviour, and the log shows it many times. The defect is the default policy combined with a quiet surface, not a broken guard. The parts of the claim about the August build's missing END11 are about the home Mac and do not matter for this verdict.
- Direction: Silence must not mean Skip in a single-user tool. On timeout either keep a provisional recording that is discarded if declined, or collapse to a one-click chip that stays for the life of the call; make hover pause the real timer. No-code today: set default_prompt_action to record.

**D030. A browser meeting has one end signal, the active tab title of an on-screen window, and nothing that can say the call is alive**

- Severity high (partially confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/Lifecycle/Adapters/BrowserMeetingLifecycleAdapter.swift:154`. Related: END5, END17, DET6, MIC7; audit F032, F158. Effort M.
- Owner sees: A Google Meet recording stops while the meeting continues after working in another tab, another desktop or a full-screen app for a while; or the last stretch of the meeting is missing from the notes.
- Mechanism: For a plain browser the only end evidence is ShareableContentSignal: is there an on-screen window (SCShareableContent with onScreenWindowsOnly: true) owned by the browser whose title (its active tab) matches a meeting pattern. Switching tabs, minimising, or moving to another Space or a full-screen app all read as ended, and nothing (no process-audio vetoer, no Leave control, no teardown line) can say the call is still up. Only a browser quit is definitive. On a source-attached browser recording at HEAD this opens an END17 hold that the 720 s lone dwell confirms with no possible veto, and the held tail is then trimmed (F032). At c56f2869 the same edge ends after the 120 s floor. The worse path, and the one the field pack shows, is not the hold. When the browser lifecycle engine is in .starting during a running recording, a browser_tab_title end ends it directly with no dwell (PromotionEngine.handleStarting, still present at HEAD and at c56f2869). This happens for a hand-started recording with no source: confirmLateStartIfRecording needs a source with the same bundle id, so the engine is never promoted to .inMeeting, and handleMeetingEnded honours any end for a source-less recording (the END11 bundle check is skipped when src is nil). One tab-title-off poll therefore stops the manual recording at once.
- Field: demonstrated (1 mid-call browser end in 2 browser-related recordings, by a path the entry does not describe). 2026-11-11 (era B, stem 20261111-155951, hand-started): browser title matched at 14:12:18.900Z (lifecycle/starting for Chrome), left at 14:14:28.160Z with zero Chrome windows in the on-screen list, lifecycle/ended 3 ms later, recording stopping 9 ms later; title present again at 14:14:36.352Z, prompt timed out at 14:15:06.823Z; transcript tail is mid-topic. No 120 s wait: the engine was in starting, where a tab-title end is direct. The 120 s floor route appears once and correctly: 2026-10-05T15:20:08.575Z provisional to 15:22:08.604Z ended, with a spoken close 1 s before the title left (120 s late, not early). Two Chrome prompts flashed on 2026-10-07 (title true, then false 0.5 s and 1.1 s later). Pre-pack candidate: 2026-09-28T17:38:30Z Chrome stop with speech running to the last second of the audio.
- Build eras: A: 1 genuine end via the floor. B: 1 mid-call end from starting. C: no browser recording.
- Verifier: Code at HEAD: BrowserMeetingLifecycleAdapter.swift:154-164 has the claimed snippet, and it maps ShareableContentSignal presence to .browserTabTitle live or ended. ShareableContentSignal.defaultFetch calls getExcludingDesktopWindows(false, onScreenWindowsOnly: true), and evaluate() treats as present only a window with the context bundle id and a matching title. So a browser window that is off-screen (another Space, minimised, behind a full-screen app) or showing another tab reads ended. A plain browser has no handle.meetingWindow (the AX title signal is PWA only), WorkspaceSignal fires only on quit, and vetoesLoneEnd is true only for processAudioIsRunningInput, which the browser adapter never emits. PromotionEngine: isDefinitive is true only for workspaceAppTerminated, and defaultLoneDwell is 720, so in .inMeeting the claimed hold and dwell path holds. c56f2869 has endDebounceFloor 120 for browserTabTitle and the same onScreenWindowsOnly probe, so the installed claim holds too. The field mechanism differs from the one D030 describes. handleStarting ends directly on a non-corroboration-required .ended ("Meeting ended before the recorder armed ... End directly"), at both commits. MeetingSessionController.confirmLateStartIfRecording returns false unless the recording has a source whose bundle id matches. handleMeetingEnded skips the other-app guard when src is nil and calls stopRecording. Field pack (python3 -I script over all four events files sorted by ts), era B (2026-11-11, before the 2026-11-12 rebuild): Chrome lifecycle engaged at 13:59:31 with window_present false. recording_started bundle_id manual (stem 20261111-155951) at 13:59:52. window_present true and lifecycle starting at 14:12:18.900Z. A poll with 0 Chrome candidate windows on screen at 14:14:28.159Z gives window_present false at .160Z and lifecycle ended at .163Z (leading browser_tab_title_left_meet_pattern, confirmed_by []). coordinator recording to stopping at .169Z, and recording_stopped bundle manual after 877 s of audio. The window was back at 14:14:36.352Z (new starting, prompt_shown), and prompt_timeout with default skip came at 14:15:06.823Z. So the live meeting was chopped and not resumed. There were no browser lifecycle rows in era C (from 2026-11-12). The 2026-10-05 provisional at 15:20:08.575Z to ended at 15:22:08.604Z is the 120 s floor route in era A. I did not check whether it was mid-call. The core claim, that the active on-screen tab title is the browser's only end signal and nothing can say the call is alive, is confirmed and caused a mid-call stop in the log. The specific HEAD consequence D030 claims (the 720 s hold and trim) is code-derived only. The demonstrated chop came through the unguarded .starting direct-end path on a source-less recording, which is still present at HEAD.
- Direction: Give browsers a liveness level: the browser's audio helper process holding mic input (HAL process objects matched by PID ancestry, which the resolver already supports) and far-side speech on the recorder's system channel; treat the tab title as a hint, not as the end.

**D050. The mic-in-use detector has never produced a reading, so nothing catches a browser call the tab title misses**

- Severity high (partially confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.swift:150`. Related: DET10 (partial, probe owed), DET1, DET3, DET11, F039. Effort M.
- Owner sees: A Meet (or any unlisted web) call is running with the orange mic dot lit and MeetingPipe shows no prompt at all. The README promises a quiet catch-all prompt in this case; it has never appeared.
- Mechanism: The DET1 mic-in-use catch-all and DET3 mic-busy spans have one input: AVCaptureDevice.isInUseByAnotherApplication on the device that was the default when MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.wireMicObserver ran (MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.swift:149-173 at HEAD, :137-160 at c56f2869). It is resolved once and never re-resolved on a default-input change, and a nil is never retried. Unlike HALSystemMuteProbe, it does not listen for kAudioHardwarePropertyDefaultInputDevice. The detector does produce readings, but on the work laptop not one of them ever read true. Across all 114,553 events (2026-09-29 to 2026-11-13, all three build eras) there are 0 mic_busy_started, 0 mic_busy_ended, 0 mic_in_use_prompt and 0 mic_device_unavailable, although there were 74 recording_started (66 of them Teams calls that held the mic). Because there is never a span, evaluateMicInUseTier can never return a source, so an unlisted or title-missed web call cannot get the catch-all prompt. The log cannot say whether the cause is a stale device binding or the property never reporting shared inputs. The pack also shows no specific Meet call that went unprompted.
- Field: demonstrated (0 mic_busy_started, 0 mic_in_use_prompt, 0 mic_device_unavailable in 38 active days; 59 idle picks and 408 engage-time readings with the per-process flag true). Every era's build could emit all three rows (strings date from 2026-07-12 and 2026-08-15). Examples of a proven mic client with the daemon idle and no span: discovery picks at 2026-09-30T10:02:45Z (era 1), 2026-10-15T14:41:46Z (era 2), 2026-11-12T15:46:06Z (era 3). mic_busy_started is logged on any rising edge, before the frontmost-app filter, so the device flag never read true. The recorder resolved the built-in microphone in 65 of 71 recordings, so a launch-time binding to a device that later went away does not explain a zero across the whole window by itself; the log cannot show which device the observer bound.
- Build eras: all three
- Verifier: Code. At HEAD, wireMicObserver calls AVCaptureDevice.default(for: .audio) once from start(). On nil it logs mic_device_unavailable and returns with no retry. Otherwise it stores micDevice and adds KVO on isInUseByAnotherApplication. sampleMicBusy reads only micDevice, and is driven by that KVO and the backstop poll. Nothing re-resolves micDevice; the only place it changes is stop(), which sets it to nil. MicBusySpanTracker.update returns .started on any rising edge while no span is open, with no frontmost-app filter. emitMicBusy then logs it without condition, and sampleMicBusy runs even outside idle (only scheduleScan is gated by PERF17). Log.event has no verbosity gate. evaluateMicInUseTier needs micBusyTracker.open to be non-nil before it can emit mic_in_use_prompt. I found no other producer of a mic-busy span: grep finds micBusyTracker.update and onMicInUseDwell only in this file. The same code shape and all three event literals are present at c56f2869. So the mechanism holds at both HEAD and c56f2869, and no guard, timer or caller rescues it.

Field pack. My script (the session scratchpad plus grep over logs/events*.jsonl) found:
- 114,553 events from 2026-09-29T09:45Z to 2026-11-13T07:44Z, with app_rebuild at 2026-10-12T08:24Z and 2026-11-12T09:19Z.
- 0 mic_busy_started, 0 mic_busy_ended, 0 mic_in_use_prompt, 0 mic_device_unavailable.
- Detector-category actions are only candidate_dropped 445, discovery_shadow_pick 108 and browser_tab_rejected 1, so the watcher was running and logging.
- 62 detector rows have process_audio_is_running_input true (60 picks, 2 drops).
- 74 recording_started: 66 Teams, 3 Zoom, 1 Chrome, 4 manual.
A rising edge should fire during any of the 66 Teams recordings regardless of idle state, yet none appears in any era. The dead signature is therefore demonstrated in the log in all three build eras, including the newest one from 2026-11-12. The recorder log shows repeated "input device changed mid-recording" re-arms, which is consistent with the default input moving, but this does not prove the stale-binding branch over the property-semantics branch.

Downgrades:
1. "Never produced a reading" is imprecise: the detector reads, it just never reads true.
2. The owner-visible Meet miss is inferred: the pack shows no specific unprompted Meet call.
3. Critical becomes high, because this is a backstop tier and the owner's daily Teams calls are found by the AX and per-process paths.
- Direction: Do not repair this signal; replace it. Ask CoreAudio which processes hold input (process object list with IsRunningInput, excluding the daemon's own PID) and treat any holder for N seconds as the catch-all trigger, named by its bundle. Keep an explicit health state so a detector that has not produced a reading in days says so in the menu and in doctor.

**D064. A prompt nobody clicks within 30 s becomes a skip that suppresses the call for its lifetime**

- Severity high (confirmed). Location `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingSessionController.swift:1033`. Related: UX10, DET7, F011, START1. Effort M.
- Owner sees: 'It did not catch my meeting': the panel appeared while joining, timed out, and the menu bar reads Suppressed until the call is over. Even an answered prompt loses the seconds before the click.
- Mechanism: With default_prompt_action skip (ConfigStore.swift:315 fallback "skip", the same fallback at MeetingSessionController.swift:1033, ConfigDefaults.promptTimeoutSec 30, ConfigDefaults.autoConsentApps []), an unanswered prompt runs DetectionStateMachine.abandonPrompt after 30 s. That arms the reprompt cooldown and the SkippedMeetingLatch, and the menu bar shows Suppressed(<app>) while the latch holds. Discovery sightings refresh the latch, so it lasts while the meeting stays visible, not strictly for the call's lifetime: several timeouts were followed by a fresh prompt for the same bundle 8 to 56 minutes later. The same code is at c56f2869 (lines 783 and 802). On the work laptop, events.jsonl through events.3 hold 32 coordinator/prompt_timeout rows, all default_action skip (30 com.microsoft.teams2, 2 com.google.Chrome, none for Zoom), each followed by state_change prompting->idle within 0.2 s. They run from 2026-09-30 to 2026-11-11, so they fall in eras 1 and 2 and none in the post-2026-11-12 era. 8 were followed within an hour by a direct idle->recording start, 1 of them manual. The time from prompt_shown to that start was about 33 to 2017 s.
- Field: demonstrated (32 prompt_timeout rows; 9 followed by a hand start (8 recordings)). default_action skip on all 32. 13 had Leave, Mute and mic at the pick. Lost head on the 8 hand-started recordings: 32.9, 39.2, 55.6, 89.3, 153.6, 1107.9, 1633.2, 2017.5 s. Examples: 2026-11-05T15:30:18Z (attach 1602.9 s later, 20261105-175701), 2026-10-01T14:35:35Z and 15:01:01Z (attach at 15:08:43Z, 20261001-180843), 2026-11-06T15:28:25Z (source-less start 9.0 s later).
- Build eras: A 16, B 16, C 0 of 5 prompts
- Verifier: Code at HEAD: MeetingSessionController.swift:1033 reads defaultPromptAction with a "skip" fallback. In the default branch, line 1052 calls abandonPrompt(source:), which runs cancelPromptTimeout, recordCooldownEnd, armSkipLatch and setIdle (DetectionStateMachine.swift:270-275). Lines 1059-1062 then set statusUI.setSuppressed, which polls isSkipLatched, and send notifySkippedMeeting(origin: .timedOut). The timer guard (DetectionStateMachine.swift:301) acts only while the state is still .prompting for the same source, so a click inside 30 s wins. Nothing else turns a timeout into a recording unless the action is record or byo. ConfigDefaults: promptTimeoutSec = 30, autoConsentApps = []. ConfigStore.swift:315 falls back to "skip". At c56f2869 the same snippet sits at line 783 and abandonPrompt at line 802, so the label "both" holds. The latch (SkippedMeetingLatch.swift) is refreshed by discovery sightings and lapses after skipLatchGraceSec without one, so "for its lifetime" means "while discovery keeps seeing it". In the log some timeouts were followed by a new prompt 693 to 3582 s later, so the suppression is not permanent. The field check was re-run with python3 -I over the four event files sorted by ts (script at the session scratchpad It found 32 prompt_timeout, all default_action skip, 30 Teams and 2 Chrome, and 417 prompt_shown and 6 user_skipped, matching the claim. Each timeout was followed by prompting->idle within 0.2 s. A direct idle->recording start (not through a prompt) followed 8 of them, at 2.8, 9.0 (manual), 25.6, 59.3, 123.5, 461.4, 1077.8, 1603.2 and 1987.4 s after the timeout. That is 9 starts, because the 2026-10-01 14:35 and 15:01 timeouts both precede the same 15:08:43 start. Adding the 30 s prompt window gives a lost head from prompt_shown of about 33 to 2017 s, consistent with the checker's list. The first timeout was 2026-09-30T13:19Z and the last 2026-11-11T14:15Z, so the signature is demonstrated in the build eras before 2026-10-12 and from 2026-10-12 to 2026-11-12, and is absent from the newest era, which has only about one day of data. The Zoom 2026-10-13 example in the original claim is not in the work-laptop pack, which has no Zoom timeouts, so it came from another log. I did not re-verify the checker's sub-claim that 13 timeouts had Leave, Mute and mic present at the pick. The mechanism is the configured design behaviour, not a crash path, but it directly produces the owner-visible "did not catch my meeting" plus Suppressed symptom on the work laptop.
- Direction: For a single-owner tool invert it: begin capture at detection (pre-roll into a provisional session) and make the panel Keep or Discard, with timeout meaning keep. Short of that, set default_prompt_action to record and make hover pause the acting timer.

**D084. Browser Meet admission needs a room code in the active tab's title, pinned by tests, with no captured browser title to back it**

- Severity high (confirmed). Location `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/Lifecycle/Adapters/LifecycleAdapter.swift:105`. Related: END5, DET6, START2, VALOWN1. Effort S.
- Owner sees: Google Meet meetings in the browser are identified sometimes, but rarely.
- Mechanism: MeetingTitlePatterns.googleMeet (LifecycleAdapter.swift:96-110, unchanged since END5 88871c85 and identical at c56f2869 and HEAD) accepts a browser title only if it contains meet.google.com, or "meet" plus a 3-4-3 lowercase code. A scheduled Google Meet in Chrome on the work laptop titles its tab `Meet: "<event name>"`, with no code. The same matcher therefore blocks the meeting in two places. (1) Admission: decideBrowserAdmission with strongTitleMatchers returns .noMeetingSignal, not even weakTitleOnly, because no defaultTitleMatcher fires on that shape. The browser is dropped silently, with no candidate and no browser_tab_rejected event. (2) Lifecycle: when admission does happen through some other window or a moment when a code was visible, BrowserMeetingLifecycleAdapter's ShareableContentSignal runs the same matcher over SCWindow titles. It reads not-live, so lifecycle/starting and the prompt never come. Case (2) is what the field pack shows. The comment's "a real in-call tab title always carries Meet ... code" is false for named meetings. One correction to the checker: the title_match=true on discovery_shadow_pick is preTitleMatch, which the scanner hardcodes to true for every admitted browser. It is not independent proof of a code match.
- Field: demonstrated. detector/discovery_shadow_pick winner_kind browser, then signal/shareable_content_window_present value false reason initial with a shareable_content_no_match row, and no lifecycle/starting. FOUND for 4 of 9 browser picks (2026-10-06T08:58:38Z, 2026-10-07T09:03:34Z, 2026-10-21T12:32:54Z, 2026-11-11T13:59:31Z); 79 unmatched Chrome candidates over those 4 days have that shape and 0 carry a code. A fifth pick fell inside a recording; the other 4 reached .starting and a prompt, and 1 became a recording.
- Verifier: Code: the snippet is present verbatim in LifecycleAdapter.swift:105-109 at HEAD and at c56f2869. strongTitleMatchers holds only googleMeet (BrowserMeetingLifecycleAdapter.swift:97). defaultTitleMatchers starts with googleMeet, and the adapter's shareableContent.start uses those matchers over SCWindow titles. The scanner calls collectSignals with preTitleMatch: true for every admitted browser, so the pick's title_match is always true and proves nothing. Field pack: I sorted events*.jsonl by ts with python3 -I and ran three checks. (a) There are 9 detector/discovery_shadow_pick events with winner_kind browser, all com.google.Chrome. (b) 4 of them are followed within 0.3 s by signal/shareable_content_window_present value=false reason=initial and by a shareable_content_no_match reason=initial: 2026-10-06T08:58:38Z, 2026-10-07T09:03:34Z, 2026-10-21T12:32:54Z and 2026-11-11T13:59:31Z. None of those is followed by lifecycle/starting at that point. (c) The candidate_titles of Chrome shareable_content_no_match events on those 4 days hold 491 titles, 83 of which contain "meet" (definition: lowercase substring). 0 of the 83 match the code regex and 0 contain meet.google.com. I checked the structure character by character without printing any text. Every such title begins with the letters "Meet", then a colon, a space and a quotation mark. Some also carry Chrome's speaker glyph, which Chrome adds to a tab title while the tab plays audio, so these are live in-call tabs. That is the named-meeting title shape, and the regex cannot match it. Eras: 10-06 and 10-07 fall in the pre-2026-10-12 build. 10-21 and 11-11 fall in the 2026-10-12 to 2026-11-12 build. The newest era (from 2026-11-12) has no browser Meet activity, so the signature cannot be shown there, but the code at HEAD is unchanged. The other 5 picks produced value=true at initial and reached starting and a prompt, and 1 became a recording. This fits Meet being identified only sometimes. The silent admission miss (case 1) cannot be counted from the log by construction, but it follows from the same title shape. Severity stays high: it is the direct cause of the owner's symptom that browser Meet is rarely identified.
- Direction: Capture real Meet titles first, then decide the matcher from them; do not widen blind. Pair a weaker title match with the mic-holder reading instead of requiring the code alone.

**D094. A browser engage that fails to start is never torn down and keeps deciding starts and ends for hours**

- Severity high (confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.swift:283`. Related: D001, D004, D005; audit F038. Effort S.
- Owner sees: A Google Meet call in a browser is rarely picked up; prompts flash and vanish
- Mechanism: Discovery calls onDiscovered only when a scan has a winner (same line at c56f2869 and HEAD, unchanged since); lifecycle disengage happens only at the idle transition (Coordinator.swift:293 via disengageLifecycle), at stop (MeetingSessionController.swift:755), or implicitly when engage() for another bundle disengages first. A Chrome engage whose window read never matched therefore persists. Four such engages on the work laptop: 2026-10-06T08:58:38Z for 95.3 min (11 Chrome signal rows, ended by a Teams build), 2026-10-07T09:03:34Z for 176.5 min (43 rows, ended in a Chrome lifecycle starting and a prompt at 12:00:03.385Z that timed out within 0.2 s of idle), 2026-10-21T12:32:54Z for 119.5 min (191 rows, 28 shareable_content_unavailable), 2026-11-11T13:59:31Z for 15.0 min. In the last, a manual recording started 21 s after the stale engage (13:59:52Z), engageLifecycleIfNeeded skipped because already engaged, and the stale Chrome adapter raised lifecycle starting at 14:12:18Z and lifecycle ended at 14:14:28Z, which stopped the manual recording, followed by a fresh Chrome prompt that timed out.
- Field: demonstrated. Chrome ax_handles_built, then shareable_content_window_present value false reason initial, then Chrome signal rows continuing with no lifecycle starting until an ax_handles_built for another bundle or lifecycle idle. Found 4 times: 2026-10-06T08:58:38Z to 10:33:55Z, 2026-10-07T09:03:34Z to 12:00:03Z, 2026-10-21T12:32:54Z to 14:32:26Z, 2026-11-11T13:59:31Z to 14:14:28Z.
- Verifier: Read MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.swift:283, Coordinator.swift:506 and 293, MeetingSessionController.swift engageLifecycle/engageLifecycleIfNeeded/disengage sites; no no-winner disengage exists. Field numbers from my script: for each Chrome coordinator.ax_handles_built, walk forward until an ax_handles_built for another bundle, a Chrome rebuild, or lifecycle.idle; duration is end ts minus build ts; signal rows are category signal with bundle_id com.google.Chrome. The 2026-11-11 case is in the 2026-10-12 to 2026-11-12 era and the code path is unchanged at HEAD (no commit since touches it). Weak-token start via the window signal remains read from code, not proven.
- Direction: Give an engage that has not reached starting a short life (for example a few scan intervals without a renewed sighting), and tell the session when a scan stops admitting the engaged bundle. Use one matcher set for admission and for the window signal's start decision.

**D095. N01: The end-side Teams window matcher has not held a match through a Teams call since 2026-10-08, while the start side accepts the same titles**

- Severity high (confirmed). Location `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/Lifecycle/Adapters/LifecycleAdapter.swift:137`. Related: END14, F030, F031, G024, F040, N02, N03, D017. Effort M.
- Owner sees: In October the prompt flashed and vanished again and again and whole calls were lost; today the false window-gone reading is only masked, and it is what a start loses to when the mask slips.
- Mechanism: The two Teams window recognizers disagree, identically at HEAD and at c56f2869. On the start side, MeetingSourceScanner.isActiveMeetingWindow accepts any Teams title that is not an exact chrome word (HEAD line 639, `return !lowered.isEmpty`). On the end side, NativeLifecycleConfig.teams feeds MeetingTitlePatterns.teams into ShareableContentSignal, and that matcher needs a localized "meeting" stem or the substring "microsoft teams" (LifecycleAdapter.swift:137-142). In the work-laptop pack, starting 2026-10-08T14:31:18.668Z, Teams windows carry titles the end matcher rejects and the scanner accepts. I counted 1,264 shareable_content_no_match rows for com.microsoft.teams2 with a non-empty candidate list and no token from the list; the scanner predicate accepts at least one title in 1,262 of them, and before that moment there are 0 such rows. Era A (to the 2026-10-12 rebuild) logged 317 Teams lifecycle ended rows led by shareable_content_window_gone with empty confirmed_by: 217 on 10-08, 89 on 10-09, 10 on 10-12, and 0 before 10-08 14:31. Eras B and C logged 136 end_vetoed (leading shareable_content_window_gone, vetoed_by process_audio_is_running_input_false). Window-present readings of false went from 7 before the cutoff to 489 after. At HEAD the false gone reading is only masked by the END14 veto. The mask holds only while process audio resolves and reads running, and process_audio_object_unresolved is common (7,643 rows).
- Field: demonstrated. Confirm: signal shareable_content_no_match for com.microsoft.teams2 with a non-empty candidate_titles list while process_audio_is_running_input reads true, followed in era A by lifecycle ended with empty confirmed_by and in eras B and C by lifecycle end_vetoed. Found: 1,264 rows (A 328, B 878, C 58), first 2026-10-08T14:31:18.668Z, newest 2026-11-13T07:32:27.560Z. Refute: the window reading staying true through a whole call after that date; found on 4 of 40 era B recordings (over 90 percent true) and on 0 of 5 in era C.
- Verifier: Code: I confirmed the snippet at HEAD LifecycleAdapter.swift:137-142 and at c56f2869. In both, NativeLifecycleConfig.teams uses titleMatch MeetingTitlePatterns.teams, and the scanner's Teams native branch ends in `return !lowered.isEmpty` (HEAD line 639, c56f2869 line 474). ShareableContentSignal.evaluate sets present = any window for the bundle whose title passes titleMatch, so a window the scanner accepts can read gone. I found no guard that reconciles the two recognizers.

Field: I ran python3 -I scripts over events.3, events.2, events.1 and events.jsonl, 114,553 events sorted by ts. Definitions: a qualifying row is action shareable_content_no_match, bundle_id com.microsoft.teams2, candidate_titles non-empty, and no candidate containing any teamsTokens entry. Results:
- 1,264 qualifying rows, first 2026-10-08T14:31:18.668Z, last 2026-11-13T07:42:24.598Z. With a date-only era cut at 2026-10-12 00:00 and 2026-11-12 09:19 the split is A 316, B 890, C 58; the claimed split moves slightly with the exact rebuild timestamps.
- The scanner predicate, reimplemented, accepts at least one candidate in 1,262 of the 1,264 rows.
- For 651 qualifying rows the nearest earlier process-audio row was process_audio_is_running_input value true; for 54 it was false; the remaining 559 were preceded by process_audio_object_resolved.
- Teams lifecycle ended with leading_signal shareable_content_window_gone and empty confirmed_by: 317 rows, all after the 10-08 cutoff.
- end_vetoed: 136 rows, all after 2026-10-12, all with leading shareable_content_window_gone and vetoed_by process_audio_is_running_input_false.
So the signature is demonstrated in era A (calls ended on the window reading alone) and in eras B and C (vetoed). The newest era shows 58 rows and 6 Teams vetoes.

Not verified: the claimed per-recording window-true percentages (100, 47 and 0.3 percent), the 308 sub-second prompts, the 705 of 9,163 s recorded, and the "one lost race" at HEAD. I also cannot prove from titles alone that the rejected candidate is the call window, because I deliberately did not read titles. Process audio reading true and the abrupt onset on 10-08 strongly suggest a Teams title-format change that the end matcher does not tolerate.

Severity: I kept high. At HEAD the defect is masked only by a veto that depends on process audio resolving.
- Direction: Stop keying the end-side window signal on a title vocabulary. Capture the call window's identity (window id and owning pid) at engage and ask whether that window still exists, or at minimum route both sides through one recognizer so they cannot drift again, and pin it with captured titles in the detection corpus. Then drop the veto's job of hiding a signal that is known wrong.

**D096. N02: The veto that stopped the storm now depends on which of two asynchronous first readings lands first, and a lifecycle that loses is still counted as engaged**

- Severity high (confirmed). Location `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingSessionController.swift:1657`. Related: F030, F045, F046, END14, END17, CONC9, PERF15, N01. Effort M.
- Owner sees: A recording that restarted by itself did not stop when the call ended and ran 6 min 20 s until stopped by hand. On a prompted start the same race withdraws the prompt as it appears and raises it again.
- Mechanism: At HEAD (and in work-laptop era C, from 2026-11-12 09:19Z) END14's veto of a window-gone end in .starting only holds if ProcessAudioSignal's first reading has already been ingested. CONC9 (12c88a38) moved that first evaluate onto the signal's own queue, so the two first readings now race. When the window-false reading wins, PromotionEngine.handleStarting finds no live process-audio state in `states` and ends the engine with confirmed_by []. On END17's promptless resume the controller is still idle at that moment, so handleMeetingEnded falls into `default: break` and drops the end without logging it. The engine stays in terminal .ended, which absorbs every later ingest. engageLifecycleIfNeeded then only checks `isEngaged` (activeAdapter != nil), logs lifecycle_engage_skipped already_engaged, and leaves the dead lifecycle attached. After that the recording has no lifecycle end detection until a manual stop or the idle backstop. The prompt-path variant (the end reaches handleMeetingEndedDuringPrompt and withdraws the prompt) follows from the code but has not been seen in the field.
- Field: demonstrated. Confirm: lifecycle starting, then shareable_content_window_present reason initial value false and lifecycle ended with empty confirmed_by before the process_audio_is_running_input initial row, then lifecycle_engage_skipped already_engaged and no ending_provisional when process audio later reads false. Found once: 2026-11-12T15:46:06.598Z to .813Z, then 15:50:34Z with no verdict. Era C had 2 engages with a window-false first reading and lost 1; era B had 30 and lost 0. Refute: a lifecycle in_meeting row for that recording; none exists.
- Verifier: Code at HEAD. MeetingSessionController.swift:1655-1662 has the snippet exactly. MeetingLifecycleCoordinator.isEngaged (line 278) is `activeAdapter != nil` and never looks at the engine phase. PromotionEngine.ingest returns nil for `.ended` (line 265), so a terminal engine absorbs everything. handleStarting (lines 349-384) holds a reliable end only when veto(for:in:states) finds a live vetoer already recorded; otherwise it ends at once. handleMeetingEnded (line 1206) acts only in .recording or .prompting/.suppressed and has `default: break`, so an end that arrives while idle is dropped silently.

ProcessAudioSignal history. At 12c88a38^, start() ran evaluate(reason: "initial") inline. At HEAD, start() does `run { begin(...) }`, which is queue.async, so the first audio reading is asynchronous and competes with the window signal's first reading.

c56f2869 (the home Mac build) has no veto in PromotionEngine (0 matches), no already_engaged guard and no recording_resumed_after_end. This exact mechanism therefore does not exist there, so the label is head_only and not claimed_fixed_not_holding.

Field pack, which I re-derived with my own scripts over logs/events*.jsonl. Window: 2026-11-12T15:46:06 to 15:57, era C.
- Sequence: lifecycle starting .598, then coordinator recording_resumed_after_end .606, then shareable_content_window_present (reason initial, value false) .614.
- lifecycle ended at .614: leading_signal shareable_content_window_gone, confirmed_by [].
- process_audio_is_running_input (reason initial, value true) only at .616.
- coordinator state_change idle to recording at .677. The end arrived while the state was idle, and no coordinator event records it.
- coordinator lifecycle_engage_skipped already_engaged at .813.
- At 15:50:34.737 process audio read false (poll), and ax_leave_button_state went invalid at .823. No ending_provisional, ended or in_meeting row followed, and there was no lifecycle event at all until lifecycle idle at 15:56:54.658.
- state_change recording to stopping at 15:56:54.489, with no lifecycle verdict before it. The gap is 6 min 19.75 s from the audio-false reading.

Race tally. Definition: every lifecycle starting whose first window initial reading is false.
- Era C: 2 such engages. On 2026-11-13T07:32:27, audio initial (.555) came before window (.562) and the end was vetoed (end_vetoed). On 2026-11-12T15:46:06, window came before audio and the lifecycle ended.
- Era B, from 2026-10-13: all 30 such engages were end_vetoed. Era B never logs a process_audio initial row, so audio-first ordering there is inferred from the veto outcomes, not observed directly.
- The 10 window-false ends on 2026-10-12 predate the first end_vetoed (10-13).

Severity: one field occurrence, and the race window is milliseconds wide. But when it is lost, end detection is silently gone for the whole recording, so high stands.
- Direction: In PromotionEngine ignore an ended edge from a signal that has not read live in this session (audit F030's direction), which removes the race. Latch an end for every start origin, not only from prompting (F045). Make the already-engaged guard ask whether the engine can still produce a verdict and re-engage when it is terminal.

**D100. A resumed start that loses the first-reading race records on a terminal engine: no end detection, and the idle backstop's stand-down is off**

- Severity high (confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingSessionController.swift:1269`. Related: D032, audit F030, F045, F046. Effort S.
- Owner sees: After the app resumes a recording by itself, it never stops by itself; I get minutes of post-call audio and have to stop it by hand.
- Mechanism: The claim holds as stated. One addition: the trigger is wider than the resume path. Any start that calls beginRecording from .idle while the engine is already engaged in .starting has the same gap. That covers the END17 resume and also the auto-consent branch of handleMeetingStarted. A .ended verdict that arrives during the async recorder bring-up hits `default: break` in handleMeetingEnded, because the state is .idle and the REC7 latch exists only under .prompting/.suppressed. The start then commits onto a terminal engine. confirmRecording is a no-op outside .starting, isEngaged is true because the adapter was never disengaged, and PromotionEngine.ingest returns nil in .ended. After that, no end can be reached except a manual stop, an app quit handled elsewhere, or the silence backstop. The backstop can now fire on a live call, because lifecycleCoord.current.isLive is false for .ended. At c56f2869 there is no resume path, but the same latch gap is reachable through auto-consent.
- Field: demonstrated. recording_resumed_after_end (or any start from idle), lifecycle/starting, a signal row with previous null and an ended value, lifecycle/ended, all before state_change idle to recording; then lifecycle_engage_skipped already_engaged; later end-class signal edges inside the recording with no lifecycle row. FOUND once: 2026-11-12T15:46:06.598Z to .813Z, dead edges at 15:50:34.737Z and 15:50:34.823Z, stop at 15:56:54.489Z with no lifecycle or backstop row. These are the only two end-class edges in the pack that produced no lifecycle transition while their recording kept running.
- Verifier: Code at HEAD (MeetingSessionController.swift):
- Resume branch: handleMeetingStarted, lines 1112-1123, calls beginRecording(source:) straight from .idle with no setPrompting. The auto-consent branch (about 1154-1161) does the same.
- beginRecording, lines 465-670: sets recordingStartInFlight, awaits audioRecorder.start, then calls setRecording and later lifecycleCoord.confirmRecording(). It checks endObservedDuringStart only at line 638.
- handleMeetingEnded, line 1206 onward: latches endObservedDuringStart only in `case .prompting, .suppressed` (line 1269). `.idle` falls to `default: break`. The lifecycle consumer (lines 218-262) routes .ended there and does not disengage.

Core (MeetingLifecycleCoordinator.swift and PromotionEngine.swift):
- isEngaged is `activeAdapter != nil` (line 278), so engageLifecycleIfNeeded logs already_engaged and does not re-engage.
- PromotionEngine.confirmRecording guards on .starting only, and ingest returns nil in .ended. CallTeardownOracle's confirmProvisionalEnd needs .endingProvisional, so it is also a no-op.
- handleStarting ends directly on a reliable end unless a live process-audio reading is already in `states` to veto it. That is the ordering race.
- handleIdleAutoStop passes lifecycleCoord.current.isLive, which is false for .ended, so the native stand-down is lost.

Build history: the 26 lines added to MeetingSessionController between fcae9623~1 and HEAD (REC20 low-space) do not touch this path, so era C is the same as HEAD here. At c56f2869 the handleMeetingEnded switch, the REC7 latch and the auto-consent beginRecording are present and the engine ignores input once .ended. Only the END17 resume trigger is absent there.

Field pack, work laptop, era C, from a script I ran over events*.jsonl sorted by ts:
- 15:46:06.598Z: lifecycle starting.
- .606Z: recording_resumed_after_end, since_end_sec 38.6.
- .614Z: shareable_content_window_present with previous null, value false, reason initial.
- .614Z: lifecycle ended, leading_signal shareable_content_window_gone, confirmed_by [].
- .616Z: process_audio_is_running_input with previous null, value true. This arrived 2 ms after the end, too late to veto it.
- .677Z: state_change idle to recording.
- .743Z: recording_started.
- .813Z: lifecycle_engage_skipped already_engaged.
- 15:50:34.737Z: process_audio_is_running_input true to false.
- 15:50:34.823Z: ax_leave_button_state healthy to invalid.
- No lifecycle row of any kind from then until 15:56:54.489Z (state_change recording to stopping), followed by lifecycle idle at .658Z.
- No auto_stop_silence and no held_end_excluded for this stop. Tail = 15:56:54.489 minus 15:50:34.737 = 379.75 s.

Frequency: a second script counted idle-to-recording transitions where the engine had already published lifecycle ended after its latest starting. It found exactly 1 (this one). recording_resumed_after_end occurs exactly once in the pack. So the mechanism is shown once, in 1 of 74 recordings. When it fires it removes all automatic end detection and inverts the backstop's stand-down, which is why I keep the severity at high.
- Direction: When a start commits, check the engine: if it is ended or idle, re-engage and confirm instead of trusting isEngaged. Latch an end for idle-origin starts the way REC7 does for prompted ones, and have the engine ignore an ended reading from a signal that has not read live in this session.

**D106. NEW-1: a Teams log line of any age is applied as the current mute state and beats the live button**

- Severity high (confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/MicGate/MicGate.swift:184`. Related: D060, F036, G027, MIC8, END12 (flush clock); consequence path NEW-3. Effort S.
- Owner sees: Mic usage or mute state is identified wrongly
- Mechanism: LogMuteAdapter (Adapters/LogMuteAdapter.swift consume) keeps only the last parsed line of a batch, deduped against its own lastMuted, and hands MicGate an Event with no age; reading.at is only printed as line_ts. MicGate.applyMuteReading (MicGate.swift:184-193) stamps lastLogReadingAt = now() and MuteSourceFusion.accepts always accepts .log, then refuses every AX reading for 5 s. Both AX producers dedupe on what they already delivered (MeetingAXWindowWatcher lastEmitted, the probe's ax_mute_button_state_kept), so a stale log state stays until the next real toggle. The MIC10 VAD-contradiction rescue cannot catch it because it keys on the watcher's own AX fused state, not on the gate. Same code at c56f2869 (MIC8 db9725db, 2026-08-04, is its ancestor) and at HEAD. Field pack, re-derived: 38 log_mute_event, age = event ts minus line_ts, median 41.9 s, 26 of 38 over 5 s; in 11 of 38 the last AX reading before the log event held the opposite state and was newer than the log line; 8 of those 11 also had a line older than 5 s. Each was followed within about 3 ms by verdict_changed to the log's state, and the next mute reading came after 282, 409, 1999, 33, 117, 8, 1325 and 181 s (some of those next readings agreed with the log, so not every interval is proven wrong). The checker's 16 intervals and 5,121 s total did not reproduce. Its era-3 example (2026-11-12T15:39:42Z) has a 4.1 s line, under its own 5 s threshold, and is ambiguous. The clean examples are in eras 1 and 2.
- Field: demonstrated. micgate/log_mute_event whose ts minus line_ts exceeds 5 s, followed within ms by verdict_changed to the log's state while the last mute_state_polled or ax_mute_button_state says the opposite, then mute_reading_suppressed. FOUND: 16 intervals over 5 s in 14 recordings (5,121 s), for example 2026-10-12T15:30:35Z (line 59 s old, wrong for 1,999 s), 2026-10-07T13:22:01Z (line 1,170 s old), 2026-11-06T15:04:13Z (line 46 s old, wrong for 1,325 s), and one in era 3 at 2026-11-12T15:39:42Z (line 4 s old, 7 s).
- Verifier: Code path confirmed at HEAD: LogMuteAdapter.consume emits log_mute_event with line_ts and calls the sink with an Event with no timestamp. MicGate.applyMuteReading uses now() for lastLogReadingAt. MuteSourceFusion.accepts returns true for .log unconditionally and false for .ax within 5 s. I found no age guard anywhere. The AX side does not re-assert: MeetingAXWindowWatcher only calls onMuteEvent when fused != lastEmitted, and the probe logs ax_mute_button_state_kept without delivering. So the stale state persists until a real transition. The VAD-contradiction rescue keys on the watcher's AX fused == .muted, so it cannot fire when the log forced muted while AX reads unmuted. mute_state_cleared_vad_contradiction occurs 0 times in the pack. The same code exists in c56f2869. Field (python3 -I script over events*.jsonl, sorted by ts): median age 41.9 s, 26 of 38 over 5 s, and 11 of 38 cases where the AX side's last reading was opposite and newer than the line. Concrete sequences: 2026-10-07T13:22:01Z had AX unmuted at -0.2 s, then a log muted line 1170 s old, then verdict muted_by_app, then the AX poll unmuted was suppressed, with no AX muted until +282 s. 2026-11-06T15:04:13Z had AX muted twice within 0.85 s, then a log unmuted line 45.6 s old, then verdict hot, then nothing until recording_stopped 1325 s later. 2026-10-12T15:30:35Z had a log muted line 59 s old against AX unmuted, then verdict muted_by_app, and the AX unmuted at +3.4 s was suppressed (inside the window). These are in era 1 and era 2. Era 3 holds only one ambiguous case, but the code there is unchanged, so the defect is live at HEAD. Not every interval is provably wrong, since the ground truth is unknown and some next AX readings matched the log. Also, the checker's 16-interval/5,121 s figure did not reproduce. Severity stays high because the gate can be latched opposite to the button for tens of minutes.
- Direction: Carry the line's own time into the fusion and compare it with the time of the newest AX reading: a log line older than the newest AX reading, or older than a couple of seconds, must not overwrite it or open a suppression window. Have the 1 Hz poll re-assert its current state when the window lapses instead of deduping against what it believes it delivered. Pin it with a replay of the field rows above.

**D082. The start-miss instrument has no input on this Mac: zero mic-busy spans in 32 days while a call demonstrably held the microphone**

- Severity high (confirmed). Location `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.swift:165`. Related: DET10, DET1, DET3, DET11, F039. Effort M.
- Owner sees: Mic usage is not identified; calls the whitelist does not recognise get no prompt at all, and no report shows them.
- Mechanism: On the work laptop, detector.mic_busy_started, mic_busy_ended and mic_device_unavailable have zero rows in all 114,553 events (2026-09-29 to 2026-11-13), across all three build eras. That window covers 74 recordings and 417 prompts. Over the same span, signal.process_audio_is_running_input read value=true 420 times on 27 distinct days: 415 for com.microsoft.teams2 and 5 for us.zoom.xos (era A 344, era B 68, era C 8). The detector category is logged in this pack (445 candidate_dropped, 108 discovery_shadow_pick), and the scan poll that also calls sampleMicBusy is running. So AVCaptureDevice.isInUseByAnotherApplication on the launch-time default device never read true, while a meeting process demonstrably held audio input. As a result, DET3 spans are empty and the DET1 mic-in-use tier, which reads micBusyTracker.open, can never fire. The code does not settle whether the property misses contention (DET10) or the call used a non-default input device (F039). The field checker's numbers ("32 days", "six Zoom reads on 2026-10-13", "3 recordings and 8 prompts") were not reproduced and are superseded by the counts above.
- Field: not checked
- Verifier: Code at HEAD (MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.swift:149-172): wireMicObserver resolves AVCaptureDevice.default(for: .audio) once in start() and never retries. If it gets nil, it logs mic_device_unavailable (present since DET10, 23992db1, 2026-08-15, so in every work-laptop era). Otherwise it KVO-observes isInUseByAnotherApplication. armPollTimer also calls sampleMicBusy on every 3 to 12 s poll. MicBusySpanTracker.update has no filter, so any busy=true sample opens a span and logs mic_busy_started. evaluateMicInUseTier reads micBusyTracker.open, so without spans the tier returns nothing. The same code shape exists at c56f2869 (lines 137 and 152), so applies_to both holds.

Field pack (python3 -I script the session scratchpad over every events*.jsonl sorted by ts):
- 0 rows of mic_busy_started, mic_busy_ended or mic_device_unavailable, in all eras.
- The watcher demonstrably runs and logs category detector (445 candidate_dropped).
- process_audio_is_running_input value=true occurs 420 times on 27 days.
- micDevice was therefore non-nil (no unavailable event), and every sample read false.

I found no guard, timer or caller that would explain the absence other than the property reading false. Rebuilds and restarts re-resolve the default device, and the instrument is still dead in every era, but a Teams-selected non-default input could still explain it, so the cause stays open.

What is demonstrated in the log is the dead instrument, in all three eras including the newest (era C, from 2026-11-12). The downstream owner symptom, that an unlisted call gets no prompt, follows from code. The pack has no event that names an unlisted app holding the mic.
- Direction: Replace the device-level property with the all-process CoreAudio input read DET8 validated, logged always-on; run the owed DET10 probe only to close the question.

**D003. A prompt that is not answered in 30 s becomes a skip that follows the browser, not the meeting**

- Severity medium (partially confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/Coordination/DetectionStateMachine.swift:270`. Related: UX10, START1, DET7, DET9, DET13. Effort M.
- Owner sees: The panel shows up while the pre-join page is still open, goes away after 30 s, and the call that follows is not offered again while its tab stays visible. From the owner's side the meeting was not caught.
- Mechanism: On the default skip action, a prompt timeout calls abandonPrompt, which arms a 60 s cooldown and a skip latch keyed on the browser bundle id. handleDiscovery refreshes that latch silently on every scan whose confident winner is that browser. A browser wins on a strong Meet title alone: brand plus a 3-4-3 code, which pre-join and in-call tabs both carry. So the suppression lasts as long as any Meet-titled tab is the active tab in that browser, not as long as the call, and it lapses only after 60 s (5 polls of 12 s) without a sighting. This is the intended UX10 behaviour, and it comes with a menu-bar label and a start-late notification. The field pack does not show a live call lost to it. The 2026-11-11 timeout followed a lifecycle end on the tab's title leaving the Meet pattern, so the call may already have ended. That event also exposes a separate defect: a manual recording was stopped by the Chrome lifecycle.
- Field: demonstrated (2 of 7 Chrome prompts timed out; 1 lost the rest of a live call). 2026-11-11T14:15:06Z prompt_timeout default_action skip, 30.1 s after a prompt raised 8.6 s after the recording was cut; no Chrome row after 14:15:07Z and no recording until a Teams call at 21:23Z. 2026-09-30T13:19:07Z prompt_timeout on a tab the owner had already skipped at 12:56:05Z and 13:16:45Z. The same tab was re-engaged 1,222.9 s and 58.9 s after those skips, so the latch followed sightings of the tab, not the meeting. The tracer's claim that the prompt fires on the pre-join page cannot be tested: no row says when Join was pressed.
- Build eras: Era 1: 1 timeout, 2 re-prompts after a skip. Era 2: 1 timeout. Era 3: none observed. abandonPrompt unchanged at HEAD.
- Verifier: Code at HEAD 4949c9c0. DetectionStateMachine.swift:270-275 matches the snippet.
