MeetingSessionController.swift startPromptTimeout (about line 1027) turns any default other than record or byo into abandonPrompt, plus statusUI.setSuppressed and notifySkippedMeeting(.timedOut). handleDiscovery (1502-1522) refreshes the latch and returns without logging when isSkipLatched. handleMeetingStarted (1133) also returns while the latch holds. skipLatchGraceSec is DiscoveryScanCadence idle multiplied by 5, which is 60 s. Discovery calls onDiscovered on every scan that has a winner (MeetingDiscoveryWatcher about line 277). MeetingSourceScorer lets a browser win on its title alone. googleMeet (LifecycleAdapter.swift:97) requires "meet" plus a 3-4-3 code, which is not specific to being in the call. The same abandonPrompt and refresh code exists at c56f2869 (DetectionStateMachine:174, MeetingSessionController:1141). The mechanism holds in both versions; the only guards are clearSuppression (manual start or Always) and the resume window (END17). Field pack: I re-ran python3 -I over the four events*.jsonl files, 114,553 rows sorted by ts. Chrome counts: prompt_shown 7, user_skipped 2, prompt_timeout 2 (both default_action skip), recording_started 1. This matches the checker. 2026-11-11, era 2 (rebuilds of 10-12 to 11-12). A manual recording started at 13:59:52. At 14:14:28 the Chrome lifecycle reported ended (leading_signal browser_tab_title_left_meet_pattern), and that stopped the manual recording; this is a separate defect. lifecycle starting followed at 14:14:36.369, prompt_shown at 14:14:36.731 with timeout_sec 30, and prompt_timeout at 14:15:06.823. daemon.log has "prompt timed out -> idle + cooldown" and no Chrome line after it. Because the latch branch logs nothing, the log cannot tell whether the latch held or the tab closed. The title had just left the Meet pattern, so 'lost the rest of a live call' is not demonstrated. 2026-09-30, era 1. An explicit skip at 13:16:45 was re-prompted at 13:18:37, 112 s later. The latch therefore lapsed while the owner was still on Meet, which means sightings were intermittent and the latch did not hold for the whole time the tab was in use. That case is evidence against the claim, not for it. The claim that the prompt fires on the pre-join page fits the matcher, but no row records when Join was pressed, so it is untested. Severity is lowered because this is the designed timeout-as-skip behaviour (UX10), it has a visible surface and a start-late action, Chrome accounts for 7 of 417 prompts, and no field loss is proven.
- Direction: Do not let silence mean no. While the call is still up, keep a persistent quiet affordance instead of expiring into a latch, re-offer when the mic goes live, and key any suppression on a meeting identity. Consider recording into a discardable pre-roll while the question is open.

**D005. Selecting another tab or changing Space while the prompt is up dismisses it**

- Severity medium (confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/Lifecycle/PromotionEngine.swift:371`. Related: START1, END14, END17, F030. Effort S.
- Owner sees: The panel appears and vanishes within about a second of looking at another tab, the calendar or another desktop, before it is answered. It comes back only when the Meet tab is selected again and a poll runs.
- Mechanism: While the prompt is up, the engine is in .starting. Any poll in which the browser's on-screen windows no longer contain a matching title (the page retitles itself, a tab switch, or a Space change, because the probe uses onScreenWindowsOnly: true at 1 Hz) emits browserTabTitle .ended. That kind does not require corroboration and a browser has no process-audio veto, so handleStarting ends the meeting at once and handleMeetingEndedDuringPrompt dismisses the panel with no cooldown and no skip latch. This was seen twice in the field, both in era 1 (before 2026-10-12): 2026-10-07T12:00:03Z and 13:00:38Z. In both cases the trigger was the page changing its own title while the Chrome window stayed on screen. A tab switch or a Space change has never been observed causing it.
- Field: demonstrated (2 of 7 Chrome prompts). Exact signature found twice, not 0: lifecycle starting, window_present false, lifecycle ended with leading_signal browser_tab_title_left_meet_pattern and empty confirmed_by, state_change prompting to idle with no user_skipped or prompt_timeout. 2026-10-07T12:00:03.385Z prompt up 0.18 s (Meet title became the named shape). 2026-10-07T13:00:37.578Z prompt up 0.84 s (a Teams launcher tab, refused as weak 12.1 s earlier, native Teams recorded 20.4 s later). The path is proven; the trigger in both was the page changing its own title, not a proven tab switch or Space change. The same direct end from starting is what cut the manual recording on 2026-11-11.
- Build eras: Era 1: 2. Era 2: 1 direct end from starting (during a recording). Era 3: none observed. handleStarting direct end unchanged at HEAD (PromotionEngine.swift:377).
- Verifier: PromotionEngine.swift:371-379 at HEAD: for a kind that does not require corroboration, lead is event.kind, and the meeting ends directly with phase .ended. requiresCorroboration returns false for .browserTabTitle. veto(for:) needs a live process-audio reading, which the browser adapter never emits. BrowserMeetingLifecycleAdapter maps ShareableContentSignal.onChange(false) to browserTabTitle .ended. ShareableContentSignal.defaultFetch uses onScreenWindowsOnly: true, so a Space change also reads as absent. MeetingSessionController's .prompting branch calls handleMeetingEndedDuringPrompt, which cancels the timeout, dismisses the prompt and sets idle. It stamps no cooldown (unlike abandonPrompt). At c56f2869 the same direct end exists (PromotionEngine line ~295 'End directly'). Field pack, my script sorting events.*.jsonl by ts: there are 8 browser lifecycle starting rows. 2 of them show the exact signature: prompt_shown, then shareable_content_window_present false (reason poll), then lifecycle ended with leading_signal browser_tab_title_left_meet_pattern and confirmed_by [], then prompting to idle 0.18 s and 0.84 s after the prompt, with no user_skipped or prompt_timeout. In the 12:00 case, shareable_content_no_match listed the same Chrome window still on screen with a changed title. So it was a self-retitle, not a Space change. The three prompting-to-idle rows on 2026-09-30 are user skips, logged 2 to 4 s after the transition.
- Direction: Give the prompt its own lifetime: a title flip while prompting should park the panel, not end the meeting, unless a definitive signal (browser quit, mic released) arrives.

**D007. Misses are invisible: no row, no heartbeat, and the one miss auditor reads a dead input**

- Severity medium (partially confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingSourceScanner.swift:302`. Related: DET3, DET5, DET6, DET10, DET11, F163. Effort S.
- Owner sees: After a missed meeting there is nothing to look at. The app cannot say whether it saw a Meet tab and refused it, never scanned, or was not looking, and this has kept the same class open since June.
- Mechanism: At HEAD and at c56f2869, MeetingSourceScanner skips a listed browser whose windows give `.noMeetingSignal` with a bare `continue` (MeetingSourceScanner.swift:302) and logs nothing. Only `.weakTitleOnly` reaches `rejectedBrowserTabs` and becomes `browser_tab_rejected`, and the drop and unreadable lists are built from candidates only. No daemon code emits a periodic scan or heartbeat row. The DET3 miss auditor depends on `detector.mic_busy_started`/`mic_busy_ended`. That emitter exists (MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.swift:202), but it produced 0 rows on the work laptop across all three build eras, even with 66 Teams recordings, so the auditor has a dead input. One part of the claim is overstated: since DET10, `mp analyze-detection` reports the zero-span case as `no_spans` (analyze_detection.py:409), not as a clean result, so its output does not show "no misses". What it cannot tell apart is a quiet period from a blind auditor. The claimed log signature ("0 detector/lifecycle/signal/prompt rows after 2026-10-16T09:46Z") does not fit the work-laptop pack. It probably describes the home Mac.
- Field: demonstrated (32 of 38 active days have no browser row at all; 0 mic-busy spans for the auditor). The detector category holds only candidate_dropped (445), discovery_shadow_pick (108) and browser_tab_rejected (1); there is no heartbeat and no row for a browser whose title matches nothing. Every miss counted here is visible only because a scan engaged Chrome and the engage then logged on-screen titles for up to 176.5 min. A named-title call that no scan caught during page load is invisible, and mp analyze-detection has 0 mic-busy spans to audit. Neither the title the scanner admitted on nor the title the window read matched on is ever logged. Correction to the tracer: misses are partly observable on this machine, through candidate_titles.
- Build eras: All three eras.
- Verifier: Code: I confirmed the snippet at HEAD, and c56f2869 has the same clean-miss `continue`. Only `.weakTitleOnly` is recorded. `.noMeetingSignal` leaves no trace, and the daemon sources contain no periodic detector heartbeat. Field pack: I ran python3 -I over every events*.jsonl file, sorted by ts. The detector category holds only candidate_dropped (445), discovery_shadow_pick (108) and browser_tab_rejected (1). It has 0 mic_busy_started and 0 mic_busy_ended rows (grep -c gives 0 in all four files). That holds in every era, the newest included (from 2026-11-12T09:19Z: 23 candidate_dropped, 6 discovery_shadow_pick). Only 6 of 38 active days have any row that mentions Chrome or a browser (my definition: the serialized event contains 'com.google.Chrome' or 'browser'). So a scan that saw a browser with no meeting signal leaves no row, and the miss auditor has no spans to work with. The pack shows directly that the app cannot answer "saw it and refused" versus "never looked", which is the symptom. Parts I refute or correct: (1) the claimed silence after 2026-10-16 is false for the work laptop, where detector rows appear on 36 of 38 days. (2) analyze-detection does not report a dead input as clean. It labels it no_spans ("quiet window, or a silently dead input"), so the gap is about telling those two apart, not a false clean result. I did not find why mic_busy never fires (micDevice is nil, isInUseByAnotherApplication never reads true, or the mic_device_unavailable path); that needs a separate check. Severity lowered to medium: this is an observability gap that hides misses, not a cause of misses.
- Direction: Log a throttled row whenever a listed browser shows the Meet brand without admission (shape of the title, not its text), and a low-rate detector heartbeat with scan and trigger counts and mic samples read true.

**D015. Zoom counts as a call only while its Leave and Mute controls are in the accessibility tree, so pickup waits for a poll that catches them**

- Severity medium (partially confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingSourceScorer.swift:95`. Related: DET5, DET12, DET15, PERF6, F038. Effort M.
- Owner sees: A Zoom call is running and no prompt comes for tens of seconds, or at all; the prompt tends to appear right after the pointer moves over Zoom.
- Mechanism: The rule is real at both commits. A lone native with hasAudioLeg true, which includes Zoom (MeetingSourceScanner.swift:246 sets it false only for Webex), must pass isConfidentNativeMeeting: the toolbar, or at least 2 distinct signals (MeetingSourceScorer.swift:95-97, 146-148). c56f2869 is older than DET12 (62ea01e0, 2026-08-18), so it has no audio signal. Every work-laptop build era does include DET12. The title-only drops between picks that D015 cites are not from the work laptop. They come from the home Mac's ~/Library/Logs/MeetingPipe/events.jsonl: 18 drops and 9 picks on 2026-10-13, and process_audio_is_running_input is absent from those rows. On the work laptop the failure looks different. There are 8 Zoom detector rows (5 candidate_dropped, 3 discovery_shadow_pick), one per call, across 3 calls, one in each build era. Every drop shows Mute only: title_match false, leave_button false, toolbar false, process audio false, score 2. Each pick comes when process audio turns true. Two of the three picks are Mute plus audio, score 5, with no title. The time from the first logged Zoom drop to the pick was 12 s (2026-09-30), 64 s (2026-10-13) and 24 s (2026-11-12, newest era). In each case prompt_shown followed within 0.2 s of the pick. So in every era the 2-signal bar holds back a Mute-only Zoom until Zoom's input is running. DET12 audio is what released it, not the controls. The claim that the toolbar or Leave auto-hides is not shown on the work laptop. The log does point at two missing signals: the "zoom meeting" title literal matched in only 1 of the 8 rows, and Leave was absent in 7 of 8. The field checker's 2 rows with audio true and every control false (2026-10-15T14:41:54Z and 14:41:55Z) are com.microsoft.teams2, not Zoom, so they are not evidence for D015. Every Zoom call in the pack got a prompt (3 of 3). The log shows neither a missed Zoom prompt nor any link to pointer movement.
- Field: demonstrated (2 of 3 Zoom calls). detector/candidate_dropped with process_audio_is_running_input true and every control false, with no prompt_shown in the following minutes. FOUND: 2 rows, 2026-10-15T14:41:54Z and 14:41:55Z (scores 4 and 3); next prompt 14:46:42Z after a relaunch at 14:46:36Z. All other 443 drops have the flag false.
- Build eras: B, C
- Verifier: Code: I re-read MeetingSourceScorer.swift:95-97 and the lone-native branch at 138-149. hasAudioLeg is true for Zoom (Scanner.swift:246). NativeLifecycleConfig.zoom has usesProcessAudio: true (NativeLifecycleAdapter.swift:54-58), and the comment there admits Zoom was never measured. Scanner.swift:431-436 feeds audio into the signal set, and distinctSignalCount counts it. The Zoom toolbar needle is "meeting controls" (MeetingAXHandleBuilder.swift:366) and the title test is lowered.contains("zoom meeting") (Scanner.swift:612), both as claimed. The quiet poll is 12 s (DiscoveryScanCadence.swift:24). git: c56f2869 (2026-08-18 10:20) is not a descendant of 62ea01e0 (DET12), and it has the same isConfidentNativeMeeting with no audio. Field pack: I ran python3 -I over events*.jsonl (114,553 events, sorted by ts) and kept detector rows whose JSON contains us.zoom. That gives 5 drops and 3 picks, with the per-row signals listed above. The prompt_shown and recording_started times are from the same script. I ran the same script over the home Mac log, which reproduced the 18/9 title-only pattern without the audio attribute. That confirms D015's log evidence comes from the home Mac, where it fits the 'fixed_at_head_not_installed' label. I also searched the pack for drops with audio true: there are exactly 2, both Teams. Conclusion: the snippet and the gating mechanism hold, and on the work laptop they cause a pickup delay of 12 to 64 s in all three build eras, including the newest. The specific mechanism D015 names (title-only while the toolbar is hidden, fixed by DET12) is home-Mac evidence. On the work laptop the gate is released by audio arriving, and the second-signal gap there comes from the title literal and the Leave needle missing. I downgraded severity to medium because all 3 Zoom calls did get a prompt.
- Direction: Install HEAD to get DET12, and measure Zoom once. Then stop gating on scraped controls: a registered meeting process whose HAL input has been running for a few seconds is the call; title and controls only name it.

**D019. The mic-in-use catch-all has never fired, so FaceTime, Discord, WhatsApp, Telegram and every unlisted app are invisible**

- Severity medium (confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.swift:165`. Related: DET1, DET3, DET10, DET11, F039, F037. Effort M.
- Owner sees: A call in an app that is not Zoom, Teams, Slack or Webex never prompts; mic use is not noticed.
- Mechanism: The DET3/DET1 mic-busy input has never read true on the work laptop in any of the three build eras. Across 114,553 events (2026-09-29 to 2026-11-13) there are 0 mic_busy_started, 0 mic_busy_ended, 0 mic_in_use_prompt and 0 mic_device_unavailable, while process_audio_is_running_input read value true 420 times (415 com.microsoft.teams2, 5 us.zoom.xos) and discovery itself was clearly running (417 prompt_shown, 445 candidate_dropped). Because MicBusySpanTracker.update opens a span on the first busy=true sample with no dwell, and sampleMicBusy runs from both the KVO and the 3 to 12 s backstop poll regardless of session state or plausibility filtering, AVCaptureDevice.isInUseByAnotherApplication on the device resolved once at start never returned true at any sample, even during Teams calls where Teams held the mic. Since the DET1 catch-all tier is gated entirely on an open span (SpanLatch.evaluate returns nil with no span), it is dead, so FaceTime, Discord, WhatsApp, Telegram and unlisted browser calls have no detection path. Root cause (pinned non-rebound default device per F039 vs the property staying false for a shared input) is not separable from this log. The consequence for unlisted apps is code-derived; the owner used none in the pack. Overlaps independent-audit F039.
- Field: demonstrated (0 mic-busy rows in 114,553 events). mic_busy_started, mic_busy_ended, mic_in_use_prompt and mic_device_unavailable never occur across 12 launches, while process audio read true 420 times (415 Teams, 5 Zoom) and 74 recordings ran. MicBusySpanTracker opens a span on the first busy sample with no dwell, so the AVCaptureDevice in-use read never returned true at a KVO fire or a 3 to 12 s poll. That part is demonstrated; the consequence for unlisted apps is not observable, none was used.
- Build eras: A, B, C
- Verifier: Snippet confirmed at HEAD MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.swift:165 and at c56f2869 :152 (same code; DET10's mic_device_unavailable emitter landed 23992db1 on 2026-08-15, so it is present in all work-laptop eras and in c56f2869). Traced: start() calls wireMicObserver() unconditionally; the device is resolved once via AVCaptureDevice.default(for: .audio) and never rebound; sampleMicBusy() is called from the KVO (with .initial) and from armPollTimer's self-rearming poll, and emits mic_busy_started on the first true sample (MicBusySpanTracker.swift:55-59, no dwell, no bundle filter). Searched for a guard that would suppress emission: none; PERF17 only skips scans, not sampling. So zero mic_busy_started over 38 days with 74 recordings and 420 true process-audio input reads means the KVO/poll read was false at every sample. Re-ran counts with python3 -I over logs/events*.jsonl: total 114,553; mic_busy_started 0, mic_busy_ended 0, mic_in_use_prompt 0, mic_device_unavailable 0; process_audio_is_running_input value true: teams2 415, zoom 5 (false: 70 and 5). The checker's numbers reproduce. Signature demonstrated in all three eras (none of them has a single mic-busy row), including the newest era from 2026-11-12. Severity lowered to medium for the work laptop: the owner's calls there are Teams and Zoom, which whitelist discovery handles; the owner-visible symptom (an unlisted app never prompting) is not observed in the pack, only implied by the dead tier. Could not refute: no alternate path catches unlisted mic use.
- Direction: Replace the device flag with the HAL process-object list DET8 already resolves (which process has input running), listened to rather than polled, and let it open the generic prompt for any registered or unknown process.

**D027. END8's revert never reaches the engine: a healthy, already armed Leave button re-arms as a no-op, so an off-screen call window still ends the recording**

- Severity medium (confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/Lifecycle/Signals/AXLeaveButtonSignal.swift:232`. Related: END8, END14, END17; audit F002, G006. Effort S.
- Owner sees: Minimising the call, moving it to another desktop or going full screen in another app stops the recording even though the call window still has its Leave button.
- Mechanism: The mechanism is real at c56f2869, in era A and at HEAD. The host rescue calls the arm path with the Leave button it found, but _armIfNeeded (AXLeaveButtonSignal.swift:232) returns early when the armed element still probes healthy, and _evaluate only calls onChange when the state changes. So when the Leave control was healthy all along, no .live reaches the engine, and END8's same-class revert never fires. A window-gone hold then reverts only if the window itself comes back, which counts as a returning .live. In era A (before 2026-10-12, engine between bd32468c and 8d4a9449) a window-gone lead did not require corroboration, so it ended on the debounce. The pack shows the exact signature 3 times, not 4: provisional led by shareable_content_window_gone, then ax_leave_button_rearmed, then lifecycle_provisional_end_rescued with leave_buttons_found 1, then lifecycle ended with confirmed_by [] about 2 s later. The cases are 2026-10-08T14:38:37Z, 2026-10-09T09:17:28Z and 2026-10-09T14:31:32Z. The fourth case the checker cited (2026-10-09T09:18:49Z) does not match: there the Leave control went invalid and the end was confirmed by ax_leave_button_invalid, so it is a different path. The other 7 window-gone rescues in era A survived only because the window came back about 1 s later. In each of those, in_meeting follows a shareable_content_window_present row with previous False, not a Leave edge. Era A's leads were shareable_content_no_match title misses, not minimise. In eras B and C (END14, then END17/HEAD) there were 0 window-gone provisionals: all 136 window-gone ends were absorbed by the process-audio veto (end_vetoed). At HEAD the defect is latent. It matters only when process audio is unresolved or not live and the call window stays off screen for 720 s or more. In that case the lone dwell ends the recording and trims the held tail.
- Field: demonstrated (4 recordings, all era A; 0 of 16 rescues ever produced a Leave live edge). Exact signature three times: ending_provisional led by shareable_content_window_gone, ax_leave_button_rearmed plus lifecycle_provisional_end_rescued (leave_buttons_found 1), then lifecycle/ended with confirmed_by [] 2.0 to 2.1 s after the provisional: 2026-10-08T14:38:37.386Z to 14:38:39.455Z (stem 20261008-173712), 2026-10-09T09:17:28.281Z to 09:17:30.337Z (20261009-121708), 2026-10-09T14:31:32.182Z to 14:31:34.202Z (20261009-173127). Fourth, same lead with the Leave control reading invalid 0.3 s in: 2026-10-09T09:18:49.044Z to 09:18:51.435Z (20261009-121844). Last readings before each: mic-held true, Leave healthy. The debounce is 2 s (PromotionEngine.defaultDebounce = 2.0 at ae035d58), not 5 s. The HEAD consequence (a window-gone hold running to the 720 s lone dwell) is not seen: eras B and C have no window-gone-led provisional at all, 136 were vetoed instead.
- Build eras: A demonstrated. B and C: masked by the END14 veto, not fixed.
- Verifier: Code read. At HEAD, AXLeaveButtonSignal._armIfNeeded has `if let current = element, probe(current) == .healthy { return }`, and the same line is at line 205 at both c56f2869 and 8d4a9449^. _evaluate returns .steady when lastState == state, and it calls onChange only on an invalid or healthy edge. MeetingSessionController.rescueProvisionalEnd goes through lifecycleCoord.armLeaveButton to NativeLifecycleAdapter.armLeaveButton and then axLeaveButton.armIfNeeded. Nothing else emits a .live for the rescue. In PromotionEngine.handleEndingProvisional at HEAD, the hold reverts on a .live that is same-class, returning, or from a vetoer. A healthy Leave reading already sitting in states does not count, and tick does not look at it. tick checks only for a live vetoer, then the pair or lone dwell. ShareableContentSignal uses onScreenWindowsOnly: true, so minimising or moving the window to another Space does produce window-gone. Era A engine (8d4a9449^): tick promotes a non-corroboration lead on the debounce, and only a same-class .live reverts it. That matches the roughly 2 s ends in the log. Field pack, from scripts I ran with python3 -I over all events files sorted by ts: 16 lifecycle_provisional_end_rescued rows in total. Of the 11 window-gone provisionals in era A, 3 ended with confirmed_by [] 2.0 to 2.1 s after the provisional, each right after an ax_leave_button_rearmed (TECH-END2 internal retarget, healthy to healthy, so no emit) and a rescue with leave_buttons_found 1. That is the claimed failure exactly. Era boundaries come from the main/app_rebuild rows at 2026-10-12T08:24:58Z and 2026-11-12T09:19:44Z. In eras B and C, ending_provisional leads were only process_audio_is_running_input_false (41) and ax_leave_button_invalid (22). end_vetoed was 136, every one window-gone vetoed by process audio. The one era C ended led by window-gone came from .starting, not from a hold. So the symptom is demonstrated in era A (END10-era code). In the newest era the process-audio veto has masked it, and at HEAD it remains code-derived. I lowered severity to medium because at HEAD the Teams/Zoom exposure needs process audio to be not live plus 12 or more minutes off screen, and an off-screen window that returns still reverts the hold. Webex and Slack have no vetoer and stay exposed to the 720 s lone dwell.
- Direction: Stop depending on an emitted revert: have the decision read current levels, so a Leave button that reads healthy now (or a mic that reads live now) contradicts a window-gone lead without anyone having to re-send it.

**D032. A signal's first reading is forwarded as an end edge, and an explicit start reaches .starting late, so a seconds-old recording can be ended directly**

- Severity medium (partially confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/Lifecycle/Signals/ShareableContentSignal.swift:173`. Related: END11, PERF15, CONC9, REC7, START1; audit F030, F045. Effort S.
- Owner sees: A recording started by hand (hotkey, menu, start-late notification, or an automatic resume) stops within a second or two of starting.
- Mechanism: The core mechanism is real and present at both commits: ShareableContentSignal forwards its first reading (lastValue nil) as a change, so an initial window_present false reaches the engine as an end, and handleStarting ends a .starting directly for a reliable kind unless a live process-audio reading vetoes it (END14). The predicted owner symptom, a hand-started recording stopped within seconds, is not observed: all 8 lifecycle_engage_explicit_start engages had process_audio .live recorded first and the 4 with a false first window reading were end_vetoed; recording_ended_during_start has 0 rows. The demonstrated harm is different: on the resume start of 2026-11-12T15:46:06Z (era C, discovery engage, not explicit) the Leave first reading put the engine in .starting at .598, the window first reading false at .614 ended it (confirmed_by empty) before process_audio .live at .616, the controller was still idle so the end was dropped, and the recording that started at .743 ran with an engine already in terminal .ended, so its later Leave-invalid and mic release at 15:50:34 produced no lifecycle end. The same first-reading shape killed an era B prompt at 2026-10-12T07:30:54 (prompt shown then cleared within 20 ms, owner restarted by hand). Within 3 s of lifecycle/starting, 306 lifecycle/ended rows followed a first-reading false (295 era A, 10 era B, 1 era C).
- Field: demonstrated (1 in era C (of 2 at-risk engages), with a different consequence than predicted). 2026-11-12T15:46:06Z, a resume start: ax_leave_button_state healthy (previous null) at .597, lifecycle/starting .598, shareable_content_window_present false (previous null, 2 Teams windows on screen, no title match) at .614, lifecycle/ended confirmed_by [] at .614, process_audio true (previous null) at .616. The other era C engage with the same order of Leave first (2026-11-13T07:32:27.544Z) was saved because the mic reading landed before the window reading (end_vetoed). The predicted direct stop did not happen: the controller was idle during bring-up, so the end was dropped (recording_ended_during_start 0 rows, lifecycle_late_start_confirmed 0 rows). Instead the recording started against a terminal engine: at 15:50:34.737Z and .823Z the mic release and Leave invalid produced no lifecycle row, and the recording ran 380 s more. In era A the same first-reading edge ended 302 engines in starting (the prompt flashes); in era B 29 were vetoed.
- Build eras: A 302 (prompt phase). B vetoed. C 1 during a recording start.
- Verifier: Snippet found at ShareableContentSignal.swift:173 (HEAD) and the same lastValue logic at c56f2869 line 137. handleStarting ends directly for non-corroboration kinds without veto. handleMeetingEnded honours an end only in .recording for the same bundle; when the end lands while idle it is dropped, and the engine then absorbs everything in .ended, which is what the field pack shows for stem 20261112-174606. The specific explicit-start race that stops a fresh recording is plausible from code (handleMeetingEnded would honour it once state is .recording) but every logged explicit engage was saved by process audio arriving first, so that consequence stays code_derived; the defect itself is demonstrated in era C with a worse, silent consequence.
- Direction: An end may only come from a signal that has read live in this session (the browser adapter's hasMatchedMeetingTitle gate, applied to every signal), or drop edges altogether in favour of sampled levels.

**D034. Installed build: a second signal from another evidence class confirms an end on arrival (the mic-switch chop)**

- Severity medium (confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/Lifecycle/PromotionEngine.swift:349`. Related: END2, END6, END15, END17, DET8. Effort S.
- Owner sees: Changing the microphone inside a Teams or Zoom call, or a brief audio restart, stops the recording.
- Mechanism: The instant cross-class confirm in handleEndingProvisional exists at c56f2869 (lines 349-354, verbatim) and is still present at 8d4a9449 (END14, line 447), which is the code the work laptop ran from 2026-10-12 to 2026-11-12. It was removed in 01e904a5 (END17, 2026-10-24). At HEAD a weak second .ended is absorbed and only isDefinitive (workspaceAppTerminated) ends a hold on arrival. tick() confirms only after a dwell, and a returning signal reverts the hold. So the defect is live on the home Mac (c56f2869) and was live on the work laptop in era B. It is fixed in the work laptop's era C build and at HEAD. Field pack: exactly 1 instance, in era B, on 2026-10-22 for stem 20261022-150025. In that instance an ax_leave lead was confirmed by process_audio false 5.72 s after ending_provisional, and the user re-attached manually 23 s later with Leave, mute and mic all live. Era C has 0 fast confirms of any kind.
- Field: demonstrated (1 in the pack (era B); 1 more confirmed by transcript before the pack, mechanism probable). 2026-10-22 (stem 20261022-150025, build 8d4a9449): ax_leave_button_state invalid and ending_provisional at 12:14:53.287Z, lifecycle_end_held live_signal call_is_up at .443, process_audio false at 12:14:59.002Z, lifecycle/ended confirmed_by [process_audio_is_running_input_false] at .006Z. Two Teams windows on screen before and after; record_attached manual at 12:15:21.948Z found Leave, mute and mic all live; transcript tail is mid-sentence and the next head is mid-conversation. So this happened on END14 code ten days after it shipped, not only on the August build. Pre-pack: 2026-09-14T14:20:36Z (20260914-163053, era A code) stopped 6.5 s after a hold was logged, mid-topic, and was re-attached by hand 405 s later. Era C: 0; the one comparable event (mic release at 2026-11-12T15:39:16.639Z, Leave invalid 1 s later) was held for 22.0 s and reverted.
- Build eras: A probable (pre-pack). B 1. C fixed by the pair dwell.
- Verifier: Code. git show c56f2869 confirms the snippet in handleEndingProvisional verbatim. git log on PromotionEngine.swift shows the cross-class branch survived END14 (8d4a9449, 2026-10-12, line 447 of that revision). It was removed by END17 (01e904a5, 2026-10-24). At HEAD the .ended case confirms only when event.kind.isDefinitive, and isDefinitive is true only for workspaceAppTerminated. Every other .ended is absorbed into endingProvisional and left to tick()'s dwell (pairHeld needs two signals ended for at least confirmDwell). In the .live case, a returning signal, a same-class signal or a vetoer reverts the hold. I found no guard at c56f2869 or 8d4a9449 that stops the instant confirm. In the incident, END14's lifecycle_end_held (call_is_up) fired and still did not prevent the ended verdict.

Field evidence. The script the session scratchpad counts lifecycle ended rows that meet all of these conditions: confirmed_by is non-empty and is not lone_dwell, the row comes within 60 s of the preceding ending_provisional, and that ending_provisional is for the same bundle. It found 17 in era A, 37 in era B and 0 in era C. Only one of them has process_audio_is_running_input_false as the confirmer after an ax_leave lead: 2026-10-22T12:14:59.006Z, com.microsoft.teams2, dt 5.72 s, era B.

The raw sequence for that one:
- ax_leave_button_state invalid at 12:14:53.286Z.
- ending_provisional at .287.
- lifecycle_end_held with live_signal call_is_up at .443.
- process_audio_is_running_input false at 12:14:59.002Z.
- lifecycle ended at .006, followed by recording_stopped for 20261022-150025.wav.
- record_attached manual at 12:15:22Z, with ax_handles_built found_leave and found_mute true and process_audio initial value true.

That is the chop signature. The other fast confirms are process_audio leads confirmed by ax_leave, mostly consistent with real hang-ups, and I did not judge them.

Severity. I lowered it to medium because neither the current work-laptop build nor HEAD can take this path. Only the home Mac build and the historical era B could.
- Direction: Already removed in source; needs the install. Then take the owner's outstanding END15 measurement so the 60 s dwell is a measured number.

**D036. A browser call has one weak end signal, so every browser end is late: 120 s installed, 720 s at HEAD**

- Severity medium (partially confirmed). Location `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/Lifecycle/PromotionEngine.swift:291`. Related: END5, END17 (owner decisions 1 and 2), ADR 0017 'Negative, accepted', PD-3, F032, DET6, MIC7. Effort L.
- Owner sees: After leaving a Google Meet call in Safari or Chrome the recording keeps running. Installed build: the HUD still reads Recording for two minutes. HEAD: it reads Confirming end for twelve minutes. The owner stops it by hand.
- Mechanism: For a regular (non-PWA) browser call, the only weak end signal is `.browserTabTitle` (ShareableContentSignal). The other routes are an app quit, which is definitive and ends at once, and an AX window-title signal that exists only for PWAs. TeardownHoldPolicy.consult returns nil for any hold whose kind is not `.native`. So at HEAD the 60 s pair in PromotionEngine.tick cannot be reached for a plain browser tab. The tab title leaving the meeting pattern is confirmed only by the 720 s lone dwell, with HUD "Confirming end" for the whole time. A browser quit still ends immediately, and a PWA can reach the 60 s pair through windowTitleLeftPattern, so "every browser end is late" overstates it. The 720 s is a deliberate owner decision (END17, commit 01e904a5, 2026-10-24). Its purpose is to survive a presenter sharing another tab, and the hold start is recorded as a trim point. At c56f2869 (home Mac) and in work-laptop build A (before 2026-10-12), the same single signal had `endDebounceFloor` 120 s. Work-laptop field evidence: the only sourced browser recording (20261005-180843, com.google.Chrome, build A) was ended by the lifecycle automatically 120.0 s after the tab title left, not stopped by hand. The 2026-10-16 Safari manual stops cited by the reporter are not in the work-laptop pack. HEAD's 720 s path has never been exercised on the work laptop.
- Field: demonstrated (1 of 1 sourced browser recording). 20261005-180843: lifecycle/ending_provisional (browser_tab_title_left_meet_pattern) 2026-10-05T15:20:08.575Z, lifecycle/ended 15:22:08.604Z with confirmed_by [], 120.0 s later; 121.5 s of tail after the last transcript segment. That is build A's 120 s floor. HEAD's 720 s lone dwell was never exercised: builds B and C have no sourced browser recording.
- Build eras: A demonstrated; B and C not exercised
- Verifier: Code at HEAD: BrowserMeetingLifecycleAdapter.start emits .browserTabTitle from ShareableContentSignal and .workspaceAppTerminated from WorkspaceSignal. It emits .windowTitleLeftPattern only when handle.meetingWindow exists, which happens for PWAs only. The snippet sits at PromotionEngine.swift:289-292. EndEvidenceClass gives browserTabTitle its own class, so `dwelled.count >= 2` needs a second weak class, and a plain tab never has one. handleInMeeting opens .endingProvisional for every non-definitive end. TeardownHoldPolicy.consult has `guard hold.context.kind == .native else { return nil }`, so no teardown route exists for browsers. defaultLoneDwell is 720 and defaultConfirmDwell is 60. The 15 min silence backstop is the only other automatic route. At c56f2869, endDebounceFloor returns 120 for .browserTabTitle and the adapter emits the same three kinds. The mechanism is therefore real in both. It is mitigated: a browser quit ends instantly, PWAs have a second class, the 720 s was an explicit owner tradeoff, and the hold start becomes a trim point. That is why I lower severity to medium. Field check: a python3 -I script over all four events files (114,553 events, sorted by ts) found exactly one browser recording_started, on 2026-10-05T15:08:43.954Z (com.google.Chrome, file 20261005-180843, build A). It had lifecycle ending_provisional (kind browser, leading_signal browser_tab_title_left_meet_pattern) at 15:20:08.575Z. lifecycle ended followed at 15:22:08.604Z with confirmed_by [], then state_change recording->stopping 5 ms later. That is an automatic end 120.0 s after the title left, matching build A's floor. It is not a manual stop. The other Chrome lifecycle ended rows were: two in the prompting phase (2026-10-07), and one on 2026-11-11 at 14:14:28Z that ended a Chrome lifecycle engaged during a manual recording (a different issue). No browser recording exists in builds B or C, so the 720 s HEAD path is code_derived only. The reporter's 2026-10-16 Safari manual-stop evidence does not appear in this pack. The 2-minute late end in build A is demonstrated in the log.
- Direction: Give the browser a second, independent end signal that does not depend on which tab is frontmost: the browser's audio helper stops capturing the mic (HAL process objects, already enumerated for Teams and Zoom), and optionally per-tab URL presence. Then a tab-title end plus mic release confirms in tens of seconds, and the lone dwell becomes a rare fallback instead of the normal path.

**D039. Any matching title inside the hold reverts it, so back-to-back browser meetings fuse into one recording**

- Severity medium (confirmed). Location `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/Lifecycle/PromotionEngine.swift:469`. Related: END17, ADR 0017 (lone dwell sized for a ten-minute tab share), F046, DET13. Effort M.
- Owner sees: The owner leaves one Meet call and joins the next within 12 minutes (2 minutes on the installed build). No new prompt appears; the HUD goes from Confirming end back to Recording; the Library shows one long meeting under the first meeting's title and workflow, containing both calls and the gap.
- Mechanism: The mechanism holds at HEAD and at c56f2869. A plain browser recording has one end-signal class (browserTabTitle). Its hold can only confirm on the lone dwell (720 s at HEAD; at c56f2869 it was the 120 s endDebounceFloor). During the hold, a browserTabTitle .live reverts the hold to .inMeeting, because it shares the lead's class. That .live fires whenever any window of the same browser bundle has a title matching any of the four matchers. Nothing in the engine identifies which meeting it is: the context is bundle plus pid, and the Meet code is thrown away. handleDiscovery returns early while recording (isAcceptingPrompts), so the second call never gets a prompt and is merged into the first recording. Not seen on the work laptop: the pack has 1 browser recording (Chrome, 2026-10-05). Its hold opened at 15:20:08Z and ended by dwell at 15:22:08Z with no revert. The field checker's "FOUND once" row is a us.zoom.xos native AX-led hold, a different mechanism, so it is not evidence for this item.
- Field: demonstrated (0 reverts in 1 browser provisional end). lifecycle_end_held led by ax_leave_button_invalid, then process_audio_is_running_input false and shareable_content_window_present false, then ax_leave_button_state healthy reason health_poll with no ax_leave_button_rearmed row, lifecycle/in_meeting and lifecycle_end_hold_released. FOUND once: us.zoom.xos 2026-11-12T15:19:45.186Z to 15:19:53.356Z (held_sec 8.2). The owner stopped by hand 1.3 s later, so the stuck state is inferred, not observed.
- Build eras: E1 n=1; E2 and E3 none
- Verifier: Code, HEAD (daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/Lifecycle/PromotionEngine.swift):
- Lines 469-470 are present as quoted. The guard accepts a .live of the lead's own evidence class, so a browserTabTitle .live reverts the hold to .inMeeting (line 480).
- tick() has two routes. pairHeld needs two classes with ended readings. loneHeld needs 720 s (defaultLoneDwell). A regular Chrome tab context has only browserTabTitle, plus workspaceAppTerminated, which fires only when the whole browser quits. So the hold sits for 12 minutes, and any matching-title return in that time reverts it.

Code, browser side:
- BrowserMeetingLifecycleAdapter feeds ShareableContentSignal with all four defaultTitleMatchers.
- ShareableContentSignal line 149-150 reads present when any summary with the same bundleIdentifier has a matching title. A second Meet tab, or even an unrelated "... | Microsoft Teams" tab, therefore reads live.
- No meeting-code identity exists anywhere in the engine. The code is only used in MeetingTitleResolver for naming.

Code, discovery:
- MeetingSessionController.handleDiscovery opens with guard isAcceptingPrompts, so no new prompt is raised while recording.

c56f2869:
- The same `.live where evidenceClass == leading.evidenceClass` revert exists, with a 120 s endDebounceFloor for browserTabTitle. So the label "both" holds, with a 2 minute window there.

Field pack (python3 -I script the session scratchpad over all 114,553 events sorted by ts):
- Browser bundles have exactly one recording_started (com.google.Chrome, 2026-10-05T15:08:43.954Z, era 1).
- That recording has one ending_provisional, led by browser_tab_title_left_meet_pattern at 15:20:08.575Z. It was followed by ended at 15:22:08.604Z (120 s, pre-END17 floor) and recording_stopped. There was no in_meeting revert.
- The other Chrome activity is starting, prompts, skips and timeouts with no recording.
- So no instance of the fusion appears in any era, and browser meetings are rare for this owner (1 of 74 recordings).
- The checker's cited instance is us.zoom.xos, led by ax_leave_button_invalid. It is irrelevant to the browser same-class-title mechanism.

Severity is lowered to medium: the defect is real in code but has low field exposure on this machine.
- Direction: Carry a per-call key (the Meet code from the title or URL; a Teams or Zoom call id where one is readable). Same key returning reverts the hold; a different key ends the first recording at the hold start and starts a new one.

**D041. The idle backstop is not a ceiling: it stands down for native apps and any sound resets it**

- Severity medium (partially confirmed). Location `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/SilenceAutoStopPolicy.swift:24`. Related: TECH-END3, END7, REC17, G025, ADR 0017 'The backstop'. Effort S.
- Owner sees: Everyone else has left and the owner forgot to click Leave, or an end was missed. The recording continues for hours with a 'Still in a meeting?' notification at 8 and 15 minutes of silence, or with no notification at all if music, a video or the next call is playing.
- Mechanism: The mechanism is real in the code, but the work-laptop pack shows no runaway recording. SilenceAutoStopPolicy.shouldAutoStop is byte-identical at HEAD and at c56f2869. At HEAD, MeetingSessionController.handleIdleAutoStop (about lines 1374-1400) stands down whenever a native source is lifecycle-live. It logs auto_stop_silence_skipped with reason lifecycle_still_in_meeting, calls keepAlive() and re-nudges. IdleStopBackstop.ingest resets the streak on a non-silent MicGate verdict or on system level above systemSilenceThresholdDb. It holds the streak on SystemChannel.down. The recorder holds a PreventUserIdleSystemSleep assertion. There is no wall-clock duration cap. The only other automatic bounds are the low-space stop and the WriterBacklogPolicy hard cap, and neither is a time limit. So for a missed end where the lifecycle verdict stays live, or where system audio keeps playing, the recording has no time ceiling. In the pack, the longest of the 74 recordings ran 97.3 min (start 2026-10-05T13:30Z), and it ended through a normal lifecycle ended at 15:07:32Z, not as an overrun. The pack contains no multi-hour recording.
- Field: demonstrated (1 recording where the backstop was the only automatic stop left and never accrued; 1 correct stand-down; 1 backstop stop). 20261112-174606 (E3): after the mic release at 2026-11-12T15:50:34.737Z the MicGate verdict was hot by RMS except for three stretches of 56.9, 2.4 and 25.0 s (verdict_changed rows 15:53:03Z to 15:56:17Z), so the streak never reached the nudge and no silence_notified was written in the 380 s before the manual stop. The stand-down clause was seen once and it was right: auto_stop_silence_skipped at 2026-11-10T14:44:06.666Z, after which the meeting ran 32 more minutes with speech. The backstop did end one missed end: auto_stop_silence at 2026-10-06T14:43:59.495Z, 562 s after mic release. An unbounded run was not seen because the owner stops by hand.
- Build eras: E1 (1 backstop stop), E2 (1 stand-down), E3 (1 no-accrual)
- Verifier: Code check: the snippet is at daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/SilenceAutoStopPolicy.swift:24, unchanged since c56f2869. I traced it through the caller and IdleStopBackstop and found no guard, timer or cap that bounds recording length by time.
- Recording length is bounded only by a manual Stop, a lifecycle end, the low-space stop or the writer-backlog byte cap.
- The streak resets on a hot MicGate verdict other than the confidentlyUnmuted floor (VAD or RMS), or when the system level is above the threshold. That covers far-side music, video or a following call, as claimed.

Field check, from my script over all four event files sorted by ts:
- 74 recording_started and 74 recording_stopped.
- 6 silence_notified, 1 auto_stop_silence, 1 auto_stop_silence_skipped.
- The field checker's "zero rows" claim is wrong. These rows exist.
- auto_stop_silence at 2026-10-06T14:43:59Z, Teams, 240 s after its nudge at 14:39:59Z. The backstop does fire for native Teams once the lifecycle is no longer live.
- auto_stop_silence_skipped at 2026-11-10T14:44:06Z, reason lifecycle_still_in_meeting. The earlier checker reports the meeting ran on with speech, so that stand-down was correct.
- Durations are recording_started to the next recording_stopped. The longest is 97.3 min, then 79.2, 76.3 and 73.3. I inspected the 97-min one, and it ended through lifecycle ended plus lifecycle_end_held, not as an overrun.

Verdict: the owner-visible symptom of a recording running for hours is not demonstrated in any build era of the pack. The mechanism is code-derived. I lowered severity to medium because the hole needs a lifecycle that wrongly stays live, or ongoing system audio, which overlaps the separate missed-end findings. Calling this backstop "the ceiling" is still inaccurate.
- Direction: Add one unconditional ceiling that no lifecycle state can stand down: after N minutes with no far-side speech from the meeting app (per-app output activity, not display-wide audio), ask once with a default of stop, and conclude any open hold so the trim applies.

**D046. An end that lands while an idle-origin start is in flight is dropped, and the engine then stays terminal (builds on F045)**

- Severity medium (confirmed). Location `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingSessionController.swift:1269`. Related: F045, REC7, END17 item 5, F046. Effort S.
- Owner sees: An auto-consented or resumed recording starts just as the call ends. It records an empty room until the owner stops it.
- Mechanism: The REC7 latch at MeetingSessionController.swift:1269 lives only in the .prompting/.suppressed branch; a lifecycle .ended that arrives while the state machine is still .idle during a resume/auto-consent start falls to default: break, and the engine sits in .ended so no later end arrives. Shown in the newest build era on 20261112-174606.
- Field: demonstrated (1 (1 of 1 resumes, 1 of 6 build C recordings); first field evidence for F045 and F030). 20261112-174606: recording_resumed_after_end 2026-11-12T15:46:06.606Z, lifecycle/ended (shareable_content_window_gone, confirmed_by []) .614Z, state_change idle->recording .677Z, no recording_ended_during_start, no lifecycle/in_meeting, lifecycle_engage_skipped already_engaged .813Z. Mic release 15:50:34.737Z produced no lifecycle row; stop with no cause row 15:56:54.489Z. The trigger is the audit's F030: the window's first reading (false) was logged 2 ms before the mic's first reading (true), so END14's veto had nothing to veto with. Build B started the signals synchronously, mic first (34 of 34 false-first-window Teams engagements); in build C the window led in 1 of 2 (the other, 2026-11-13T07:32:27Z, had the mic 7 ms ahead and logged end_vetoed).
- Build eras: C only (HEAD code identical)
- Verifier: Code at HEAD: `case .prompting, .suppressed:` holds the `if recordingStartInFlight { endObservedDuringStart = reason; return }`, followed by `default: break`. The same block is at c56f2869 (line 960), so both. Field, my script over the E3 window: recording_resumed_after_end 15:46:06.606Z (since_end_sec 38.6), shareable_content_window_present value false (initial) 15:46:06.614Z, lifecycle ended with confirmed_by [] and leading_signal shareable_content_window_gone 15:46:06.614Z, process_audio_is_running_input true (initial) 15:46:06.616Z, state_change idle to recording 15:46:06.677Z, recording_started 15:46:06.743Z, lifecycle_engage_skipped reason already_engaged 15:46:06.813Z. E3 has 0 recording_ended_during_start rows and 1 recording_resumed_after_end. The end was decided in the .starting phase on window-gone before process audio's first reading existed, which is the trigger; the controller then dropped it because the state was .idle. The recording kept running past the call.
- Direction: Make the in-flight start a state, or check one end latch for every origin immediately before `setRecording`.

**D051. A browser call carries no microphone evidence on the start side or the end side**

- Severity medium (partially confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingSourceScanner.swift:421`. Related: DET6, DET12, END5, MIC7, DET10. Effort M.
- Owner sees: Google Meet in a browser is detected only when the tab title happens to match and is admitted, and its end is detected only when the title changes. The fact that the browser is holding the mic, which is the most direct sign of a live call, plays no part.
- Mechanism: At HEAD, a browser or Chromium PWA candidate never gets a per-process mic read. MeetingSourceScanner.readsProcessAudio (MeetingSourceScanner.swift:421) is true only for a native kind whose NativeLifecycleConfig opts in, and BrowserMeetingLifecycleAdapter fuses only ShareableContentSignal, WorkspaceSignal and WindowTitleSignal, with no process-audio signal. So a browser call is admitted on a title match and ends on a title change. At c56f2869 there is no start-side process read for anyone, and the browser adapter is the same. The claim that the browser holding the mic "plays no part" is overstated in code. The DET1 MicInUseTier (MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.evaluateMicInUseTier plus MeetingSessionController.handleMicInUseDwell and handleMicBusyBundle) is device-level and keyed on the frontmost app. It can prompt for a browser after a 30 s mic-busy dwell when no whitelist winner exists, and it stops a DET1-tagged recording when the mic is released. It is a fallback, not corroboration for a title-matched browser candidate. On the work laptop that tier never ran in any build era: the pack has 0 mic_busy_started, 0 mic_busy_ended, 0 mic_in_use_prompt and 0 mic_device_unavailable events. So in the field, mic evidence played no part for browsers.
- Field: demonstrated (9 browser picks, all score 2 with every control and the audio flag false; 0 process_audio rows for a browser bundle; 1 browser recording). com.google.Chrome picks in era 1 (6) and era 2 (3); signal/process_audio_* rows exist only for com.microsoft.teams2 and us.zoom.xos; the Chrome recording 20261005-180843 ended on browser_tab_title_left_meet_pattern with confirmed_by empty. No browser activity in era 3.
- Build eras: eras 1 and 2 (no browser sighting in era 3)
- Verifier: Code: the snippet is present verbatim at HEAD. The doc comment says browsers answer false on purpose: a browser on the mic names the browser and never the tab, and admitting a tab on mic evidence is left to DET6. BrowserMeetingLifecycleAdapter's init takes only shareableContent, workspace and windowTitle signals, so the end side has no process-audio input either. git show c56f2869 on MeetingSourceScanner.swift finds 0 ProcessAudio references. MicInUseTier.swift does exist at c56f2869. Partial refutation: DET1 is a device-level mic path that does cover browsers. plausibleBundles is scanner.browserBundles plus micPlausibleBundles. It prompts only when the scan finds no winner, and the holder is attributed to the frontmost app, not to the process holding the mic. So "plays no part" is false as a statement about the code, but true for a title-matched browser candidate's scoring and its end. Field pack, from my own script (python3 -I over logs/events*.jsonl, 114,553 events). Browser discovery_shadow_pick rows: 9 for com.google.Chrome, 6 before 2026-10-12 and 3 from 2026-10-12 to 2026-11-12 09:19Z. All 9 have winner_score 2, title_match true, and leave_button, mute_button, calling_controls_toolbar and process_audio_is_running_input all false. process_audio_* rows exist only for com.microsoft.teams2 and us.zoom.xos. I found 1 browser recording_started, com.google.Chrome at 2026-10-05T15:08:43Z. Its lifecycle ended row at 15:22:08Z for com.google.Chrome has an empty confirmed_by. I did not see the reason string the field checker quoted, because my attribute key differed. No browser rows fall in the third era. A grep of the event log for any action containing "mic" found no mic_busy_* or mic_in_use_* actions, so the DET1 fallback was dead on this laptop for the whole pack. Its cause, a nil device or the in-use flag never reading true, is DET10 territory and separate from this item. Severity is lowered to medium because this is a deliberate, documented design gap. Meet is still detected and ended through its title. The field shows only 1 browser recording against 9 browser picks, and the pack cannot show how many browser calls were missed.
- Direction: Measure first (owner probe below), then let a browser candidate be corroborated by 'a process belonging to this browser is running input', and let the release of that input lead a held end for browser recordings the way it does for Teams and Zoom.

**D065. A recording started by hand with no detected source never adopts the meeting, so it has no automatic end**

- Severity medium (partially confirmed). Location `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingSessionController.swift:316`. Related: END11, DET13, F004, PERF17. Effort M.
- Owner sees: Detection missed the call, the owner pressed Record, and the recording kept going after the call ended. It is filed with no app, the default workflow and no title.
- Mechanism: A manual start with no live suppressed candidate records with source nil, and nothing later gives it an identity: handleDiscovery and handleMeetingStarted both return unless the state machine accepts prompts, the start's AX build, the MicGate engage and the Leave arm are all skipped because source is nil, and the resolved workflow is the default. So its only automatic stops are the idle backstop (which stops a source-less recording because SilenceAutoStopPolicy has no source kind to stand down for) and any lifecycle `.ended` from whatever adapter happens to be engaged, which a source-less recording honours for any app (END11 item 4 deliberately exempts source nil). Two parts of the claim are overstated: (1) "no lifecycle" is not absolute, because an unrelated adapter can engage and its end stops the recording (seen in the pack); (2) the backstop is not starved in practice, because the RMS gate is fed from the recorder tap even when MicGate.start was never called, so verdict_changed rows arrive within seconds of speech. Field impact is real but modest: of 4 manual recordings, 2 were stopped about 15 s after Teams discovery lost the call, 1 ran about 5 minutes past the loss and was stopped after the still-meeting nudge, and 1 was ended by an unrelated Chrome lifecycle end.
- Field: demonstrated (4 of 74 recordings; 6 of 113 meta sidecars have no source). 20261012-103055 (A, after 2 vanished prompts), 20261021-103054 (B, 10.3 s after a skip), 20261106-172834 (B, 9.0 s after a timeout), 20261111-155951 (B, no prompt at all). None has a lifecycle/in_meeting row. 3 ended with a stop that has no cause row (one after silence_notified at 975 s); the fourth was stopped by an unrelated adapter's end (N2).
- Build eras: A 1, B 3, C 0
- Verifier: Code (HEAD 4949c9c0): MeetingSessionController.swift:312-318, startManual falls to beginRecording(source: nil) when liveSuppressedCandidate() is nil. In beginRecording, recordStartBuild = source.map{...} is nil, so attachWhenBuilt (MicGate engage, END11 explicit-start lifecycle engage, Leave arm) never runs (:563, :624-626). handleDiscovery (:1502) and handleMeetingStarted (:1099) both guard isAcceptingPrompts, so nothing adopts a source mid-recording. Since PERF17 (2815e7d0, 2026-10-26) the watcher also skips scans outside idle (MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.swift:224); before that, scans still ran during recording (the pack shows detector/candidate_dropped during the Oct manual recordings), but their results were thrown away by that same guard. handleMeetingEnded (:1206-1250) honours an end from any bundle when src is nil, and handleIdleAutoStop stops source-less recordings. At c56f2869 the same startManual (line 205-209) and handleDiscovery guard (1129-1130) exist, so the label "both" holds. Backstop starvation subclaim: MicGate.ingest(rmsDb:) is called from Coordinator.swift:410 regardless of start(), and rmsGate.onChange publishes, so verdicts flow without an engage. In the pack, first verdict_changed came 48 s, 11 s, 0.1 s and 22 s after the four manual starts (script: first micgate/verdict_changed row after recording_started). The F004 dedupe can delay the first feed but did not starve it here. Field pack (python3 -I over logs/events*.jsonl sorted by ts): 74 recording_started, 4 with bundle_id manual (stems 20261012-103055, 20261021-103054, 20261106-172834, 20261111-155951), all in the 2026-10-12 to 2026-11-12 build era; none in the newest era. None has a lifecycle in_meeting row. Overrun, defined as time from the last detector/candidate_dropped teams2 no_confident_winner row to the recording->stopping state_change: 20261012-103055 14 s; 20261021-103054 about 5 min (dropped 07:43:16Z, silence_notified 07:47:09Z, stopping 07:48:17Z, no cause row, consistent with a user Stop after the nudge); 20261106-172834 15 s. 20261111-155951 had lifecycle starting then ended for com.google.Chrome (leading_signal browser_tab_title_left_meet_pattern) during the manual recording, and that end stopped it at 14:14:28Z, which contradicts "no lifecycle rows" but confirms the unrelated-adapter end path. So the signature (manual start, no adoption, stop without cause) is demonstrated in the middle era, but the owner-visible overrun it produced is one roughly 5-minute case, not a recording that ran on indefinitely; the idle backstop never had to fire (0 auto_stop_silence in these four). Downgraded to medium.
- Direction: Keep presence detection running during a source-less recording and adopt the first confident meeting: attach source, lifecycle and MicGate to the running session and log session_adopted.

**D097. N03: A prompt withdrawn by a lifecycle end arms no backoff and leaves no trace, so the next scan raises it again**

- Severity medium (confirmed). Location `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingSessionController.swift:1281`. Related: N01, N02, D018, DET13, END9. Effort S.
- Owner sees: The prompt appears and disappears before it can be clicked, then comes back, for as long as the call lasts; nothing in the menu or a notification says a call was seen.
- Mechanism: handleMeetingEndedDuringPrompt (MeetingSessionController.swift:1281 at HEAD, :971 at c56f2869, identical text) only cancels the timeout, dismisses and goes idle: no cooldown, no skip latch, and handleMeetingEnded's noteMeetingEnded also drops any attachable candidate. Nothing stops the next confident scan from re-engaging and re-prompting. At HEAD setIdle fires onIdleTransition, which calls discoveryWatcher.refreshNow (Coordinator.swift:289-297), so the re-raise is immediate. Field (era A only): 304 prompts withdrawn by a same-bundle lifecycle ended (within 2 s before) plus prompting to idle within 1 s; 298 of them followed by another prompt_shown for the same bundle inside 15 s with no timeout, skip or start between, median gap 10.9 s, first 2026-10-08T14:31:19Z, last 2026-10-12T08:11:36Z (before the 08:24:58Z rebuild). The ends were lone shareable_content_window_gone with confirmed_by [], the prompt visible about 15 to 30 ms.
- Field: demonstrated. Confirm: prompt_shown, state_change prompting to idle within 1 s with a lifecycle ended row and no prompt_timeout or user_skipped, then another prompt_shown for the same bundle and pid inside 15 s. Found 298 consecutive pairs, all era A, 2026-10-08T14:31:19Z to 2026-10-12T08:11:36Z. Not found in eras B or C, where the false end itself was vetoed.
- Verifier: Read both functions at HEAD and c56f2869; abandonPrompt is the only path that arms cooldown and latch, and the end path does not call it. Re-derived the field count with my own script (definition stated above) and got exactly 298 pairs, all era A. No guard found that refutes it; the absence in eras B and C is because END14/START1 stopped the false end, not because the withdraw path gained a backoff, so a false end during a prompt at HEAD would loop the same way and faster.
- Direction: Treat a starting-phase end as unproven: keep the prompt up, or keep the sighting as an attachable candidate and require the scanner to lose confidence before the prompt is withdrawn; if it is withdrawn while the scanner still sees the call, back off instead of re-raising.

**D098. N04: A hand start in the first seconds after a Skip or a timeout records source-less, because the prompt's own source is thrown away**

- Severity medium (confirmed). Location `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/Coordination/DetectionStateMachine.swift:270`. Related: D018, F004, DET7, DET13. Effort S.
- Owner sees: Pressing Record right after the prompt went away gives a recording with no app, the default workflow and no automatic stop.
- Mechanism: abandonPrompt (DetectionStateMachine.swift:270) arms cooldown and skip latch but writes no suppressedCandidate; the candidate is written only by handleDiscovery's latch or cooldown branch (MeetingSessionController.swift:1521, 1550) on a later confident scan. startManual (line 312) falls to beginRecording(source: nil) when liveSuppressedCandidate is nil. Same at c56f2869. Field, era B: of the abandon-then-start pairs within 120 s, the two hand starts at 10.3 s after user_skipped (2026-10-21T07:30:54Z, stem 20261021-103054) and 9.0 s after prompt_timeout (2026-11-06T15:28:34Z, stem 20261106-172834) recorded with bundle_id manual, with no discovery sighting logged in between; the attached starts were 2.8 s (notification action, record_attached), 25.6 s and 59.3 s after a timeout. At HEAD the onIdleTransition refreshNow narrows the window only when that immediate scan is confident.
- Field: demonstrated. Confirm: prompt_timeout or user_skipped for a native bundle, then recording_started with bundle_id manual inside about 12 s and no record_attached row. Found 2 of 2 (2026-10-21T07:30:54Z, 2026-11-06T15:28:34Z). Refute: a record_attached inside 12 s of an abandon; the only one is the notification action 3 s after a timeout, which carries its own source.
- Verifier: Traced both abandon callers (Coordinator+Delegates.swift:20 skip, MeetingSessionController.swift:1052 timeout); neither stores the prompt's source for startManual. Re-ran the field check and found the same 2 source-less starts in era B. No guard refutes it; the menu row 'Record this <app> meeting' is also driven by liveSuppressedCandidate, so it is absent in the same window.
- Direction: Seed the candidate inside abandonPrompt with the source the prompt already holds (noteSuppressedCandidate with the skip-latch origin), so a hand start attaches from the first second.

**D099. The Teams window signal has read 'gone' on live calls since Teams changed its window titles on 2026-10-08, and the matcher is unchanged at HEAD**

- Severity medium (confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/Lifecycle/Adapters/LifecycleAdapter.swift:137`. Related: D017, D027, END14 (documents the title change, fixed the symptom with a veto), audit F031, G024, F030, F040, DET15. Effort M.
- Owner sees: Era A: the recording stops seconds into a Teams call and the prompt keeps flashing. HEAD: nothing visible, but one of the two evidence classes an end needs is already spent on almost every Teams call.
- Mechanism: The claim holds, but the defect is already known. MeetingTitlePatterns.teams (LifecycleAdapter.swift:137-142, last changed by END5 on 2026-07-12) is byte-identical at c56f2869 and HEAD. It accepts a Teams window only if the title contains a localised "meeting" stem or "microsoft teams". From 2026-10-08T14:31:18.668Z the work laptop's Teams windows carry neither, so ShareableContentSignal reports the call window gone. Era A (before 2026-10-12): that reading ended .starting at once and confirmed .endingProvisional 2 s later. That produced the 4 chopped recordings and the start/end flapping that flashed the prompt. From END14 on (eras B and C): the process-audio veto absorbs it (136 end_vetoed, all led by window_gone and vetoed_by process_audio_is_running_input_false; B 129, C 7). PromotionEngine still stores the vetoed .ended reading in states. tick() then counts the callWindowOrControl class as ended even while the Leave button reads live. So at HEAD, after the mic is released, the Leave button adds no protection, and the class gate rests on the process-audio drop alone. END14 (filed 2026-10-12) already records this exact title change and explicitly declined to widen the title match. The tick half is the audit's G024. No task fixes the matcher. Era C shows no wrong end from it: all four era C recording ends were led by a process-audio drop and also had ax_leave_button_invalid.
- Field: demonstrated. signal/shareable_content_no_match for com.microsoft.teams2 with a non-empty candidate list while a recording runs; era A then shows ending_provisional led by shareable_content_window_gone and lifecycle/ended about 2 s later, eras B and C show lifecycle/end_vetoed. FOUND: 843 rows inside recordings, first 2026-10-08T14:31:18.668Z; 4 chops (2026-10-08T14:38:39Z, 2026-10-09T09:17:30Z, 09:18:51Z, 14:31:34Z); 136 end_vetoed rows (B 129, C 7); confirmed_by on all 4 era C ends includes shareable_content_window_gone.
- Verifier: Code. The teamsTokens snippet exists at LifecycleAdapter.swift:137 and is unchanged since 88871c85 (2026-07-12); it is identical at c56f2869. NativeLifecycleAdapter.swift:46 wires MeetingTitlePatterns.teams into ShareableContentSignal (line 168). ShareableContentSignal.evaluate reports present only if some window of the bundle passes titleMatch, and otherwise logs every candidate title of that bundle as shareable_content_no_match. In PromotionEngine at HEAD:
- handleInMeeting records a vetoed end in states and stays in .inMeeting (lines ~400-404). The comment there says the remembered window-gone is meant to pair with a later mic release.
- tick() builds endedSince per evidence class from any member reading .ended (lines 285-288) and never checks a live sibling. axLeaveButton and shareableContentWindow share callWindowOrControl, so a healthy Leave button does not cancel a stale window-gone.
- handleEndingProvisional only reverts on an arriving .live event, and an already-healthy Leave button emits none.

Field pack (my scripts, python3 -I; events sorted by ts across all 4 files):
- Teams no_match rows: 1,264 with a non-empty candidate list overall, the first at 2026-10-08T14:31:18.668Z. Defined as recording_started to recording_stopped, 859 rows inside recordings have candidates and 11 are empty (the claim says 843 of 854; the gap is the boundary definition).
- Of 2,511 candidate titles, none contains any teamsTokens entry, and only 1 contains "teams". The shapes are a bare single segment (1,385) and "Chat | ..." style (1,201 with a chat segment).
- Before the change, every no_match row had an empty list. So whenever Teams had a window on screen, one matched: the suffix used to be present on every window. This confirms a Teams-side title change.
- Era A lifecycle rows show ending_provisional led by shareable_content_window_gone, then ended with confirmed_by [] about 2 s later, at 2026-10-08T14:38:37/39, 2026-10-09T09:17:28/30 and 14:31:32/34. 2026-10-09T09:18:49/51 is the same pattern, but its end lists ax_leave_button_invalid. Many direct .starting ends on window_gone came every few seconds on 2026-10-09 (the prompt flapping).
- 48 of 49 Teams recordings started after the change had a non-empty no_match row or window_present=false while recording.
- The four era C recording ends (2026-11-12 14:33, 14:54, 15:45 and 2026-11-13 07:43) were led by process_audio_is_running_input_false, and each listed confirmed_by ax_leave_button_invalid plus shareable_content_window_gone. The window_present=false behind each was 184, 1091, 345 and 658 s old.

Evidence verdict: the defect caused the owner-visible chopping and prompt flashing in era A (demonstrated_in_log). At HEAD the effect is a weakened safety margin with no wrong end observed, which is why I lowered severity from high to medium. Known context: END14's own spec records this title change and rules out widening the match, and G024 covers the tick pairing. The applies_to 'both' label stands for the matcher. On the home Mac's c56f2869 build (no END14 veto), the same title change would chop recordings as era A did, if Teams there has changed its titles too.
- Direction: Stop using a title substring as proof that a call window exists. Tie the window signal to the window that owns the call controls, or drop window-gone as end evidence for Teams altogether, and count an evidence class as ended only when no member of it reads live and its ended edge falls inside the current hold.

**D102. The idle backstop reads a muted verdict as silence even while the owner is talking, and on this machine its horizon is 8 minutes**

- Severity medium (confirmed). Location `the repo root/daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/MicGate/IdleStopBackstop.swift:152`. Related: D033, audit F036, G027, F004. Effort S.
- Owner sees: 'Still in a meeting?' appears while I am speaking; a call the app has mis-read as muted, or a quiet live call, is one guard away from being stopped after 8 minutes.
- Mechanism: IdleStopBackstop.isSilent returns true for mutedByApp, mutedByHardware, silentByRMS and uncertain whatever the mic level (HEAD line 152, c56f2869 line 116), so under a muted verdict only far-side audio resets the streak. The stand-down (SilenceAutoStopPolicy.shouldAutoStop) keeps a recording only when the source is native and the lifecycle verdict isLive, which is false for endingProvisional, idle and ended. Field, era B: on 20261012-182939 the verdict went muted_by_app at 15:30:35.632Z on a headset mute event, an AX unmuted reading was suppressed at 15:30:39Z with winner log, no later verdict change, and silence_notified fired at 15:43:37.594Z; in the 240 s of recording time before the nudge the transcript's owner speaker label holds 230 s of speech and no other label any. On 2026-11-10 the auto-stop horizon was reached at 14:44:06.666Z and only the lifecycle_still_in_meeting stand-down kept the recording. Nudge to decision gap is 240 s in every pair (so a 480 s auto-stop setting, nudge at half), but no live call was stopped this way in the pack.
- Field: demonstrated. micgate/verdict_changed muted_by_app with no later change, coordinator/silence_notified 240 s into a stretch where the transcript shows the owner label speaking, then auto_stop_silence or auto_stop_silence_skipped 240 s after the nudge. FOUND: nudge during owner speech once (2026-10-12T15:43:37.594Z; no decision row followed 240 s later, so the streak was broken, and non-owner speech resumes about 32 s after the nudge); horizon reached on a live call once (2026-11-10T14:44:06.666Z, skipped). No live call was stopped this way in the pack.
- Verifier: Read the backstop and the host stand-down at HEAD; no guard looks at the mic level under a muted verdict. Re-derived the event sequence and computed speaker durations from the transcript json (labels only, no text) for the pre-nudge window. Owner-visible nudge during speech is demonstrated once; the auto-stop of a live call remains code-derived.
- Direction: Do not let the mute oracle stand in for silence: reset the idle streak on mic energy or voice activity whatever the mute verdict says, and refuse the auto-stop while the meeting app still holds the mic, whatever the lifecycle phase.

**D103. Teams' teardown line is not on disk when the call ends, so the newest build ends every Teams call on the 60 s pair dwell**

- Severity medium (confirmed). Location `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingSessionController.swift:1895`. Related: D044, D045, F031, G024, END12, END17, ADR 0017. Effort M.
- Owner sees: The meeting end is not caught: after leaving a Teams call the HUD reads Confirming end and the recording runs one more minute, every time
- Mechanism: Line 1895 itself is not a bug; it works as END17 designed it. The hold's only fast exit for a Teams end is CallTeardownOracle.decide. It confirms only when CallTeardownWatcher has already parsed an UnregisterCall line stamped at or after max(hold start minus 15 s, recording start). Otherwise it returns .hold and PromotionEngine's 60 s confirm dwell ends the call. In the newest work-laptop build (from 2026-11-12 09:19Z) the teardown line never arrived during any hold. So all 4 of 4 recorded Teams ends were held for 60.01 to 60.07 s with the HUD showing Confirming end, and each ended with confirmed_by [ax_leave_button_invalid, shareable_content_window_gone] followed by held_end_trimmed. The design fails on its premise from ADR 0017 / END9 that the line is already on disk when the provisional opens. The code does what it was told. Not every time across builds: in era 2 (after the log became readable around 2026-10-30) the oracle confirmed twice with teardown_observed and held_sec 0 (2026-11-09 16:02Z, 2026-11-12 07:39Z). Cost: about 60 s of extra recording and a delayed end, kept (trim only suggested) outside the regulated/NDA postures. No audio is lost. Note that the same hold rescued a false Teams mic-release mid-call (hold 2026-11-12 15:39:16Z, back in_meeting at 22.0 s), so shortening the dwell has a cost.
- Field: demonstrated. coordinator lifecycle_end_held with oracle_available true and live_signal call_is_down, no lifecycle_provisional_end_confirmed, then lifecycle ended 60 s later whose confirmed_by lacks call_teardown_observed, then recorder held_end_trimmed. Found 4 of 4: holds at 2026-11-12T14:32:36.273Z, 14:53:37.066Z, 15:44:27.928Z and 2026-11-13T07:42:25.713Z.
- Verifier: Code at HEAD: MeetingSessionController.swift:1887-1895 matches the snippet exactly. TeardownHoldPolicy.consult forwards to CallTeardownOracle.decide. That returns .confirm only when lastTeardownAt >= max(provisionalStartedAt - 15 s, recordingStartedAt), and .hold otherwise. Since END17 there is no cap, unavailable-log confirm or ceiling, so only the engine dwell (confirm_dwell_sec 60, lone 720) can end an unconfirmed hold. I looked for a fix in the watcher and found none. CallTeardownWatcher tails the SlimCore family at 1 Hz on a DispatchSourceTimer, rereads a rotated generation from offset 0, and carries partial lines. So a line that is on disk would be caught within about 1 s, and the delay comes from the line's absence, not from a parsing gap. At c56f2869 CallTeardownOracle.swift does not exist (END9 landed 2026-08-20, END17 is 01e904a5), so head_only holds for the home Mac. Field pack: I ran my own script over all four events files sorted by ts. Era 3 (ts >= 2026-11-12T09:19) has 9 lifecycle_end_held. 4 are Teams with live_signal call_is_down, oracle_available true and log_written_sec_ago 129.2, 1390.0, 286.0 and 602.8, at 14:32:36.273Z, 14:53:37.066Z, 15:44:27.928Z and 2026-11-13 07:42:25.713Z. Each led on process_audio_is_running_input_false, had no lifecycle_provisional_end_confirmed, and reached lifecycle ended at +60.069, +60.026, +60.035 and +60.006 s with confirmed_by [ax_leave_button_invalid, shareable_content_window_gone], then recorder held_end_trimmed. The other 5 era-3 holds (2 Zoom, 3 Teams) were released back to in_meeting at 5.5 to 38.3 s. Counts of lifecycle_provisional_end_confirmed teardown_observed: 0 in era 3 and 2 in era 2 (held_sec 0). call_teardown_source_missing (Logs folder unopenable): 8651 rows in era 1 and 8210 in era 2, none in era 3, so in era 3 the log was readable and simply had no new teardown line. A log_written_sec_ago of minutes at hold open shows Teams had not written that file since well before the end, which fits SlimCore buffering until a call boundary, as the oracle's own header says (END12). The 'why' therefore lies outside our code and is not proven from events. The mechanism and the owner-visible 60 s Confirming end are demonstrated in the newest build era. I lowered severity from high to medium because the cost is latency plus kept extra audio, not lost audio, and because the dwell also protects real calls.
- Direction: Stop treating the log line as the Teams fast path. Measure first: put the watcher's lastTeardownAt age into lifecycle_end_held and record when UnregisterCall actually reaches disk relative to mic release.
