| ShareableContentSignal (shareable_content_window_gone, and browser_tab_title_left_meet_pattern on the browser adapter) | SCShareableContent.getExcludingDesktopWindows(false, onScreenWindowsOnly: true) filtered by owning bundle id and a title regex | poll | 1 s poll at HEAD (0.5 s while present in the installed code); 2 s probe timeout, 30 s gate [...] | Weak end for native apps (same evidence class as the Leave button) and the single end signal for a plain browser meeting | Measures 'a matching window is on the current screen', not call state: off-Space, minimised, hidden and title-format changes all read as gone; browser title is the active tab only; title tokens are brand-wide for Teams, Zoom and Webex so any other window masks a real end; forwards only changes, including a first reading of false; nil on TCC denial holds the prior state |
| AXLeaveButtonSignal (ax_leave_button_invalid) | kAXUIElementDestroyedNotification on the resolved Leave element plus an AXRole health probe poll and a throttled full-tree re-walk | mixed | Notification sub-second when delivered; poll 1 s backing off to 5 s; recovery re-walk at m [...] | Weak end that always opens a hold (it is not subject to the live-mic veto); its healthy edge is the only thing that reverts such a hold | Element validity tracks UI rendering: Teams tree blackouts of 1 to 65 s, compact view without a Leave container, share re-renders, Zoom controls destroyed mid-call (field row). Re-arm on an already healthy element emits nothing, so the engine never hears 'still healthy'. Not available in browsers |
| WorkspaceSignal (workspace_app_terminated) | NSWorkspace.didTerminateApplicationNotification filtered by bundle id plus NSRunningApplication.isTerminated KVO | edge | Immediate | The only definitive signal: ends at once with no hold | Built only by the browser adapter, so native apps have no quit signal; for a browser it fires only when the whole browser quits, which is not how a Meet call ends; matched by bundle id alone |
| WindowTitleSignal (window_title_left_pattern) | kAXTitleChangedNotification on the PWA window element, title read through kAXTitleAttribute | edge | Immediate when delivered; no poll backstop | Weak end for Chromium PWAs only | Reads the same title as the shareable-content signal but sits in a different evidence class, so one title change satisfies the pair dwell (audit F158) |
| CallTeardownWatcher and CallTeardownOracle (call_teardown_observed) | 1 Hz tail of Teams' SlimCore log family for the UnregisterCall line; newest timestamp kept | log_tail | Line leads the AX edge by about 1.3 s per the repo's measurement, but SlimCore buffers flu [...] | Host-side definitive confirm for an open native hold; never opens one | Correlated by time only (the call id is parsed and discarded) and consulted for every native bundle, so another call's teardown confirms this hold; the source flaps unreadable (END16, not implemented); Teams only; not in the installed build at all |
| IdleStopBackstop inputs (MicGate verdict plus system-channel level) | MicGate verdict stream re-ingested at about 1 Hz from the mic level callback; system level mirror against a -50 dBFS threshold; recorder channel health | level | 15 minute horizon by default, nudge at 8 minutes | Ceiling for a forgotten recording; the only audio-derived input to any end decision | Used only as a stop, never as evidence the call is alive; stands down only for a native meeting in .starting or .inMeeting, so it can stop during a hold and on any browser, manual or mic-only recording |
| Mic-busy span (mic_in_use_stop) | AVCaptureDevice.default(for: .audio).isInUseByAnotherApplication KVO plus a backstop poll, attributed to the frontmost app | level | 5 s release debounce | Only end path for a recording started from the mic-in-use tier | Device resolved once at launch (audit F039), so a mid-call input switch reads as release; attribution is whichever app was frontmost; zero mic_busy rows in this log, so the input may never read true on this Mac |
| Window or tab title present (shareable_content_window_gone, browser_tab_title_left_meet_pattern) | SCShareableContent.getExcludingDesktopWindows(false, onScreenWindowsOnly: true), matched by bundle id and a title predicate | poll | 1 s poll at HEAD (0.5 s installed); 2 s probe timeout; a wedged request blocks new ones fo [...] | Weak end lead for native apps; the ONLY end signal for a plain browser tab. Also the start signal for browsers. | Sees only the active tab of an on-screen window, so tab switch, minimise, another Space and tab close all look the same. Emits on change only. A nil probe (Screen Recording not granted, stale grant after a rebuild, SCK timeout) holds the prior value, so blind reads as still live. Brand tokens are broad: any title containing `zoom` or `microsoft teams` keeps it live after the call. Carries no meeting identity (the Mee [...] |
| AX Leave button health (ax_leave_button_invalid) | kAXUIElementDestroyedNotification plus a kAXRoleAttribute probe poll, with a fresh tree walk before declaring invalid | mixed | Under 100 ms when the notification is delivered; 1 s poll after an arm, 5 s once backed of [...] | Weak end lead for Teams, Zoom, Webex, Slack. Its return to healthy is what reverts a hold. | The Leave control is absent in Teams' compact and screen-share views for as long as the owner leaves the window alone, and the whole AX tree blanks for 1 to 65 s at a time (gotchas.md). Labels are English only (DET15). Only `invalidUIElement` reads invalid, so `cannotComplete` from a dead app reads healthy. Not vetoed by a live mic, so it opens a hold on a live call. |
| Per-process mic capture (process_audio_is_running_input) | kAudioHardwarePropertyProcessObjectList enumerate and match by bundle prefix, then kAudioProcessPropertyIsRunningInput OR-ed across objects; HAL listeners plus [...] | mixed | Listener immediate; poll 1 s backing off to 5 s; DET8 measured Teams clearing within 11.4 [...] | The only positive call-is-up reading. At HEAD its `.live` vetoes every dwell and absorbs window-gone; its `.ended` is a weak lead and half of the 60 s pair. | Wired for Teams and Zoom only; never for Webex, Slack or any browser. Emits on change only and holds the prior value on nil, so a vanished object freezes the reading at live with no freshness bound (F033, G025). Zoom's release at call end is unmeasured: the local log has six readings, all `initial true`, and no false edge. A stale cached object beside idle helpers reads false (G028). |
| App terminated (workspace_app_terminated) | NSWorkspace.didTerminateApplicationNotification plus NSRunningApplication.isTerminated KVO | edge | Immediate | The one definitive signal in the engine: ends a recording at once. | Constructed by the browser adapter only. A Teams or Zoom quit or crash produces no such event, although ADR 0017's tier table lists it for the meeting app. For a browser it only fires when the whole browser quits, which is rare for Safari or Chrome. |
| Teams teardown line (call_teardown_observed) | 1 Hz tail of Teams' SlimCore log in its Group Container, matching `SlimCoreModule::WindowContentProtectionProvider: UnregisterCall <id>` | log_tail | The line's own stamp leads the AX edge by about 1.3 s (END9), but Teams buffers log lines [...] | Definitive end for Teams, host side. Confirms an open hold at once. | Only consulted while a hold is already open, so it can never end a recording by itself. Consulted for every native app's hold, not just Teams (DET15). The folder read flaps (about 21,700 `call_teardown_source_missing` transitions, END16, unfixed) and one failed listing resets the tail to end-of-file, dropping lines appended in the gap. Private, unversioned log format. Absent from the installed build. |
| AX window title (window_title_left_pattern) | kAXTitleChangedNotification on the meeting window element | edge | Immediate | Second weak class for Chromium PWAs only, which lets a PWA reach the 60 s pair. | Reads the same title as the shareable-content signal, so the pair is two reads of one fact (audit G024). Not wired for plain browsers. |
| Idle backstop (auto_stop_silence) | MicGate verdict (RMS gate, HAL VAD, app mute) plus a 1 s system-audio level mirror, re-fed at about 1 Hz from the mic level callback | level | Nudge at 480 s, stop at 900 s of continuous joint silence (configurable 60 to 1800 s) | Documented ceiling for a forgotten recording and for every recording with no lifecycle (manual, mic-tier). | Stands down for a native recording while the lifecycle reads live. Reset by any system audio from any app (capture is display-wide) and by any speech in the room. Held while the system channel is down. Not fed at all until a MicGate verdict differs from the previous recording's last one. Stops without a trim. |
| User Stop | Menu Stop, HUD Stop, toggle hotkey, automation toggle -> `toggleManual`; force-stop hotkey and URL verb -> `forceStop` | edge | Immediate | Definitive. In the local log it is the route that actually ended both browser recordings. | `toggleManual` writes no event, so a manual stop is only visible as the absence of every other route. A Stop during an open hold skips the trim (G031). |
| mic_busy (device in use by another app) | AVCaptureDevice.isInUseByAnotherApplication, KVO plus a 3 s / 12 s poll, on the device returned by AVCaptureDevice.default(for: .audio) at launch | mixed | KVO immediate if it fires, else up to 12 s; span close debounced 5 s; prompt needs a 30 s [...] | Only input of the DET1 catch-all prompt, of DET3's mic-busy miss auditing, and of the stop for a DET1 recording | Device bound once at launch and never rebound; nil at launch never retried; has never read true in any log; cannot name the holder so attribution is the frontmost app; whether the property works at all on shared macOS inputs is unmeasured (DET10 probe owed) |
| process_audio_is_running_input, start side | kAudioHardwarePropertyProcessObjectList + kAudioProcessPropertyBundleID / PID + kAudioProcessPropertyIsRunningInput, one enumeration per discovery scan | poll | One scan interval: 3 s active, 12 s idle, plus an 80 ms coalesce | Corroborating start signal (weight 3) and control-walk admission for native Teams and Zoom. HEAD only | Browsers, PWAs, Webex and Slack are never read; unknown degrades to false; true means the process has an input IOProc, not that a call is live (device tests and pre-join count); never run on the owner's machine |
| process_audio_is_running_input, end side | Same HAL property with a listener per matched process object, a process-list listener for re-resolve, and a 1 s poll backing off to 5 s | mixed | Tens of ms when the listener fires, else 1 s to 5 s | Live reading vetoes weak ends; a false edge leads a held end (requires corroboration). Teams and Zoom only | A vanished object freezes the reading at live (F033); first reading forwarded as an edge (F030); no freshness on the veto (G025); mic switch inside a call reads as a drop (END15); nothing for browsers |
| HAL system input mute | kAudioDevicePropertyMute, input scope, on the default input device; listener, rebinds on kAudioHardwarePropertyDefaultInputDevice | edge | Listener latency, with an initial read at start and at each rebind | Highest precedence in MicGate.decide (mutedByHardware) | Does not see in-app mute in Teams, Zoom, Meet; read false in all 5 logged samples; watches the default input, which is the capture device only by convention |
| HAL voice activity | kAudioDevicePropertyVoiceActivityDetectionState, read only when kAudioDevicePropertyVoiceActivityDetectionEnable is already set by someone else | edge | Listener latency when supported | Would make the verdict hot(vad_active), reset the idle backstop on speech and arm the MIC10 contradiction discredit | The probe never enables it, so it is unsupported unless another process enabled it: unsupported in 3 of 3 logged recordings; binds once per MicGate.start and never rebinds (F037) |
| RMS gate | Per-buffer RMS of the daemon's own AVAudioEngine input tap (100 ms buffers), open at -45 dBFS for 80 ms, close at -55 dBFS for 350 ms | level | About 200 ms to open, 350 ms to close | The only working speech evidence; decides hot versus silent whenever no AX mute is known; feeds the idle backstop and, under the regulated gate, whether mic audio is written | Absolute thresholds with no noise-floor or device adaptation; measures the default input, not the device the call uses; never opened during either built-in-microphone call in the log |
| AX mute button probe | AXObserver value/title-changed notifications on a cached AXUIElement plus a 1 s health poll backing off to 5 s; label matched against MuteLabels.toml by app and [...] | mixed | Notification latency, else 1 s to 5 s; re-arm after 5 unknown reads | Fast path for app mute on native Teams, Zoom, Webex, Slack | Localized label scrape; cached element goes stale on re-render; latches the prior state across unknown reads; records a reading as delivered before the fusion accepts it (G027) |
| AX mute window watcher | 1 Hz re-resolving attribute read of the mute control per meeting window (MuteControlCache at HEAD, full walk on the installed build) | poll | 1 s; blind clear after 3 s; contradiction discredit after 4 s | Backstop for the probe; injects fused app-mute state; owns the two discredit heuristics | Cannot tell the live window from a stale one; blind clear is ungated by capture mode (F003); contradiction needs HAL VAD, which is unsupported here; runs for browsers with nothing to read |
| Teams SlimCore mute log | Tail of the newest MSTeamsNM_SlimCore_ log generation, 1 s poll | log_tail | About 1 s; leads the AX read by a median of 50 ms per MIC8's measurement | Second app-mute oracle for Teams; wins over AX for 5 s after each line | Transition stream that is silent until the first toggle; undocumented vendor string; a refused AX reading is never re-sent (F036); Teams only; not exercised in this log |
| Input device running somewhere | kAudioDevicePropertyDeviceIsRunningSomewhere across input devices, read once before the engine arms | level | One read at record start | Wrong-mic nudge when the default input is idle and another input is running | Usable only before the daemon's own capture starts (END6 confound); a prompt answered before the call opens its mic reads nothing; never fired in this log |
| Mic coverage tally | Peak per-buffer RMS over un-muted frames, accumulated on the render thread, evaluated at stop | level | After the recording stops | Dead-mic warning (mic_recorded_nothing) and the mic_silent sidecar flag | A single buffer above -65 dBFS anywhere in the file defeats it, including audio after the call ended; no quiet tier (MIC17 unbuilt); nothing live during the call |
| Lifecycle verdict stream | AsyncStream<MeetingLifecycleVerdict> (unbounded), produced by PromotionEngine via MeetingLifecycleCoordinator.publish | edge | engine hop plus main hop; a dwell is checked every 0.25 s while provisional; a .starting a [...] | Raises the prompt, opens and reverts the hold, ends the recording, dismisses a stale prompt | Carries a bundle id and pid but no session id; deduped on equality; consumed by one serial loop with an unbounded await in it; the engine's .ended phase is terminal and absorbs everything until the next engage; nothing re-reads levels after a missed edge |
| Discovery winner | MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.onDiscovered(AppSource) from MeetingSourceScanner.scan() | poll | 3 s active, 12 s idle, plus workspace and mic KVO triggers; no scans outside idle since PE [...] | Engages the lifecycle, refreshes the skip latch, writes the attachable candidate, feeds the resume window | Only a confident winner counts; identity is the bundle id; Zoom shows gaps of two to three minutes between confident winners in the log; blind for the whole of a prompt, a recording and a stop |
| AppState | DetectionStateMachine.current (idle, prompting, suppressed (legacy), recording, stopping) | level | synchronous | The gate every handler reads; idle transition disengages the lifecycle and triggers a scan | No starting, no confirming-end, no resume-window state; copied by hand into StatusBarController.titleState, LibraryWindowModel.status and the HUD |
| Prompt timeout | Schedulers.mainRunLoopOnce(prompt_timeout_sec) in DetectionStateMachine.startPromptTimeout | edge | 30 s (owner config) | Applies default_prompt_action; with skip it arms cooldown and latch | Default is skip; hover pauses only the visual (F011); wall clock, so it fires on wake |
| Skip latch, reprompt cooldown, suppressed candidate, resume candidate | SkippedMeetingLatch, RepromptCooldown, DetectionStateMachine.suppressedCandidate, ResumeAfterEnd | level | 60 s grace, 60 s cooldown, 24 or 60 s candidate, 120 s resume window, all Date() | Decide whether a sighting prompts, attaches, resumes or is ignored | All keyed on bundle id and held in process memory; each window was sized from one incident; any two compose (DET14); lost at relaunch |
| Engine dwell | PromotionEngine.tick(at:) with confirmDwell 60 s and loneDwell 720 s | poll | 0.25 s tick, only while provisional | The only route to an end for Zoom, Webex, Slack and every browser short of an app quit | Wall clock; a class counts as ended while a sibling reads live (F031); the veto has no freshness (G025); a plain browser has one class so only the lone dwell can fire |
| Teams teardown oracle | CallTeardownWatcher.snapshot() consulted by TeardownHoldPolicy.consult | log_tail | immediate on hold open, then 1 Hz | The one definitive end for Teams; sets the trim point | Teams only; reported unreadable intermittently (END16); the hold it resolves is host state |
| MicGate verdict | MicGate.verdicts AsyncStream, re-fed at about 1 Hz from recorder.onMicLevel | mixed | change-only stream; 1 Hz re-ingest | Feeds the idle backstop and the regulated-gate writer | Deduped against a lastVerdict that survives stop (F004); never engaged for a source-less recording |
| Idle backstop | IdleStopBackstop.ingest -> onNotify at 480 s, onAutoStop at 900 s | level | 8 and 15 minutes of mic-silent plus far-side-silent | Named everywhere as the ceiling for a missed end | Stands down for a native source whose lifecycle verdict is .starting or .inMeeting, which is the missed-end case; wall clock; needs both channels silent |
| Mic-busy bundle (DET1/DET3) | AVCaptureDevice.isInUseByAnotherApplication KVO plus poll -> onMicBusyBundle | poll | KVO or the 3 to 12 s poll | Prompt for unlisted apps and the stop for a DET1 recording | Zero mic_busy rows in this Mac's log across three calls although the binary contains the action; device bound once at launch (F039, DET10) |
| User start and stop inputs | toggle hotkey, force-stop hotkey, menu rows, HUD Stop, notification actions, meetingpipe:// URL | edge | immediate | Manual start, attach, stop | Everything except the force-stop hotkey funnels through toggleManual with no cause and no log row; ignored silently in .stopping; a start from idle leaves AppState idle during bring-up |
| Recorder and process callbacks | recorder.onWriteFailure, onLowSpace, NSWorkspace.didWakeNotification (recorder only), SIGTERM dispatch source, applicationWillTerminate | edge | immediate; 9 s termination ceiling | Forced stops and the termination flush | No session-level reaction to sleep, wake, lock or a display change; quit drops latch, cooldown, resume, hold and trim |

## The test corpus and what a reliability harness needs

**Coverage of the trace corpus.** Counted on disk: the corpus directory holds 43 files, of which 41 are traces (INDEX.json and README.md are the other two). All 41 are indexed and replayed. By engine: 33 promotion, 5 lifecycle, 3 micgate. By origin: 20 hand-written synthetic (6 seeds, 7 END17 incident reconstructions, 4 lifecycle, 3 micgate), 20 distilled from the Teams work-laptop log (verdict sequence only, signal timeline reconstructed by the extractor), 1 raw (Safari, 3 events). By app: Teams native 31 plus 1 micgate; Zoom 2; Chrome Meet tab 2; Webex 1; Meet PWA 1; Safari tab 1; Slack 0; Teams, Zoom or Webex in a browser tab 0; FaceTime, Discord, WhatsApp, Telegram 0; source-less manual recording 0. Cells are: start | true end | false-end guard | long hold | missed end | mute. Teams native: start none | true end 20 distilled (16 through the confirm pseudo-event, 4 re-timed to the 720 s lone dwell) + 2 seeds + 1 synthetic lifecycle | false-end guard 8 synthetic (END9, END12, END13, END14, END15 x2 as promotion; END9 and END12 as lifecycle), 0 raw | long hold 2 synthetic that never end (END13 past 7,200 s; END12 180 s then revert), none that ends genuinely after a long hold (G001) | missed end none | mute 1 synthetic two-state trace with German labels. Zoom native: start none | true end 1 seed + 1 synthetic lifecycle with a trim point | false-end guard none (a real one sits unextracted in this Mac's log, 2026-10-13T08:03:00Z to 08:03:25Z, and the T8 extractor turns it into a 5-event raw trace with one read-only command) | long hold none | missed end none | mute none. Webex native: start none | true end 1 seed that ends on the 12 minute lone dwell | all other cells none. Slack huddle: nothing in any column. Google Meet, Chrome tab: start none | true end 1 seed, ended 721 s after the leave | false-end guard 1 synthetic (tab away ten minutes) | long hold that same trace | missed end none | mute none. Google Meet, Safari tab: start none | true end none | false-end guard 1 raw trace labelled no_end over 58 s, which in the log is an end the owner made by hand 20 s after the tab left | long hold none | missed end none (that raw incident is one, filed under the opposite label) | mute none. Google Meet PWA: true end 1 seed (title plus app quit) | all other cells none. Teams, Zoom or Webex in a browser tab; FaceTime, Discord, WhatsApp, Telegram (the mic-in-use tier); manual source-less recordings: nothing in any column. App-agnostic mute: 2 single-state traces (hardware mute wins, RMS default). Column totals: start 0 of 41. True end 28, and every one of those expectations is the engine's own output, not an observed end time. False-end guard 10 (9 synthetic, 1 raw carrying the extractor's default label). Genuine end after a long hold 0. Missed end 0, and neither trace format can express one (no truth field, no idle backstop in the replay). Mute 3 traces holding 4 single states, no sequences. Start detection has a separate fixture, daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeTests/Fixtures/window_titles.json: 21 rows, all native, all seeded guesses, 0 captured from a running app, 0 browser rows, and its floor test is an allow-listed skip.

**What replay proves.** A promotion trace proves one thing: given this exact sequence of already-derived signal booleans (live or ended, per signal kind, one fixed context), the pure PromotionEngine at its default dwells (60 s pair, 720 s lone) emits the pinned verdict sequence. That is a real regression fence for the engine's transition table: the veto while process audio reads live, the revert rules, the evidence classes, first-stamp semantics, and the two dwell constants. A lifecycle trace proves a little more: PromotionEngine, TeardownHoldPolicy and CallTeardownOracle.decide composed on a virtual clock produce the pinned outcome (no end, or an end within a second of ended_at with the cut within a second of trim_at), and DetectionCorpusTests has one mutation check showing the replay can fail when the lone dwell is shortened to 30 s. The redaction gate and the reproducibility check also work: I ran `scripts/extract_detection_trace.py --raw ... --check-against` read-only against this Mac's log and the committed Safari trace reproduces. So replay is sound evidence for "a change to the engine or the hold policy changed an outcome that was pinned", and for nothing beyond that.

**What it cannot prove.** 1. That the expected outcome is right. No trace carries ground truth. Distilled traces assert what the engine of their day logged (the README says a false end replays as the false end it was), then END17 re-timed them to today's engine; the raw trace carries the extractor's default label. The corpus detects that the engine changed, never that it is wrong.

2. Anything before the engine. Signal rows are written by the signal objects after their own derivation (title match, AX validity, HAL read) and only on a state change. A signal that never fired, never resolved, or was never wired leaves no row, so it cannot be replayed, and a dead probe is indistinguishable from a healthy quiet one. Every field incident that started in a recognizer (Teams' 2026-10-08 title change, the blank AX tree, process audio asking the wrong PID for 19.8 days, ScreenCaptureKit returning zero Zoom windows on 2026-10-13) is outside what a trace can hold. The distilled Teams traces contain no process-audio events at all, because the probe was dead when they were logged, so they pin outcomes for a world without the veto.

3. Start detection. Every trace begins at the first live signal, after discovery has already chosen a winner and the adapter is engaged. Scanner, scorer, admission gate, prompt, cooldown, skip latch and resume are not replayed by anything.

4. The host. LifecycleTraceReplay is a hand-written copy of MeetingSessionController's hold wiring, not the controller. It stops at the first end, treats the AX rescue as inert, and has no idle backstop, no user Stop, no mic-release stop, no resume. A wiring regression of the END9 or END11 kind (a watcher never started, a nil adapter) replays green.

5. The teardown route. The watcher's reading (lastTeardownAt) is never logged; the extractor rebuilds it from the confirm row, so a raw trace re-asserts the logged decision.

6. Real time. Timers on the main run loop, the three-queue start-up race (F030), poll cadence, and ordering between adapter callbacks are replaced by a sorted list and a virtual clock.

7. What the owner experiences after the verdict: whether the recorder stopped, where the file was cut, whether the transcript lost live audio, whether a second prompt followed. Outcome fields are ended_at and trim_at only.

8. Mute over time. MicGate traces are single calls to MicGate.decide; fusion, dedupe, blind clear and latch timing are not in any trace.

9. Rates. With 1 raw trace and 20 distilled traces from one app, the corpus says nothing about how often anything happens.

**Tests that pin a defect instead of the intent.**

- daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeCoreTests/TeardownHoldPolicyTests.swift:86 test_a_hold_of_one_second_or_more_trims_at_its_stamp, with daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeTests/CallTeardownHoldTests.swift:313 and HeldEndSessionTests.swift:88: pin the cut at the hold's first stamp however long the hold ran. Intent is to cut where the call ended. Audit G001, F032.
- daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeCoreTests/PromotionEngineTests.swift:275 test_a_browser_meeting_left_open_ends_on_the_lone_dwell_END17, and the seed Fixtures/detection-corpus/google_meet_browser_clean_leave.json: pin a browser leave ending 720 s later and call it a clean leave. Intent is a prompt end when the owner leaves. The two Safari recordings in the field log were hand-stopped 33 s and 20 s into that wait.
- Fixtures/detection-corpus/safari_browser_raw_tab_left_held.json: the only raw trace asserts no_end for 58 s over an incident the README itself describes as stopped by hand 20 s in. The label is the extractor's default (scripts/extract_detection_trace.py:552), not an observation.
- Fixtures/detection-corpus/teams_native_rewalk_end_01 to _13 and teams_native_flicker_rewalk_end_01 to _03 (16 traces): assert ended through the confirm_provisional_end pseudo-event with evidence ax_leave_rewalk, the pre-END9 empty re-walk confirm that HEAD no longer performs. They pass for any dwell, veto or pairing rule. teams_native_rewalk_end_06 (window-gone lead, re-walk confirm 170 ms later) has the same shape as the confirmed false end in this Mac's log on 2026-10-13.
- Fixtures/detection-corpus/teams_native_debounce_end_01 to _04: real sessions that ended 2 s after a lone window-gone are re-timed to end 720.5 s after it and still labelled real; they also omit process audio, which at HEAD would veto that signal outright for Teams.
- Fixtures/detection-corpus/webex_ultrasound_retention_en.json: pins a 720 s late end for every Webex leave; its description still narrates the retired 2 s debounce.
- daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeCoreTests/PromotionEngineTests.swift:57 test_ended_signal_during_starting_promotes_straight_to_ended, with :335 test_ended_phase_swallows_further_signals: pin that one weak signal ends the lifecycle while the prompt is up and the engine then ignores everything. For a browser (no process audio, so no veto) a tab switch before the owner answers dismisses the prompt, and handleMeetingEndedDuringPrompt writes no event of its own. Related F030.
- daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeCoreTests/PromotionEngineTests.swift:577 test_real_end_after_a_veto_takes_the_process_audio_path_END14: pins a window-gone remembered from 599 s earlier as the independent second class. Audit G024, F031.
- daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeCoreTests/PromotionEngineTests.swift:482 test_a_lone_process_audio_drop_holds_until_the_lone_dwell: requires a lone process-audio drop to end the meeting after 720 s, opposite to the safety rail the signal's own comments state. Audit G028.
- daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeCoreTests/PromotionEngineTests.swift:434 test_window_gone_provisional_reverts_when_ax_leave_returns_healthy: drives the END8 rescue with a Leave-button event production does not emit. Audit G006, F002.
- daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeCoreTests/PromotionEngineTests.swift:672 test_workspace_termination_is_not_vetoed_by_live_process_audio_END14: asserts native app quit on a signal no native adapter builds. Audit G025, F033.
- daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeCoreTests/PromotionEngineTests.swift:561 test_a_live_mic_blocks_both_dwells_END13: pins a hold open past 7,201 s and stops there; no test or trace ends that hold genuinely, which is the case G001 loses audio in.
- daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeTests/SilenceAutoStopPolicyTests.swift:8 test_native_live_meeting_stands_the_auto_stop_down: pins that the 15 minute idle backstop never fires while a native lifecycle reads live, so a native missed end has no ceiling. The row auto_stop_silence_skipped is its signature; the analyzer does not read it.
- daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeCoreTests/LifecycleAdapterTests.swift:163 test_google_meet_pattern_requires_meeting_code_not_bare_meet and :183 test_strong_title_matchers_are_meet_only_END5: pin that a browser Meet is admitted only when the active tab's title carries the Meet brand and a 3-4-3 room code. No captured browser title exists in any fixture to show that real Meet tabs always look like that.
- daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeTests/WindowRecognizerFixtureTests.swift:82 test_recognizer_matches_fixture: runs 21 seeded guesses with kind hard-coded to native; the test that would say the fixture is unbacked (test_fixture_is_backed_by_real_captures) is an allow-listed skip in scripts/ci-local.sh:154.
- daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeTests/MicInUseTierTests.swift:66 test_no_prompt_for_a_native_whitelist_app: pins that the mic-in-use catch-all never covers a native app discovery missed, so a missed native start has no fallback.
- daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeTests/HeldEndSessionTests.swift:120 test_a_call_back_inside_the_window_resumes_without_a_prompt_and_links_the_stems: pins recording the next same-app call with no prompt. Audit F046.
- daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeCoreTests/MuteSourceFusionTests.swift:109 and daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeTests/MeetingAXWindowWatcherTests.swift:407: the fusion test re-injects by hand a reading production dedupes away, and the blind clear of a latched mute is pinned with no regulated-gate variant. Audit G027, G007.
- pipeline/tests/test_analyze_detection.py:78 test_pair_sessions_classifies_detector_triggered_stop and :180: pin that any lifecycle.ended inside a session is a detector success. :386 test_mic_span_with_a_prompt_is_handled pins that any prompt, answered or not and for any app, marks a mic span handled.

**Shadow mode and metrics today.** discovery_shadow_pick is not a shadow of anything. It was born on 2026-05-21 (commit d0d8d39e, TECH-C13 step 5 part 4) when MeetingDiscoveryWatcher ran beside the old detector; the watcher has been the acting detector since, and the name stayed. Today it is emitted at daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.swift:261 only when a scan has a winner and the winner changed, with the winner's five signal booleans. No second detector is evaluated anywhere in the daemon, live or offline. In this Mac's log there are 11 such rows: 8 were followed by a prompt, 3 were suppressed. The analyzer uses the row only as the reason string "discovered but not prompted". mp analyze-detection (pipeline/src/mp/analyze_detection.py) is the only reliability report and it is run by hand; nothing schedules it and CI runs only its unit tests. It has two dimensions. End: it pairs recording_started with recording_stopped and classifies the stop as detector, silence backstop, force stop or manual, where manual is inferred from the absence of the other three. Start: it pairs mic_busy_started with mic_busy_ended and calls a span a miss when no prompt or recording overlaps it. I ran its pure functions read-only over this Mac's events.jsonl. It works mechanically (3 sessions paired, 1 torn line tolerated) and it reports: Zoom 1 session, 1 detector stop; Safari 2 sessions, 2 manual stops; mic-busy "not audited" because the log holds zero spans. The one detector success is the 17 s Zoom recording that the installed build ended while the call was live, so the only number it produces for Zoom is wrong in the direction that matters. It prints percentages on n = 3. It has no notion of a false end, of fragmentation, of how late an end was, or of overrun; its only timing figure is the gap between lifecycle.ended and recording_stopped, which is flush latency. It does not read lifecycle_end_held, lifecycle_end_hold_released, end_vetoed, held_end_trimmed, auto_stop_silence_skipped, mic_in_use_stop or recording_ended_during_start, so nothing END9 to END17 added is measured, and at HEAD the two automatic stops in that list would be counted as manual misses. The DET10 guard (a zero-span window renders as not audited instead of zero misses) does hold. scripts/extract_detection_trace.py --inventory is the other counter. On this log it reports 1 lifecycle session for 3 recordings, because a session exists only when it closes on lifecycle.ended, and it would emit that one session (the false end) as an expected end. scripts/valid1_check.py measures the UX4 degraded banner, DIAR1 latency, speaker attribution, a DER lower bound, the cleanup pass and summarize cold start. It contains no check on detection start, end or mute, so the on-device acceptance script has no bar for the subsystem the owner complains about. Two properties of the log itself limit every one of these. No row carries a build or launch identity (Logger.swift:86 writes ts, category and action only), so the log cannot say that the installed binary is the August code. And 1,557 of the 1,832 rows here are doctor.probe and HAL bus subscription noise; the log holds nothing before 2026-10-12 and has no rotated generation.

**Proposal.** Goal: turn "it feels unreliable" into counted outcomes per app per week, against a truth source that does not come from the detector being judged, and make it possible to trial a new detector on live signals without letting it act. Step 0, make the log attributable (small, prerequisite for everything else). Add a daemon_started row with the build commit and a launch id, and stamp the launch id on every row. Add stop_cause to recording_stopped (lifecycle_end with its route, user_stop, user_stop_during_hold, idle_backstop, mic_release, write_failure, shutdown) and a prompt outcome row for the one case that has none today (dismissed because the lifecycle ended during the prompt). Log each teardown line the watcher observes with its own timestamp. Log a scan summary on any change of the candidate signal vector, not only on a winner change, as matcher-hit booleans and counts, never titles. Step 1, ground truth, in three tiers, cheapest first. Tier A, passive and free: an always-on call-span oracle independent of AX and window titles. Enumerate CoreAudio process objects for every process, not only the engaged app, and log mic_holder_started and mic_holder_ended per owning bundle, whether the daemon is idle, prompting, recording or just stopped. DET8 measured this read as correct for Teams helper processes; ProcessAudioScanProbe already does the enumeration. For Teams also log the RegisterCall and UnregisterCall lines the watcher already tails. Probe first, one owner-run call each in Safari and Chrome, to learn which helper bundle owns the input there; that attribution is unmeasured. Tier B, about ten seconds per meeting: a one-tap label on the "recording stopped" notification and on the Library row (right, cut early, ran long, started late, not a meeting), plus "missed entirely" offered by the weekly report for any oracle span with no recording. Stored as truth_label rows keyed by stem id, no titles. Ask only where detector and oracle disagree by more than 30 s, so a normal week costs a handful of taps. Tier C, for calibration only: a hotkey that writes truth_mark (call is live, I just left) for ten meetings, to measure each oracle's offset from the real moment. Precedence per call: owner label or mark, then the app's own log, then the mic-holder span; calls with only the third are reported separately as unconfirmed. Calendar is not a tier: CAL1 measured that the owner's calendar never reaches EventKit. Step 2, metrics, each against a truth span T0 to T1 per call, reported as counts with denominators and no percentages below 20 calls per cell. Missed start: a span of 60 s or more with no prompt and no recording. Late start: recording_started minus T0, split into detector latency (prompt_shown minus T0) and answer latency, plus head seconds lost. Prompt outcomes: accepted, skipped, timed out, dismissed by an end, and prompts per call. False end: a detector stop while the span continues more than 30 s, with seconds lost until the next recording or T1, and fragments per call. Missed end: recorder still running at T1 plus 15 minutes, or a stop whose cause is user or backstop after T1. Late end: stop minus T1 for detector stops. Overrun minutes: audio recorded after T1, before and after trim. Trim error: trim point minus T1, where negative means live audio was cut (the G001 case). Hold accounting: opened, reverted, confirmed by route (teardown, pair, lone, quit), stopped by the user, with held seconds. Mute: for Teams, seconds where the gate disagrees with Teams' own mute log; elsewhere, the share of recording time with app-mute state unknown, counts of blind clears, and empty-mic recordings. Instrument health, shown first: per recording and per signal, whether it ever produced a reading, whether it resolved, how many edges; whether the mic-holder oracle saw anything that week; AX trust; whether ScreenCaptureKit returned any window. Step 3, shadow mode. End side: in MeetingLifecycleCoordinator.ingest, on engineQueue, feed the same PrimarySignalEvent to a list of shadow engines beside the acting one, each with its own TeardownHoldPolicy and tick, and log shadow_verdict rows (engine id, verdict, leading, confirmed_by, would_trim_at). Shadows never publish to the verdict stream. PromotionEngine is pure and already takes its dwells as init parameters, so constant variants cost nothing; a different detector implements the same three calls (ingest, tick, confirm). The part that makes it useful: keep the adapters and the shadows observing for a watch window after the acting engine ends (five minutes, or until the mic-holder oracle has read released for 60 s). Today stopRecording disengages at once, so the acting detector's false end blinds every other observer at the exact moment they would disagree; the watch window also self-labels suspected false ends (the same call read live after the end), which is the evidence that exists only by accident in the 2026-10-13 Zoom log. Start side: run candidate scorers and recognizers over the same scan result and log shadow_pick when they differ from the acting pick, with the mic-holder oracle as referee. Flight recorder: a 1 Hz ring buffer of pre-derivation readings as a tri-state (live, ended, unknown) per signal plus matcher-hit vectors, AX node presence, per-object HAL readings and teardown lines, persisted only around incidents (any end, any hold, any user stop, any truth disagreement). That closes the "signal never fired" hole, because unknown becomes a recorded reading. Step 4, corpus v2 and a bench. A truth-labelled raw trace format: the raw readings plus truth {call_started_at, call_ended_at, source}. Score an engine over the whole set (false ends, missed ends, seconds lost, seconds of overrun, trim error) instead of matching a verdict sequence the engine itself produced. A candidate replaces the acting detector only when it is no worse on every labelled incident and better on some, a bar written down before the run. Keep the 16 pseudo-confirm traces as legacy characterization, outside the score. Extract the 2026-10-13 Zoom window from this Mac now; it is the first raw false end available. Step 5, weekly report. `mp detection-report --since 7d`, stdlib only, run by a weekly launchd job, written under ~/Library/Logs/MeetingPipe/reports and announced by one notification. Sections in order: build and launches covered; instrument health; one table per app with the counts above; the incident list, each with its UTC window and the ready-to-run `scripts/extract_detection_trace.py --raw --from ... --to ...` command; labels waiting for the owner; shadow disagreements with which side truth supported; four-week trend. Define "reliable" per app as four consecutive weeks with zero false ends, zero missed starts and zero missed ends over at least 20 truth calls, so that "100%" is always a statement about a counted denominator. Order and size: step 0 small, steps 1 and 2 medium, step 3 medium, step 4 medium, step 5 small once 2 exists. Nothing in steps 0 to 2 changes detection behaviour, so it can ship before any detector redesign and then judge that redesign.

## The stale install on the home Mac

Separate from the work laptop. Source estimate: e371bca3 (committed 2026-08-18T00:03:46+03:00). This corrects the premise: it is 15 commits BEFORE c56f2869, not at it. The last Swift change it contains is a15846e3 (REC12, 2026-08-17 19:08 +03:00); daemon/Sources is byte-identical from a15846e3 through 20e66c74, so the Swift code is pinned to that span and the reflog pins the exact commit. HEAD 4949c9c0 is 169 commits ahead, 80 of them touching daemon/Sources (the 2026-11-12 audit's F021 says 94 and 48 because it counted from the September file date). The binary's mtime is 2026-09-11T09:35:51Z (12:35:51 local at +03:00; the 11:35 in the premise is a daylight-saving display artifact of ls under today's +02:00 offset). Method: Three independent methods, plus a control. (1) Literal bisect. For every first-parent commit touching daemon/Sources between 4f52c285 (2026-08-15) and 2822f349 (2026-08-22) I diffed the set of plain string literals (24+ UTF-8 bytes, no escapes or interpolation) in daemon/Sources against the parent and searched the raw bytes of ~/Applications/MeetingPipe.app/Contents/MacOS/MeetingPipe. Up to a15846e3 the added literals are present (a15846e3 4 of 4, FEAT16 33381c07 3 of 3, UX65 fa61ffcc 7 of 7, UX61 7 of 7). From the next Swift-touching commits on they are absent: AUTO3 471c8cfe 1 of 8, DOC12 df878d6c 0 of 7 added while all 6 literals it removed are still in the binary, ARCH6 bd32468c both removed literals still present, FEAT14 c56f2869 0 of 2, FEAT15 b122e001 0 of 12, DET13 621dc79b 0 of 1, REC15 0 of 5, END9 0 of 6, END10 0 of 4, END11 0 of 3. (2) Resource blob match. The installed Contents/Resources/GUIDE.md hashes to git blob c343f487, which is docs/GUIDE.md only from 22c822c4 (2026-08-17) until 3f7448f7 (DOC13, committed 2026-08-18 09:30 +03:00) replaced it; rebuild.sh copies that file from the checkout it runs in. e371bca3:docs/GUIDE.md is exactly that blob. (3) Raw reflog. The first line of .git/logs/refs/heads/main is e371bca3 -> 8d4a9449 'merge origin/main: Fast-forward' at 2026-10-21 22:20:37 +03:00, so local main on this Mac was e371bca3 when the binary was built on 2026-09-11. Control: the same bisect run on daemon/.build/arm64-apple-macosx/release/MeetingPipe (built 2026-10-21 22:34:31 local, 14 minutes after that pull) finds every literal through 8d4a9449 (END14) present, so the method dates a known build correctly. Marker strings agree: the installed binary has 0 occurrences of call_teardown and browser_tab_rejected, the October build has 1 of each. Confidence: High for the commit: three unrelated methods agree and the control build dates correctly. Not excluded: uncommitted edits in the working tree when rebuild.sh ran, which no method here can see.

**Why HEAD is not installed there.** What the scripts do. install.sh builds release, assembles a fresh bundle, deletes ~/Applications/MeetingPipe.app, copies the new one, re-signs, syncs the pipeline venv, rewrites the LaunchAgent plist and restarts the job. rebuild.sh builds release, syncs the venv first (dies with 'The app was not touched' if that fails), copies only the binary, the resource bundles and GUIDE.md into the existing app, re-signs, then bootout plus bootstrap and checks a process came up. Neither is called by ci-local.sh, /tech-task or any other agent command; installing is an owner step. What the timestamps say. install.sh last ran 2026-06-16: the app's Info.plist, the LaunchAgent plist and the venv all carry that date, Info.plist still says LSMinimumSystemVersion 13.0 with no meetingpipe URL scheme, the resource bundles still sit in Contents/MacOS (pre-DIST4 layout), and the venv still holds an editable .pth into the clone. rebuild.sh last ran 2026-09-11: daemon/.build/arm64-apple-macosx/release was created 09:34:24Z, the binary landed in the app at 09:35:51Z, and daemon.log shows a fresh detection 2 seconds later. Nothing has installed since: there is no daemon/.build/release/MeetingPipe.app staging bundle, release.yaml is dated 2026-10-21, and the venv was never replaced (sync_pipeline_venv would have deleted the editable one). The only later release build is 2026-10-21 22:33 to 22:34 local, a build of 8d4a9449 that the go-live assessment ran as a gate ('release build in 88 s') and nobody copied into the app. Debug builds are current to 2026-11-12 23:10 and moved to out/Products/Debug under SwiftPM's swiftbuild backend. Most likely reason (an inference, stated as one): this is a two-machine project. Local main on this Mac sat at e371bca3 from 2026-08-18 until the first pull on 2026-10-21; every commit in between was made elsewhere and pushed (the first commit made on this Mac afterwards is 'drop allow rules pinned to another machine's home path', 2026-10-24). The Teams field incidents behind END9 to END17 cite a build installed 2026-08-21 and another at 2026-09-02 17:16 and Teams calls on dates that do not exist in this Mac's daemon.log, and Teams is not installed here. So fixes were installed and exercised on the other Mac, while this Mac got one rebuild from a stale checkout on 2026-09-11 and, after development moved here on 2026-10-24, only agent sessions that build and test in worktrees and never install. Nothing exposes a build identity (DIST6, status new): every build is 0.1.0 build 1 and rebuild.sh never rewrites the plist, so the September file date looked recent. Two audits flagged it (go-live PD-4 on 2026-10-21, F021 on 2026-11-12) and the app is still not reinstalled. How the owner can tell today: (a) ls -l the binary and compare with git log -1; (b) strings -a on the binary piped to grep -c call_teardown prints 0 on anything older than END9; (c) resource bundles in Contents/MacOS means pre-DIST4; (d) the event log has never contained an app_rebuild row or any call_teardown row since it began; (e) launchctl print gui/501/com.meetingpipe.daemon plus ps lstart confirm the process started after the file was replaced (today: pid 2345, started 2026-11-13T07:11:55Z, runs = 1).

**Risks of installing HEAD.**

- G001 (critical, arrives with END17 and T8): the held-tail trim cuts from the first weak end signal. Under a regulated or NDA posture that audio is destroyed; under the default posture the transcript and summary drop the span until Restore.
- Browser meetings end later at HEAD than today, by design. Verified in HEAD code: for a plain Safari or Chrome tab the adapter feeds only the tab-title signal and app termination (the window-title signal is wired for PWAs only), no vetoer exists, so the only routes to an end are the 720 s lone dwell, quitting the browser, or a manual Stop. The installed build's floor is 120 s. On this Mac's Safari workflow every Meet recording would run about 12 minutes past the real end and then rely on the trim that G001 shows is unsafe.
- Claimed fixed, not holding, all in the new end logic: F031 and G024 (pair dwell accepts an ended stamp of any age and counts a class as ended while its sibling reads live), G025 (the live-mic veto has no freshness), F002 and G006 (END8 rescue is a no-op when Leave is already armed, so a window-gone hold runs to the lone dwell), F045 (ends and stops during bring-up are dropped), F046 (resume-after-end records the next same-app call with no prompt).
- HEAD's end logic was tuned on Teams on another Mac. On this Mac the observed apps are Zoom and Safari, for which no definitive end oracle exists (DET15: Zoom, Webex and Slack holds read Teams' teardown log). Every END task is still partial, meaning shipped and not field-confirmed.
- END16 (new, P2): CallTeardownWatcher stats Teams' log folder every second around the clock. On a Mac without Teams that is permanent idle work and call_teardown_source_missing noise.
- First DIST4 venv sync: sync_pipeline_venv deletes the existing editable venv before uv sync --frozen. If the sync fails, rebuild.sh stops with 'The app was not touched', but the venv is already gone and the old daemon is left with no pipeline.
- rebuild.sh never rewrites Info.plist, so a rebuild-only upgrade keeps the June plist (minimum 13.0, no meetingpipe URL scheme). The first HEAD install has to be install.sh, which deletes the old bundle with no rollback (DIST6).
- F105 and DI-8: rebuild.sh overwrites the running executable in place and relaunches with no check for a recording in progress; install.sh's restart path swallows failures and prints success without checking a process came up.
- Verify after install, not a finding: no release build has run under the swiftbuild backend on this Mac. daemon/.build/release still points at the 2026-10-21 products, and install.sh only checks that a binary exists there. If the new backend does not refresh that path, the scripts would package the October build of 8d4a9449 without complaint. Check the binary's mtime and grep -c lone_dwell after installing.
- TCC: the scripts assert that the self-signed identity keeps Screen Recording across builds; the audit notes nothing tests those comments. If signing falls back to ad-hoc, system audio is missing until re-granted.
- Three months and 169 commits land at once on a Mac that has never run any of them, alongside a pipeline that has already been running at HEAD through the editable venv.

## Owner-run probes proposed by the tracers

| Area | Probe | Pass or fail threshold |
|---|---|---|
| browser-start | Scheduled-meeting title shape. Join a Meet created from a calendar event in Safari and once in Chrome, keep its tab selected for 60 s, and note the shape of the window title on the pre-join page, in the call, and after leaving (scripts/dump_window_titles.swift prints AX titles read-only from your own Terminal). Do not paste the title, only whether it carries a three-four-three letter code or the meet.google.com host. | Confirmed if in 2 of 2 scheduled calls the title has neither the code nor the host and no detector.discovery_shadow_pick row appears in that minute. Refuted if the title carries the code and still nothing is picked; then look at the selected-tab, Space and latch entries. |
| browser-start | Ad hoc control. Start an instant Meet (code in the title) with its tab selected and the app idle. | Pass: discovery_shadow_pick with winner_kind browser, lifecycle starting and prompt_shown within 15 s. A fail means the base path (grants, poll, build) is broken, independent of the title. |
| browser-start | Mic fallback. During any call of at least 60 s with the prompt skipped, then run mp logs --action mic_busy_started. Also run the DET10 probe once from your own Terminal. | 0 rows while the menu bar mic indicator is on is DET10 outcome D: the signal choice is wrong and the tier must move to process objects. Any row reopens the stale-device explanation. |
| browser-start | Who holds the mic in a browser call. During a Safari Meet and a Chrome Meet, list HAL process objects with IsRunningInput true (the DET8 enumeration). | Go for capture-led browser detection if in 2 of 2 calls per browser one identifiable browser-family process reads true within 2 s of joining and false within 15 s of leaving. |
| browser-start | Prompt dismissal. With an ad hoc Meet prompt showing, select another tab, then repeat with a Space change. | Confirmed if the panel disappears within 2 s and the log shows lifecycle ended with leading_signal browser_tab_title_left_meet_pattern followed by prompting to idle with no skip or timeout row. |
| browser-start | Timeout tally. For two weeks compare prompt_timeout with recording_started for browser bundles. | If more than 1 in 5 browser prompts time out, or any timeout is followed by a call with no later recording, change the timeout contract before tuning anything else. |
| browser-start | Which Mac and which build. State which Mac carries the daily meetings and check its installed build date and event log. | If the daily machine is not this one, repeat the log checks there before ranking causes; this report describes this Mac only. |
| native-start | Zoom, muted, toolbar hidden. On a HEAD build join a Zoom call, mute in Zoom, leave the pointer off the window for 3 minutes, then read the detector rows for us.zoom.xos. | process_audio_is_running_input true on every scan row of those 3 minutes: DET12 covers Zoom. Any stretch of false longer than 60 s: the skip latch will lapse on HEAD too and Zoom needs another liveness signal. |
| native-start | Zoom setting 'Always show meeting controls', installed build, one call, press Skip on the prompt. | A pick within 12 s of joining, no title-only candidate_dropped row during the call and no second prompt_shown: the hidden-controls mechanism is confirmed and this is a no-code mitigation until HEAD is installed. Title-only drops still appearing: the controls vanish for another reason (window not visible). |
| native-start | After installing HEAD: record a Zoom call, switch to another Space for 2 minutes, come back, leave normally. | Pass: lifecycle end_vetoed rows, zero ending_provisional before the leave, and recording_stopped no more than about 70 s after the leave. Fail: any recording_stopped before the leave. |
| native-start | DET10's existing probe during a real call (daemon/scripts/det10-mic-busy-probe.swift, named in the DET10 spec; I did not read or run it). | Flag true at any point: the daemon's wiring is at fault (outcome B). Flag never true while the default input is running: replace the signal (outcome D). |
| native-start | Prompt count per call on HEAD, five native calls in a row, Skip on at least two of them. | Exactly one prompt_shown per call and none after a Skip. Two or more for one call is a fail. |
| native-start | Start latency, five calls: note the join click with a stopwatch or screen recording and compare with recording_started. | With default_prompt_action = record or Always: 5 s or less. Otherwise: prompt response time plus 1 s. Anything beyond 12 s before the prompt is a detection miss worth a log excerpt. |
| native-start | Post-end resume on HEAD: after five genuine ends, count recording_resumed_after_end rows. | Zero rows with since_end_sec under 30. One or more means leftover controls or a lingering mic are restarting recordings. |
| native-start | Back-to-back on HEAD: leave a Zoom call and join another within 60 s. | Two recordings, the second with its own prompt or its own resumed_from link. One continuous recording with lifecycle_end_hold_released between the calls is the merge failure. |
| false-end | Which code is running: in events.jsonl count rows whose action is lifecycle_end_held, end_vetoed or stopped_candidate_retained after the next install. | Zero after a full day with at least one recorded call means the install still predates END9; any row means the hold-era code is live. |
| false-end | Installed-era chop signature: count lifecycle/ended rows whose confirmed_by is exactly [ax_leave_rewalk] and that follow ending_provisional by under 1 s. | Each one is a recording ended by the empty-walk path; one exists today (2026-10-13). After installing HEAD the count must stay at that one. |
| false-end | Zoom, HEAD build: join a call, press Record, move to another Space for 30 s, come back. Then leave the mouse off the Zoom window for 2 minutes. | Expect lifecycle/end_vetoed and no stop for the Space switch. If ax_leave_button_state invalid appears while process_audio stays true and the HUD shows Confirming end, the unvetoable-hold state is confirmed for Zoom. |
| false-end | Zoom mute: in a recorded call mute for 2 minutes and watch for signal/process_audio_is_running_input value false. | Any false row during mute means Zoom releases input on mute and every mute over 60 s is an end risk; none in three tries means it behaves like Teams. |
| false-end | Mic switch gap (END15's outstanding measurement): in a recorded Teams or Zoom call switch the input device five times. | Record the gap between each value false and the next value true. Any gap of 60 s or more falsifies the pair dwell; a recording that stops on a switch after Confirming end has shown for more than 12 minutes confirms the aged-hold end. |
| false-end | Meet in Safari: during a recorded call switch to another tab in the same window and stay there. | Installed: lifecycle/ended about 120 s after ending_provisional confirms the browser chop. HEAD: ended with confirmed_by [lone_dwell] at about 720 s and a held_end_trimmed row confirm both the stop and the lost 12 minutes. |
| false-end | Lock the screen (not sleep) for one minute during a recorded browser call and a recorded native call. | A shareable_content_window_present value false row at the lock means a locked screen reads as window-gone, so a 12 minute lock ends a browser, Webex or Slack recording. |
| false-end | Second Teams call: while a hold is showing Confirming end on one Teams call, let another Teams call or ring end. | A lifecycle_provisional_end_confirmed row with oracle teardown_observed while the first call is still up confirms the time-only match. |
| missed-end | Which build are you actually using every day, and on which Mac? On this Mac run `strings -a ~/Applications/MeetingPipe.app/Contents/MacOS/MeetingPipe \| grep -c lifecycle_end_held`. | 0 means the August code (no hold, no trim, no END9 to END17): every HEAD-only finding here is not yet your experience and every installed finding is. 1 or more means a post-END9 build. |
| missed-end | Over your next ten recorded calls, for each `recording_started` in events.jsonl find the matching `state_change recording -> stopping` and what preceded it within 1 s: `lifecycle ended`, `auto_stop_silence`, `force_stop`, or nothing (a manual stop). | Pass: at least 9 of 10 ended by `lifecycle ended` within 90 s of the moment you left, and no manual stops. Any stop with nothing before it is a missed end; any `lifecycle ended` more than 30 s before you left is a chop. |
| missed-end | Leave a Google Meet call with the Leave button and do not touch the tab for two minutes. Then read events.jsonl for that minute. | A `shareable_content_window_present value:false` within 5 s of leaving means the title drops on the post-call page. No such row means the title still matches and that tab can never end a recording by itself. |
| missed-end | Owner-run, read-only HAL probe during a browser call (the DET8 probe shape, pointed at Safari or Chrome): list the process objects whose input flag is set before joining, in the call, while muted in Meet, and for 30 s after leaving. | GO for mic release as the browser end lead if exactly one browser-owned object reads input true for the whole call including mute, and it reads false within 15 s of the leave in three calls out of three. |
| missed-end | Leave three Zoom calls normally on a current build and grep `process_audio_is_running_input` for `us.zoom.xos`. | Pass: a `value:false` row within 15 s of `ax_leave_button_state invalid` each time. If it never appears, Zoom keeps the mic and every Zoom hold at HEAD will sit on the veto until you stop it. |
| missed-end | On a current build, in a Teams call switch to another app for at least three minutes so the call window goes compact, and have the other person end the call while you are still away. Compare `held_end_trimmed limit_sec` with the true call length. | Fail if `limit_sec` is more than 10 s earlier than the real end, or if the HUD read Confirming end while you could still hear the call. |
| missed-end | On the machine where a current build runs, count `call_teardown_source_missing` rows per hour and `lifecycle_end_held` rows with `oracle_available:false`; then count Teams `lifecycle ended` rows whose `confirmed_by` lacks `call_teardown_observed`. | Pass: zero of each over a working week. Any Teams end confirmed by a dwell instead of the teardown is the oracle having been blind at the moment it mattered. |
| missed-end | Start a recording by hotkey in a silent room right after another recording ended in silence, and leave it for 16 minutes with nothing playing. | Pass: `silence_notified` at about 8 minutes and `auto_stop_silence` at about 15. If neither appears and there is no `micgate verdict_changed` row since the start, the backstop was never fed. |
| mic-and-mute | Run the existing DET10 probe (the repo root/daemon/scripts/det10-mic-busy-probe.swift, watch 240 s) from your own Terminal during a real browser call with MeetingPipe not recording, and paste its verdict. | Flag reads true at any point: the daemon's wiring is at fault (rebind on device change). Flag never true while the default input was running: the property is the wrong signal; go straight to the process-object replacement. |
| mic-and-mute | Record two Meet calls on the built-in microphone (one in Safari, one in Chrome) and one through the headset, speak normally for 30 s in each, then count micgate verdict_changed rows with verdict hot between recording_started and the end of the call. | At least one hot within 10 s of speaking is healthy. Zero during a built-in-mic call and hot only after it ends reproduces the 2026-10-16 pattern. If the headset call is healthy and both built-in calls are not, the device is the variable; if only one browser is quiet, that browser's audio processing is. |
| mic-and-mute | Same two calls: note which microphone the browser shows as selected and compare with recorder.input_device_resolved for that recording; then make a 30 s manual recording with no call, speaking at the same distance, and compare the HUD meter by eye. | Different device: wrong-mic capture. Same device and a clearly lower meter during the call than outside it: the call is depressing the shared device. Same device and same meter: the level gate thresholds are simply too high for that mic. |
| mic-and-mute | A read-only enumeration of the HAL process object list (bundle ID, PID, IsRunningInput) once a second before, during and after a Meet call in Safari and in Chrome, three calls each. | Usable as a browser start and end signal if exactly one non-MeetingPipe object reads true within 5 s of joining and false within 15 s of leaving in 3 of 3 calls per browser, and its bundle ID or parent PID ties it to the browser. Anything looser means it can corroborate but not lead. |
| mic-and-mute | After two browser recordings in a row without quitting the app, check whether the second has a micgate verdict_changed row within 1 s of recording_started. | Missing row: the first-verdict duplicate is live on that machine and its idle backstop is unfed until the mic first reads loud. |
| mic-and-mute | On the work laptop: note the app's build date, then count mic_busy_started, mic_in_use_prompt, vad_unsupported, vad_state and silence_notified rows in its events.jsonl. | Zero mic_busy_started against any number of recordings confirms the dead tier there too. Any vad_state row means VAD does exist on that machine and F035 and F037 become live risks there. |
| mic-and-mute | With the app idle, connect and disconnect a headset once and count halbus subscribe_attempt rows with selector mute that follow. | More than one per change while idle, or a count that grows after each recording, confirms surviving listeners on that build. |
| session-orchestration | Which Mac and which build: on the Mac where daily meetings happen, grep events.jsonl for coordinator/lifecycle_end_held and lifecycle/end_vetoed. | Any such row means that Mac runs END17-era code and the head_only and both items here are the daily experience; none means the installed_only items are. |
| session-orchestration | Browser mic-release probe (read-only HAL reads, no app driven by the probe): during ten ordinary Meet calls in the daily browser, sample at 1 Hz whether any audio process object belonging to the browser or its helper processes has input running, from five minutes before leaving to 60 s after, and through ten deliberate tab switches mid-call. | Adopt it as the browser's second class and veto if input stops within 10 s of leaving in at least 9 of 10 leaves and never stops for more than 5 s across the tab switches. Otherwise keep the lone dwell and look at far-side audio instead. |
| session-orchestration | Zoom leave read: on a HEAD build, leave five Zoom calls normally and read signal/process_audio_is_running_input rows for 90 s after each; in one call also mute for two minutes and switch the mic once. | value:false within 15 s on all five leaves means the 60 s pair is sound for Zoom. If any stays true past 60 s the veto would pin a hold open; if mute or the switch drops it for 60 s or more, a stale window-gone can pair with it (F031). Either way Zoom then needs a different second class before HEAD is trusted there. |
| session-orchestration | Hand-stop census on the daily Mac: count recordings whose recording->stopping has no lifecycle/ended, force_stop or auto_stop_silence before it, and how many of those follow an ending_provisional or lifecycle_end_held. | If more than one recording in five ends by hand inside a provisional end or hold, late confirmation is the owner's main end-side pain and the browser and Zoom second class comes first. |
| session-orchestration | Latch gap census: for each app, the longest stretch between detector/discovery_shadow_pick rows while the same pid stays in a call (bounded by process_audio true at the next engage). | Any app with gaps above 60 s means the skip latch and the attach row must not be refreshed by confident winners. |
| session-orchestration | Prompt census: prompt_shown to answer delays, and prompt_timeout rows whose engage read process_audio_is_running_input true. | One timed-out prompt with a live mic per week is enough to change the default from skip. |
| session-orchestration | Bring-up census: recorder/input_device_resolved to state_change ->recording, split by transport. | Any start over one second (expect Bluetooth) makes the F045 window practically reachable and raises that item to high. |

## Refuted in this focused audit

- **D047. Installed build: a recording started by hand, re-attached after a skip, or started in the cooldown has no end detection** (`daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/Lifecycle/MeetingLifecycleCoordinator.swift:229`). Code check: `git show c56f2869` has the silent `activeAdapter?.armLeaveButton(element)` at line 229, so the claim about the old build is accurate. At HEAD the guard logs `arm_leave_button_dropped`, and `attachRecordStart` runs the END11 engage step before the arm. I did not re-trace the claim that a source-less start inside the cooldown is `beginRecording(source: nil)`. That case has no lifecycle by design, because no app is attached to it.

Field check: I ran the session scratchpad with `python3 -I` over all four events files, sorted by ts.
- `record_attached` rows: 8. They fall on 2026-10-01, 10-06, 10-08 (two), 10-14, 10-22 (two) and 11-05, so they span the first two build eras. None falls in the newest era.
- Every one of the 8 is followed within about 1 s by `coordinator lifecycle_engage_explicit_start` and `lifecycle in_meeting`.
- 5 of the 8 reach `lifecycle ended` before `recording_stopped`. The other 3 (10-06, 10-08 13:30 and 10-14 ended... correction: 10-06 and 10-08 13:30) stop without `ended`. They engaged, so the cause is not a missing adapter. The field checker attributes them to a manual stop and to the failed Leave re-arm; I did not re-verify those causes.
- `arm_leave_button_dropped`: 0 in the whole pack.
- `recording_started` bundle ids: 66 com.microsoft.teams2, 4 manual, 3 us.zoom.xos, 1 com.google.Chrome. This matches the headline counts.

The mechanism therefore applies only to the home Mac's August build. No signature of it appears on the work laptop in any era, and the field checker's 8 of 8 engaged result reproduces.
- **D086. The corpus's only raw trace carries the extractor's default label, not an observation** (`scripts/extract_detection_trace.py:552`). Code: the `--expect` choices are no_end and ended, with no_end as the default, at HEAD line 552. The `--raw` mode did not exist at c56f2869 (it came with T8, e2f55eab), so head_only is correct. Format: README lines 251-252 and the two lifecycle traces show `expected_outcome: ended` with `ended_at` and `trim_at`. So the claim that the format cannot record a late or missed end is false. Trace contents (read with python): source_window 2026-10-16T09:45:30Z to 09:46:28Z, until 58.0, one logged ending_provisional at t=38.612 led by browser_tab_title_left_meet_pattern, and only 3 events. Log: the 09:4x window of 2026-10-16 is not in the work-laptop field pack. Its events.1.jsonl has only 3 unrelated rows there (a Teams candidate_dropped and two call_teardown_source_missing). The window is in this Mac's own ~/Library/Logs/MeetingPipe/events.jsonl: lifecycle ending_provisional com.apple.Safari at 09:46:08.612Z, then coordinator state_change recording to stopping at 09:46:28.257Z, then recording_stopped, with no lifecycle ended. That matches the claimed signature, 19.6 s apart. No row in the log marks the stop as manual; only the README asserts that. Nothing shows the call had ended at 09:46:28. A browser tab leaving the meet pattern is exactly the case the corpus says must be held rather than ended within seconds. So no_end across a window that stops 19.6 s after the provisional is defensible whatever the true end was, and a later true end can still be written as `ended` with `ended_at`. Symptom: git grep finds the trace referenced only in INDEX.json, the corpus README and T8.md. T8 calls the incident traces 'still owed' and END17 re-verification still pending. Nothing claims a fix was verified against this trace, so the claimed symptom (a fix declared verified against an unobserved outcome) does not occur. On the work laptop it is speculative at most.
